# Phase 5.4, "MIR: lowering, monomorphization and `async_lower`", Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use
superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or
superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use
checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** N's sixth stage, MIR, natively. S0 gains a hidden MIR dump,
`nova check --dump-mir [--before-async] FILE…` (ADR 0053's mechanism,
absent from every `--help`), printed by a new `nova_mir::dump` after
the async transform or, with `--before-async`, before it (the hook
`nova_mir::Options::stop_before_async`), through `nova_driver::MirDumps`;
std's checked bodies are kept once by `SharedItems` and spliced into each
program with their ids moved (`nova_typeck::shared_module`), but for a
program that implements anything on a type it does not own, or when std
alone gives a diagnostic or holds a test, which is checked in full
(ADR 0056 §4's fallback). N (`selfhost/`) ports `nova-mir`'s native path
function for function (`mir.nova`, `lower.nova`, `lower_call.nova`,
`lower_match.nova`, `mono.nova`, `splice.nova`, `async_lower.nova`,
`mir_dump.nova`, the HIR helpers in `hir.nova`), and `selfhost mir --std
DIR [--before-async] FILE…` prints the same dump. The differential
(`crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs`) compares the two, byte for byte
with exit codes, on the native corpus's roots that run (251 loose
programs and 10 packages' `src/main.nova`, N's own among them), the 342
HIR cases that reach MIR and the 86 MIR cases under
`tests/selfhost/cases/mir/` (both line endings), a new MIR fuzzer's 120
inputs and 25 programs nested 128 levels deep, or as deep as the parser
takes them, which N lowers on a 1 MB main thread: the after form on
every input, the before form on the inputs whose after form holds a poll
function. Every form of `KINDS` a native build prints occurs, and every
MIR code reachable from source (E0601 at both sites, E0013 at
`lower_module_with`'s bound check, `mono.rs:283`, the spec's and the
ruling's `mono.rs:276` at the base, E0075, E0078 at both limits,
E0079). A mismatch in a MIR dump is reported by its root, function,
block and statement (spec §4.3). Nothing a user sees
changes: `nova check` and `nova parse` give what they gave, byte for
byte, on every `.nova` file the base tracks, and `nova test` and `nova
build` on its 11 packages outside `crates/` (22 runs; the 95 packages
under `crates/` are test fixtures, not compared). 5.4 is
native only: WASM's and test mode's MIR stay S0's (the user's decision
4). ADR 0057 records the dump, the splice, N's port, the figures and
CI's, with the Selfhost job's lines widened by the user's answer of
2026-11-15.

**Architecture:**
- **S0's dump** (Task 1): `crates/nova-mir/src/dump.rs` (new: `KINDS`,
  `NOT_NATIVE`, `Reached`, `dump_program` and its unit test),
  `nova_mir::Options::stop_before_async` (`mono.rs`);
  `crates/nova-driver/src/lib.rs` (`MirDumps` with the full check,
  `HirDumps::dumps` and `Wanted`, `front`'s `runs=false` before the
  loader); `crates/nova-cli/src/cmd/run.rs` and `main.rs` (`DumpMirCmd`,
  `DumpCmd::Mir`, `dump_files`); `nova_resolver::dump::quote`
  re-exported; `selfhost.rs`'s S0 tests of the flag, the headers and the
  settled paths; `Checked::into_module` (`items.rs`), which `MirDumps`'s
  full check builds its module with.
- **std spliced** (Task 2): `crates/nova-typeck/src/check/items.rs`
  (`SharedItems` keeps std's functions, `shared_module`, `Shift`, the
  unit test), `shared_module` re-exported through `check.rs` and
  `lib.rs`; the driver's `MirDumps` splicing, and the guard over both
  MIR forms.
- **The cases** (Task 3): `cases/mir/`, the native sources of
  `nova-mir`'s tests extracted and a case by hand for each MIR code
  reachable from source; `selfhost.rs`'s MIR dump on S0's side alone
  (`N_MIR = false`), both line endings; the driver's guard over the MIR
  cases; `nova_driver::mir_dump`.
- **N's lowering and monomorphization** (Task 4): N's MIR files but
  `async_lower.nova`, `hir.nova`'s helpers, `mir_tests.nova`;
  `selfhost mir --before-async` (`main.nova`), N's plain `selfhost mir`
  refusing with exit 3 (`N_MIR_AFTER`, `MirForm::n_prints`); the before
  form compared on every input (decision 17); the MIR mismatch report
  in `tests/selfhost/compare.rs`, naming the function, block and
  statement where a MIR dump differs (spec §4.3, decision 25).
- **N's `async_lower`** (Task 5): `async_lower.nova`; the refusal and the
  switch gone; the after form compared on every input and the before
  form where a poll function is made (`agree_on_mir_forms`).
- **The fuzzer and the depth cases** (Task 6): `tests/selfhost/fuzz.rs`'s
  `mir_mutate`, `mir_deep` and `mir_deep_type`; `selfhost.rs`'s two tests;
  step 0's checks (decision 21); `ci.yml`'s comment on the stack tests.
- **Records** (Task 7): ADR 0057 (new); `CHANGELOG.md`; dated notes in
  `docs/phase-5-plan.md`, `nova-spec/00-MASTER-SPEC.md` and
  `nova-spec/14-CODEGEN.md`.

**Tech Stack:**
- Rust: MSRV 1.78, edition 2021.
- **No new crate; `Cargo.lock` and every `Cargo.toml` stay as they are**
  (spec §13).
- N is built by `nova build` through Cranelift. `clang-18` (CI's pin) for
  the golden suite, and Node 22 for the WASM tests, as before.

## Global Constraints

- **The workspace's minimum Rust stays 1.78,** edition 2021. CI's MSRV job
  runs `cargo check --locked --workspace` under `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.78.0`
  with `RUSTFLAGS=-D warnings`. No `std::sync::LazyLock` (1.80), no
  `Option::is_none_or` (1.82), no `std::path::absolute` (1.79). 5.4's Rust
  adds `str::repeat` and `str::replacen` (1.16), `Iterator::find_map`,
  `str::trim_start_matches` and `str::trim_end_matches` (1.30),
  `Iterator::copied` (1.36), `Option::map_or_else` (1.41),
  `matches!` (1.42), `str::strip_prefix` and `str::strip_suffix` (1.45),
  `bool::then` (1.50), `str::split_inclusive` (1.51),
  `std::thread::scope` (1.63), `Option::is_some_and`,
  `Result::is_ok_and` and `std::sync::OnceLock` (1.70) and
  `usize::div_ceil` (1.73): each at most 1.78 (a `grep` over the diff's
  added lines).
- **No new crate; `Cargo.lock` and every `Cargo.toml` stay as they are**
  (spec §13). If a step seems to need either, stop that task there and
  ask the user; do not add the crate or change a manifest first.
- **No `unwrap()` outside `#[cfg(test)]` and test files:** `expect("why")`,
  `unwrap_or…` or `?`. 5.4's Rust outside tests adds none; N's
  `unwrap()`s are on values its own code has checked.
- **No `unsafe`.** 5.4 adds none.
- **Tests never touch the internet,** bind no port, and need no `clang`
  (spec §12). The differential runs `nova` and the N it builds, nothing
  else; the MIR cases that name `serve`, `bind` or an `extern` are
  checked and lowered, never run.
- **Temporary names are fixed** (spec §12): under `std::env::temp_dir()`,
  `nova-selfhost-<name>`. 5.4 adds `mir-flag` (the flag test's `-a.nova`)
  and `mir-headers` (the header test's roots) in Task 1, `crlf-mir` (the
  MIR cases and the HIR cases that reach MIR, copied with `\r\n`) in
  Task 3, and `mir-fuzz` (the MIR fuzzer's inputs) and `deep-n-mir` (the
  depth cases) in Task 6. These are every fixed temporary name the diff
  adds (`grep -n 'fresh_dir(\|std_copy(\|temp_dir'` over its added
  lines, at each task); the driver's and the type checker's new tests,
  the MIR mismatch report's test (Task 4) and N's new `@test`s write
  nothing. Each is removed and made afresh by
  the test that uses it. A test that needs `$NOVA_HOME` sets a fresh
  one.
- **Never clean `/tmp/nova-*`:** `nova test` keeps built binaries in
  `/tmp/nova-test-bin`, and the golden suite's directory is one of them.
- **A test that needs Node** passes without running when `node` is not on
  `PATH`, and fails instead under `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1`, which CI sets. Run
  the suite here with `NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1`. No test of 5.4 needs Node.
- **`release_golden_suite` runs only under `NOVA_REQUIRE_CLANG=1`,** which
  CI's Linux leg sets with `NOVA_CLANG=clang-18`; elsewhere it prints
  `release golden: not run (set NOVA_REQUIRE_CLANG=1 to run it)` and
  passes. 5.4 adds no runtime program and changes no lowering, so it
  stays at 147.
- **`nova-cli`'s tests need two files beside `target/debug/nova`:**
  `libnova_runtime.a` and the `nova-bench-http` binary, which only a build
  of the workspace writes. `nova build` of `selfhost/` links against
  that runtime, or, in a `nova` built with `NOVA_EMBED_RUNTIME=1`, against
  the release runtime embedded in it, unless `NOVA_RUNTIME_LIB` names one.
  Every run step starts with `cargo build --locked --workspace`
  (Conventions).
- **`selfhost.rs` is not in `cargo test --workspace`:** its target says
  `test = false` (5.3a). `S` runs the whole suite as the Test job does,
  `SH` runs `selfhost.rs` as the Selfhost job does; every figure here
  counts the two apart.
- **`NOVA_EMBED_RUNTIME` and `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_OPT_LEVEL` are set for one
  command only, or for a whole step that says so** (Task 8, Steps 7 and
  8): a change of either makes cargo rebuild, and every other test that
  links a program then runs on that runtime or at that level. Each such
  step ends with `cargo build --locked --workspace` without them. Never
  build every test target at `opt-level = 1` (`cargo build --tests` or the
  whole suite): only `selfhost.rs`'s, as the Selfhost job does.
- **The process limit.** The differential starts two processes a batch,
  and the suite thousands; every test, mutant and suite runs under the
  limit of the Conventions, so that a mistake that forks without end fails
  to fork rather than filling the machine.
- **Work in one tree.** Two worktrees of this repository sharing one
  `target/` share artifact names, so cargo can take one tree's build as
  fresh for the other. Never build in another worktree, and never run two
  suites, or a suite and `SH`, at once: the differential's fixed
  directories are shared.
- **Keep `df -h /` above 6G.** Older duplicate test executables in
  `target/debug/deps` may be removed between commands, never while one
  runs, and so may `target/debug/incremental`; never `cargo clean` the
  main target. Each run of the whole suite leaves about 0.3 GB under
  `/tmp/nova-todo-*`, which is never cleaned. Every `cargo` command here
  selects the whole workspace (Conventions), so cargo keeps one copy of
  each test executable. The Rust 1.100 clippy (Conventions) and the Rust
  1.78 check (Task 8) use target directories of their own, removed after
  (about 0.4 GB each); the `opt-level = 1` build of Task 8, Step 7 adds
  about 1 GB to `target/`. The replay began with 12G free and ended with
  11G. Check `df -h /` before any build at a new opt-level or toolchain.
- **Never use the Write or Edit tool for text that holds a backslash-u
  escape,** a backslash and `u` followed by four hex digits or by a code in
  braces: they decode the escape into the character. None of 5.4's
  patches or scripts holds one. Apply the patches with `patch_block`, from
  the committed plan.
- **No patch adds a CR, a NUL or trailing whitespace.** The inputs that
  need them are written by the tests at run time (the CRLF cases), not
  kept as files.
- **Nothing writes into the repository but the patches.** `nova build -o`
  writes N under the system's temporary directory and `nova test` writes
  nothing into the package; `git status --short` is empty after every run.
- **Push and open the pull request on the user's word only.** No push
  of this branch is allowed yet: the user's answer of 2026-11-15, "Push +
  draft PR", covered only the prototype's
  `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q-54-ci` and draft Sakeerin/nova#142,
  closed unmerged. Task 8, Step 11 stops before any push, and the main
  session asks the user; the branch is pushed and the PR opened only
  after their word.
- **Merge by rebase, on the user's word only.** Never enable auto-merge.
  Nothing is published and no tag is made in 5.4.
- **This machine is Linux.** macOS and Windows results come from CI; what
  differs there is reasoned from the code (Review Focus 1).

## Conventions

- **Scratch directory:** `$P` is the session's scratch directory, outside
  the repository. Every shell exports it, `export P=…` with the
  directory's path, before a command that names `$P` (the Runs block
  below starts with it). Exported, not merely set: the scripts this plan
  writes read it from the environment, `adrcheck.py` (Task 7) and
  `parsecmp.sh`, `checkcmp.sh`, `pkgcmp.sh`, `helpcmp.sh`,
  `usagecmp.sh` and `mem.py` (Task 8), each run as a process of its own;
  without it `adrcheck.py` stops with a `KeyError` and the shell scripts
  stop at their first line.
- **The ledger:** `$P/ledger-54.md`. Every ruling made while executing
  goes there, with its cost if wrong; so do each task's figures.
- **Commits.** Each task's message is written to `$P/msg-54-<task>.txt`:
  the subject the task names, a blank line, and these two trailer lines,
  exactly:

  ```
  Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
  Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01VzjWoMjudJFFEx1oAg4fTN
  ```

  Then `git add -A`; `git status --short` lists only the task's
  files; and `git commit -F "$P/msg-54-<task>.txt"`. A task with a tests
  patch and a code patch makes one commit, after both. Every commit of
  this plan ends with those two lines: the plan's own (Task 0), each
  task's and the review's fixes (Task 8, Step 10). The pull request's
  body ends as Task 8, Step 11 gives it. No model ID (a `claude-…` model
  name) goes in any artifact: no commit, file, pull request, comment or
  ledger line names one.
- **The process limit.** Every shell that runs a test or a mutant first
  caps the tasks it may start at 600, threads as well as processes, and
  checks that the cap holds (Task 0, Step 2).
  - **As root, a pids control group.** Linux never applies `ulimit -u` to
    root, so the shell joins a group with that cap. On cgroup v1, as the
    replay ran:

    ```sh
    CG=/sys/fs/cgroup/pids/nova-limit
    mkdir -p "$CG" && echo 600 > "$CG/pids.max" && echo $$ > "$CG/cgroup.procs"
    grep nova-limit /proc/self/cgroup     # N:pids:/nova-limit
    ```

    On cgroup v2 (`stat -fc %T /sys/fs/cgroup` prints `cgroup2fs`), the
    root's `cgroup.subtree_control` must hold `pids` first, and the group
    is a child of the root:

    ```sh
    grep -qw pids /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.subtree_control || echo +pids > /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.subtree_control
    CG=/sys/fs/cgroup/nova-limit
    mkdir -p "$CG" && echo 600 > "$CG/pids.max" && echo $$ > "$CG/cgroup.procs"
    cat /proc/self/cgroup                 # 0::/nova-limit
    ```

  - **As any other user,** a systemd scope with the cap,
    `systemd-run --user --scope -p TasksMax=600 bash`, whose shell then
    runs everything; or, without systemd, `ulimit -Su 600`, which counts
    all the user's tasks, so a test may fail to start a thread sooner.
    Neither shell is in a group named `nova-limit`, so each checks its
    own cap (Runs, below), not the root's group.

  600 tasks was ample here: the replay's suites, builds, golden suite and
  mutants ran under it on 4 CPUs. Test threads scale with the cores, so a
  machine with many more may need a higher cap: if a test fails to start a
  thread, read the group's `pids.peak`, raise `pids.max`, and record it.
- **Runs.** The replay ran every command in the group and with these
  variables set. Each shell puts itself under the cap and sets them too,
  with three shorthands, and runs every command from the repository's
  root; a shell that is not under the cap stops at once:

  ```sh
  # The scratch directory (above), exported: the plan's scripts read it.
  export P=/path/to/the/scratch/directory
  # The process limit (above), then the check that this shell is under
  # it, by one of these four, the first as the replay ran (root, cgroup v1):
  CG=/sys/fs/cgroup/pids/nova-limit
  mkdir -p "$CG" && echo 600 > "$CG/pids.max" && echo $$ > "$CG/cgroup.procs"
  grep -q nova-limit /proc/self/cgroup || exit 1
  # Root on cgroup v2: the v2 lines above, then
  #   grep -qx '0::/nova-limit' /proc/self/cgroup || exit 1
  # Another user, in the shell systemd-run started (cgroup v2), without
  # the lines above:
  #   [ "$(cat "/sys/fs/cgroup$(sed -n 's/^0:://p' /proc/self/cgroup)/pids.max")" = 600 ] || exit 1
  # Another user, after `ulimit -Su 600`, without the lines above:
  #   [ "$(ulimit -Su)" = 600 ] || exit 1
  export CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 NO_COLOR=1 RUST_BACKTRACE=0 NOVA_HOME="$P/home-54"
  unset NOVA_RUNTIME_LIB NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE NOVA_REQUIRE_CLANG NOVA_CLANG NOVA_EMBED_RUNTIME CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_OPT_LEVEL
  mkdir -p "$NOVA_HOME"
  SH() { cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost -- "$@"; }
  S() { NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast "$@"; }
  G() { NOVA_REQUIRE_CLANG=1 NOVA_CLANG=clang-18 cargo test --locked --workspace --test run_tests -- release_golden_suite --nocapture "$@"; }
  ```

  So `SH` runs `selfhost.rs`, the differential and the flags' tests, `S`
  the whole suite without `selfhost.rs`, as the Test job runs it, and `G`
  the golden suite through LLVM as CI's Linux leg runs it. `NOVA_HOME`
  keeps a run by hand off `~/.nova`; every test that needs one sets its
  own.
- **Every `cargo` command selects the whole workspace,** `--workspace`
  with `--test NAME` or `--lib` to narrow it, never `-p`. Cargo unifies
  features over the packages a command selects, so each `-p` selection
  builds and links its own copy of every test executable it needs, which
  the disk does not have room for; a `--workspace` command reuses one
  copy. `S` gives each code step the whole suite, about five to seven
  minutes; its one failure is the IPv6 test (Task 0). The exceptions are
  the clippy commands and `cargo fmt --all`, written as CI writes them,
  without `--workspace`: run from the root, whose `Cargo.toml` is a
  virtual manifest (`[workspace]` alone), cargo selects every member all
  the same. A `--lib` run that names tests in two crates passes
  `--no-fail-fast`: cargo stops at the first test executable that fails,
  and `nova-driver`'s comes before `nova-typeck`'s.
- **Timeouts, and nothing in the background.**
  - Give every command a tool timeout of at least its stated time plus
    half again, never under 120 s and at most 600 s, the most a
    foreground command may take. A command whose stated time, with half
    again, passes 600 s is split into runs that each fit (below).
  - Run every command in the foreground: never with `&`, `nohup` or a
    tool's background mode, and never let a tool send one to the
    background. A block of several commands runs one command at a time,
    each with its own timeout.
  - Each code step builds the tests first, `cargo test --locked
    --workspace --no-run` as a command of its own (3 to 20 s), and
    `cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost --no-run` before
    `SH`, so that neither run holds a build.
  - **`S` in three runs.** A task's `S` took 291 to 408 s, and Task 5's
    408 s with half again passes 600 s. So `S` runs as three commands
    where its time comes near 600 s, at Task 5, Step 4 and Task 8, Step
    1 (350 s, at `430618f5`); anywhere else it may run so too:

    ```sh
    S --lib --bins > "$P/s-a.txt" 2>&1                       # the libraries' and binaries' tests
    S --doc > "$P/s-b.txt" 2>&1                              # the doc tests
    S --test '[!s]*' --test 's[!e]*' > "$P/s-c.txt" 2>&1     # every integration test but selfhost.rs
    cat "$P/s-a.txt" "$P/s-b.txt" "$P/s-c.txt" | grep '^test result' | awk '{p += $4; f += $6; i += $8} END {print p, f, i}'
    ```

    At the last commit they gave `1298 1 8` in 133 s (the IPv6 test the
    failure), `2 0 0` in 5 s and `1535 0 2` in 233 s, which add up to
    the whole run's `2835 1 10`; `grep -c 'Running tests/selfhost.rs'
    "$P/s-c.txt"` prints `0`. The globs name every integration test but
    `selfhost`: a `--test` pattern takes a `test = false` target, which
    `--tests` leaves out, and `--tests` would run the libraries' tests
    again. A difference from the whole run's sum is recorded in the
    ledger.
  - The other commands that come closest: CI's Linux leg (373 s, at
    `430618f5`), `checkcmp.sh`, run in two halves (233 and 230 s at the
    last commit's code, 252 and 245 s at `430618f5`, 473 s whole there),
    `SH` without the release runtime (178 s at the last commit's code,
    221 s at `430618f5`), and the mutants' batches,
    each one command, sized
    so that each fits under 600 s with half again (Task 8, Step 8).
  - **A child killed at `CHILD_LIMIT`.** In `SH` without the release
    runtime the longest child processes take 95 to 97 s against the
    harness's 120 s `CHILD_LIMIT` (decision 18). If a test fails with a
    child killed there (`… did not finish within 120s: killed`), run that
    test alone once, `SH --exact <test>`. If it is killed again, stop and
    report to the main session. Never raise `CHILD_LIMIT` or deal the
    inputs into more parts without the main session.
  - If a command is killed by its timeout, or the shell restarts, restore
    what it left: after a killed mutant run, `git checkout -- <file>` of
    the mutant's file, and then `git status --short` must print nothing
    before anything goes on.
- **The build.** Each "Run" step below starts with

  ```sh
  cargo build --locked --workspace
  ```

  which ends with `Finished`, so that the `nova` the steps run by hand and
  the runtime library beside it are the tree's (Global Constraints). The
  replay began every step with it.
- **The patches.** Each task's code is in patch blocks below, each marked
  `<!-- patch NAME -->` and fenced with four backticks. They are the
  replay's commits, so apply them; do not retype them. From the
  repository's root:

  ```sh
  PLAN=docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-15-phase-5-4-mir.md   # this file, committed
  block() {
    awk -v name="$1" '
      $0 == "<!-- patch " name " -->" { grab = 1; next }
      grab && /^````diff$/ { inside = 1; next }
      inside && /^````$/ { exit }
      inside { print }
    ' "$PLAN" > "$P/$1.patch"
  }
  patch_block() {
    block "$1" && git apply --check "$P/$1.patch" && git apply "$P/$1.patch"
  }
  ```

  `patch_block t1-tests` applies the block named `t1-tests`, and prints
  nothing: no 5.4 patch adds a line with trailing whitespace, a CR or a
  backslash-u escape, so `git apply` warns for none. Task 3's tests patch
  adds the 86 MIR cases (93 files) with the harness beside them
  (1,023 added lines, a block of 1,679
  lines), and Task 4's code patch 3,546 added lines (a block
  of 3,690): each is one block all the same. The
  non-ASCII characters the patches add are `§` (every task), `…` (Tasks
  1, 3, 4, 5 and 7), `×` and `—` (Task 7). A block that does not apply
  means the tree differs from the plan's: stop, and record why in the
  ledger.
- **Each task** but Task 7 is test first: its tests' patch, their run and
  the expected failure, then its code's patch, its run and the expected
  pass. Task 7's patch is the records. Each task then runs its checks:
  - `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, which prints nothing;
  - the workspace's clippy,
    `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, as
    CI runs it, which ends with `Finished` and no warning;
  - `selfhost.rs`'s clippy,
    `cargo clippy --locked --all-features --test selfhost -- -D warnings`,
    CI's second command (`--all-targets` skips a `test = false` target),
    which ends with `Finished` and no warning;
  - both clippy commands again on CI's stable, Rust 1.100, in a target
    directory of their own (`rustup toolchain install 1.100.0 --profile
    minimal -c clippy` once, if `rustup toolchain list` lacks it):
    `CARGO_TARGET_DIR="$P/target-1100" cargo +1.100.0 clippy …` with each
    command's arguments, each ending with `Finished` and no warning; the
    local stable (1.97 here) and CI's lint differently;
  - `target/debug/nova fmt --check std examples selfhost`, CI's arguments,
    and `target/debug/nova fmt --check std examples tests/wasm tests/dom
    tests/browser/router-app tests/browser/ssr-app tests/ssr selfhost`, the
    earlier plans' superset, with the `nova` the step's build made, each
    printing nothing.

  It ends with one commit. A difference from an `Expected:` is fixed in
  the smallest way and recorded in the ledger as a ruling, with its cost
  if wrong. After Task 7, `rm -rf "$P/target-1100"`.


## Review Focus

The inputs most likely to bite a user, most likely first. Each has its
check in the task that owns the code:

1. **Windows** (`HIR_ARGS_LIMIT` for the MIR dump's root lists,
   `settled_stdout`, the MIR cases' `\r\n` copies): every root list goes
   in parts under 16,000 bytes, asserted on every system (`mir_dump_of`);
   the MIR dump runs the loader, whose E0004 names a path, so `run`
   settles it as the HIR dump's; a copy that changes nothing, as on a
   Windows checkout, is not compared again (CI run 43656932684 failed
   there before the prototype's fix) →
   `the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_corpus_and_the_cases`,
   `the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_cases_with_windows_line_endings`,
   `every_run_of_a_mir_dump_settles_e0004s_paths_on_windows` (Tasks 1
   and 3) (mutants 156, 157, 164 to 166). The PR's Windows and macOS legs
   are the runs.
2. **std's bodies spliced, on both sides** (`shared_module` and `Shift`
   in S0 and N, the order, every id below `S` by `D`, every closure's or
   wrapper's by `D + P`, `closure$N` and `fnval$N` renamed; the fallback
   in S0's `HirDumps` and in N's `mir_of` and `mir_command`): a program's
   spliced module must be its full check's, and its MIR dumps the
   unshared ones, both orders →
   `shared_module_is_the_full_checks_module`, N's
   `a_spliced_module_is_the_full_checks_module`, the driver's guard,
   `mir_std_bodies_see_an_inherent_flush_on_stdout`,
   `n_checks_std_for_each_program_when_std_gives_a_body_diagnostic`
   (Tasks 2, 3 and 6) (mutants 40 to 59, 123 to 141, 154, 155).
3. **N's port, word for word** (each MIR code at its site, `mangle_ty`'s
   codes, the specializer and the splice building new nodes, the async
   transform's slots, tags and wrapper, the shim, the asserts as panics)
   against `mono.rs`, `lower.rs`, `async_lower.rs` and `lib.rs` → the
   differential on every input in both forms, N's `@test`s (Tasks 4 and
   5) (mutants 60 to 122). The reviewer reads each N function beside its
   S0 function, same names.
4. **The dump's forms** (`nova_mir::dump`, N's `mir_dump.nova`; ADR 0057
   §2): every form of `KINDS` but `NOT_NATIVE`'s occurs natively, nothing
   unlisted, each `key=bool` both ways → the dump's unit test, the corpus
   test (Tasks 1 and 3) (mutants 1 to 21, 96 to 104, 160, 162, 163).
5. **The before form and its hook** (`stop_before_async`, the shim before
   it, the before-form set read from S0's after form, the forms equal
   where no poll function is made) → the header test, the corpus test
   (Tasks 1 and 5) (mutants 22, 23, 82, 158, 159).
6. **The hidden flag stays hidden** (`DumpMirCmd`, `DumpCmd::from_args`):
   `--dump-mir` taken only first after `check`, its own usage line,
   `--dump-mir=…` refused as the base refuses it, every `--help` the
   base's → `the_mir_dump_flag_is_hidden_and_refused_as_the_spec_says`,
   `both_sides_refuse_the_mir_dumps_shapes_alike`, `helpcmp.sh`,
   `usagecmp.sh` (Task 8, Step 5) (mutants 24 to 30, 142 to 146).
7. **How far each root got** (`MirDumps`, N's `dump_mir`): `runs=false`
   before the loader, `checked=false` after any error, `lowered=false`
   with MIR's diagnostics, the entry's `main` kept →
   `the_mir_dump_has_a_header_per_root_and_says_how_far_each_got`, the
   guard's floors, the cases (Tasks 1, 3 and 6) (mutants 31 to 39, 87,

8. **The harness keeps every input** (the corpus test's counts, the
   codes, the fuzzer's kinds and floors and its clean inputs, the depth

9. **N's stack, memory and time** (spec §7.5, §8): every depth program

## Decisions

The user's, from the spec's header (2026-11-14):

1. **One pull request, split into tasks, test first** (the user's
   decision 1), as 5.3b; the dump shows MIR after the async transform,
   with a before-transform form to locate a divergence (decision 5).
2. **std's bodies shared and spliced in** (the user's decision 2): both
   sides keep std's checked functions in the shared state and splice them
   into each program with their ids moved (Task 2 on S0's side, Task 4 on
   N's); `shared_module_is_the_full_checks_module` checks that the
   spliced module is the full check's, and since step 0 N's
   `a_spliced_module_is_the_full_checks_module` too (decision 21); CI was
   measured from the prototype before this plan (decisions 10 and 11).
3. **The inputs** (the user's decision 3, as given: the corpus, the HIR
   cases that reach MIR, about 85 sources from `lower_tests.rs` and
   `pattern_lowering.rs` as `.nova` cases, as 5.2 and 5.3 did, and hand
   cases for every code reachable from source; about 38 internal tests
   stay Rust-only). As built: the native corpus's roots that run, the
   HIR cases that reach MIR, the native sources of `nova-mir`'s tests
   (74 kept of 85, ADR 0057 §7) and a case by hand for each MIR code
   reachable from source, 86 cases under `cases/mir/` with step 0's
   three (decision 21); the 38 inline tests and the 84 integration tests
   stay Rust-only, their sources cases.
4. **Native only** **(differs)** from the phase plan, not from the spec,
   which decides it (the user's decision 4): WASM's and test mode's MIR
   stay S0's, recorded in ADR 0057 §6 and §20 and the phase plan's dated
   note; the 35 WASM programs are not compared.
5. **Both forms compared** (the user's decision 5, as given: the main
   dump shows MIR after the async transform, what the emitter reads; a
   second form shows it before the transform; both are compared, so a
   difference is placed in lowering or in `async_lower`, and the early
   tasks compare before `async_lower` is ported). The after form is
   compared on every input from Task 5, the before form on every input
   in Task 4. That the before form is then compared only on the inputs
   whose after form holds a poll function, from Task 5 on, is the
   spec's own choice, not asked (its header and §7.2; decision 12):
   where no poll function is made the transform changes nothing, so the
   two forms are equal, which S0's side checks, and N's after form pins
   the lowering there (decision 17).
6. **A new type-preserving fuzzer aimed at MIR** (the user's decision 6,
   as given: a new type-preserving mutator aimed at MIR, new generic
   instances, awaits, match shapes and closures, with floors so each main
   feature occurs, within CI's budget). As built:
   `fuzz::mir_mutate`, seeds 601 to 603, 40 inputs each, four kinds of
   closed addition (generic instances, awaits, match shapes, closures),
   each in at least 10 inputs by its mark in S0's dump, every input
   lowering clean (Task 6).
7. **`RtFunc::symbol` and `signature` with the emitter in 5.5** (the
   user's decision 7, as given: 5.4 ports only the variant names the dump
   shows): N ports the 111 variant names alone (`RT_FUNC_VARIANTS`).
8. **A hidden `nova check --dump-mir`** (the user's decision 8), by ADR
   0053's mechanism; N's side `selfhost mir`; every `--help` unchanged
   (Task 1; Task 8, Step 5).

The main session's ruling (2026-11-14) and the user's answers of
2026-11-15:

9. **E0013 in `lower_trait_call` (`lower.rs:974`) is unreachable**
   **(differs)**: the spec says both E0013 sites were reproduced; the
   research's two probes both reach the bound check in
   `lower_module_with` (`mono.rs:276` at the base, 283 after Task 1's
   hook), and the prototype's 13 probes and an analysis found no input
   that reaches the lowering site (ADR 0057 §7). It is treated as E0011
   (`lower_module_with`, `mono.rs:255`) is: ported as written, no case,
   recorded unreachable; the gate reads "E0013 at `mono.rs:276`", the
   ruling's words, the base's line (283 after Task 1). The spec's dated
   amendment says so (decision 23). Cost if wrong: one site without a
   case; no output.
10. **CI measured from the prototype, the user asked past the lines:**
    the user's decision 2 asked to measure CI from the prototype before
    the plan, and the user's answer of 2026-11-15, "Push + draft PR",
    allowed the push: the prototype went to
    `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q-54-ci` with draft Sakeerin/nova#142,
    closed unmerged (2026-11-15T00:37:07Z, head `eafdae25`), and nothing
    else was pushed. Runs 43656932684 and 43657541603 (the header's
    figures, ADR 0057 §19) put Linux past 60 s on both (65.78 and 68.21
    s), macOS on one (79.62 s) and Windows on one (87.77 s against 70 s).
11. **The Selfhost job's lines widened** **(differs)** from spec §8's 60,
    60 and 70 s (the user's answer, 2026-11-15, as given: widen the
    lines): 80 s on Linux, 100 s on macOS and 110 s on Windows; no input
    or coverage cut, no code change, and the splice left whole (not the
    lazy splice, which copies only the std functions a program reaches,
    ADR 0057 §12, §20). ADR 0057 §19 records it.

How this plan is cut and checked:

12. **The spec's recommendations, not asked, as taken** (spec header):
    the before form an option of the same flag, `--dump-mir
    --before-async`, which takes no `--std`, `--target` or `--test`; the
    printer in `nova-mir`, the hook in `nova_mir::Options`; `(Mir
    runs=false)` for a root that does not run and `checked=false` with
    no diagnostic for one that stops before MIR; one line a temp; the
    before form compared only where it can differ once N ports
    `async_lower`; 5.3b's stack rule; no frozen list.
13. **The dump's grammar as the prototype settled it** (ADR 0057 §2),
    each cheap to change: every part ends with `(Diagnostics)`;
    **(differs)** a root that does not run is decided before it is read,
    so a missing or unreadable package module prints `runs=false`, never
    `IOERR`, where spec §3.1 and §3.3 give `IOERR` to every root that
    cannot be read: reading a module `check` does not lower, only to
    report it, is work no comparison needs (ADR 0057 §2, §20; the
    amendment, decision 23; cost if wrong: that read error goes
    unshown); an extern prints
    `(Extern "sym" abi=C (F64) ret=F64)`, a switch arm `0:bb2`, an absent
    return `(Return _)`; `NOT_NATIVE` lists `(Mem` and `abi=Js`; `quote`
    is re-exported from `nova_resolver::dump`, since `nova-mir` does not
    depend on `nova-ast`.
14. **The counts and projections that proved wrong** (ADR 0057 §20; the
    spec's amendment, decision 23): 4 WASM sources among the extracted,
    not 7; 19 cases hold a poll function, not 2, which counted the HIR
    cases alone (20 with step 0's case); 85 native roots take the full
    check, not 78: 5.3b's 78 and seven extracted sources that implement
    a trait on a primitive, six that give E0013 and one,
    `deeply_nested_satisfiable_bound_is_accepted`, that lowers clean (86
    with step 0's case); `Stmt` has 27 variants, 26 of them native; spec
    §8's projection of +1 to 4 s of local wall for one form, over the
    base, measured about +17 to 18 s with N's before form alone (part 2)
    and +15 to 18 s with both forms (part 4), each against the base's
    37.5 to 38.0 s as the Selfhost job runs `selfhost.rs` (ADR 0057
    §19, §20).
15. **The CI change in Task 6:** `ci.yml`'s only change is the comment
    above the Selfhost job, which now names three 1 MB stack tests, MIR's
    among them (ADR 0057 §17); it lands with the depth test it names. No
    local test can see a comment, so no mutant reaches it.
16. **Six tasks test first, then the records** **(differs)**: spec §9's
    cut, with these changes:
    - **the `\r\n` test with the cases in Task 3,** not Task 6: the
      prototype built it with the cases (`599ac774`), it checks S0's
      side from Task 3 and N's from Task 4 on, and the prototype's
      `eafdae25`, the fix of that test for a Windows checkout, is folded
      into it at each task (`split.py`'s `FIXES`);
    - **Task 3's code is one re-export,** `nova_driver::mir_dump`, which
      the harness reads `KINDS` from: its tests commit fails to compile
      for want of it (Task 3, Step 2); the cases and the harness are the
      task;
    - **`nova-mir`'s `dump.rs` is new, so its unit test comes with it** in
      Task 1's code patch; Task 1's tests patch holds `selfhost.rs`'s S0
      tests, which fail against the base's `nova`;
    - **`lower_tests.rs`'s two `..Default::default()`s are code** (Task 1):

25. **The MIR mismatch report, as spec §4.3 has it** (the user's answer,
    as given: build it as the spec says, with a test and mutants; no
    departure). Spec §4.3: "MIR has no spans, so for a MIR dump
    `compare.rs`'s report names the root, the function and block above
    the first line that differs, and the statement's index there, where
    other dumps give a source line." The prototype had not built it;
    step 0 does (ADR 0057 §11), in Task 4 (decision 16).
    - **The walk:** `first_difference` walks each root's part by the
      dump's indentation as it reads the lines both sides share, when
      the part's first line is `(Mir`, and `Mismatch::place` holds where
      the first differing line is; `report` gives it in place of a source
      line, `at line 18 of its dump, function "g.1", block bb1, statement
      1`. Every other dump's report is as it was.
    - **Which side:** the function and the block come from the shared
      lines above, so either side's text gives them; the differing line
      itself is S0's, or N's where S0's output ended, and decides what is
      named: the root's first line names the root alone; a line outside
      any function (an extern, MIR's diagnostics) says so; a function's
      header names that function, a temp its function, a block's header
      the function and that block; a statement or the terminator gives
      its index in the block from 0, as `Block::stmts` counts, the
      terminator after the last statement.
    - **Its test,**
      `the_mir_mismatch_report_names_the_function_block_and_statement`,
      beside the earlier report tests, writes two dumps by hand, two
      functions in one root and a second root, so the walk must stop at
      the nearest function, and asserts the report's text, whole for a
      statement and its first line for each other rule. Against the
      base's `compare.rs` it fails at its first assertion: the report
      names no function (Task 4, Step 2). Twelve mutants, one for each
      rule (178 to 189), are caught.
    - In `compare.rs`'s style: no map, no new crate, MSRV 1.78
      (`bool::then`, `str::trim_start_matches`). Cost if wrong: a
      report's place, never a comparison's outcome.

- [ ] **Step 2: The tools and the process limit.**
  - `clang-18 --version | head -n 1` prints

    checks' second clippy; install it as the conventions say if not).
  - The process limit, as the conventions set it: `id -u` says whether a
    pids control group is needed (`0`, root). Then the shell's membership:
    on cgroup v1, `grep nova-limit /proc/self/cgroup` prints
    `N:pids:/nova-limit` and `cat /sys/fs/cgroup/pids/nova-limit/pids.max`
    prints `600`; on cgroup v2, `cat /proc/self/cgroup` prints
    `0::/nova-limit` (or the systemd scope's path), and
    `cat "/sys/fs/cgroup$(sed -n 's/^0:://p' /proc/self/cgroup)/pids.max"`
    prints `600`; with `ulimit -Su 600` alone, `ulimit -Su` prints `600`.
    Record which way, and its output, in the ledger.

  ```sh
  cargo build --locked --workspace
  cargo test --locked --workspace --no-run
  NOVA_REQUIRE_NODE=1 cargo test --locked --workspace --no-fail-fast > "$P/base-54.txt" 2>&1
  grep '^test result' "$P/base-54.txt" | awk '{p += $4; f += $6; i += $8} END {print p, f, i}'
  grep ' FAILED$' "$P/base-54.txt"
  mkdir -p "$P/nova-base-54" && cp target/debug/nova target/debug/libnova_runtime.a "$P/nova-base-54/"
  cargo test --locked --workspace -- --list > "$P/base-list-54.txt" 2>&1
  grep -c ': test$' "$P/base-list-54.txt"
  cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost -- --list > "$P/base-list-sh-54.txt" 2>&1
  grep -c ': test$' "$P/base-list-sh-54.txt"
  cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost --no-run
  time SH
  ```

+
+// 5.4 (spec
+// `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §3, §7; ADR
+// 0057): S0's hidden `nova check --dump-mir FILE…`, natively, and its
+// before form, `--dump-mir --before-async`. Until N prints MIR
+// ([`N_MIR`]), the MIR dump's inputs run through S0 alone.
+
+/// S0's MIR dump (5.4, ADR 0057): the hidden `check --dump-mir`, before
+/// the roots; after the async transform.
+const MIR_S0: &[&str] = &["check", "--dump-mir"];
+
+/// S0's MIR dump before the async transform: `check --dump-mir
+/// --before-async`.
+const MIR_BEFORE_S0: &[&str] = &["check", "--dump-mir", "--before-async"];
+
+/// Whether N prints MIR, `selfhost mir --std DIR [--before-async]` (spec
+/// 5.4 §6.3). Until it does, the MIR dump's inputs (the native corpus's
+/// roots that run, the HIR cases that reach MIR and `cases/mir/`, with
+/// both line endings) run through S0 alone, whose dumps must be well
+/// formed, hold a header per root, every form of `KINDS` but those a
+/// native build never prints, `Await` and `async=true` in the before form
+/// alone, and every code MIR gives natively from source. Later parts of
+/// 5.4 port N's MIR and set it, and every set is then compared byte for
+/// byte.
+const N_MIR: bool = false;
+
+/// The MIR dump's two forms (spec 5.4 §3.1, §7.2): after the async
+/// transform, what the emitter reads, and before it, as lowering left it.
+#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
+enum MirForm {
+    After,
+    Before,
+}
+
+impl MirForm {
+    /// S0's arguments for the form.
+    fn s0(self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
+        match self {
+            MirForm::After => MIR_S0,
+            MirForm::Before => MIR_BEFORE_S0,
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// The MIR dump through both sides in `form`: S0's `check --dump-mir`,
+/// and N's `mir --std DIR`, each with `--before-async` for the before form,
+/// followed by the roots.
+fn mir_in(form: MirForm) -> &'static Dump {
+    static DUMPS: [OnceLock<Dump>; 2] = [OnceLock::new(), OnceLock::new()];
+    DUMPS[form as usize].get_or_init(|| {
+        let std_dir = std_dir();
+        let mut n = vec!["mir", "--std", std_dir.as_str()];
+        if form == MirForm::Before {
+            n.push("--before-async");
+        }
+        Dump {
+            s0: form.s0(),
+            n: leak_args(&n),
+        }
+    })
+}
+
+/// `form` on `files`, relative to `dir`, each a program's root, in one
+/// process a side, each side exiting with `code`: compared through
+/// [`compare_batch`] once N prints MIR ([`N_MIR`]), and S0's alone, through
+/// [`run`], before; the roots' names under [`HIR_ARGS_LIMIT`], and the dump
+/// well formed ([`assert_well_formed`]) with a header and a `(Mir` line per
+/// root. Returns S0's output.
+fn agree_on_mir_dump(form: MirForm, dir: &Path, files: &[String], code: i32) -> String {
+    let bytes: usize = files.iter().map(|f| f.len() + 1).sum();
+    assert!(
+        bytes <= HIR_ARGS_LIMIT,
+        "{bytes} bytes of root names on one command line, past HIR_ARGS_LIMIT"
+    );
+    let text = if N_MIR {
+        compare_batch(mir_in(form), dir, files, code, &nova(), selfhost())
+            .unwrap_or_else(|report| panic!("{report}"))
+    } else {
+        let s0 = run(&nova(), form.s0(), dir, files).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{e}"));
+        assert_eq!(
+            s0.code,
+            Some(code),
+            "{}: {}",
+            batch_name(&nova(), form.s0(), files),
+            s0.stderr
+        );
+        String::from_utf8(s0.stdout).expect("the MIR dump is UTF-8")
+    };
+    assert_well_formed(&text);
+    let parts = parts_by_root(&text);
+    assert_eq!(
+        parts.iter().map(|(r, _)| r.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
+        files.iter().map(String::as_str).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
+        "{form:?}: a header per root"
+    );
+    for (root, part) in &parts {
+        assert!(
+            part.starts_with("(Mir ") || part.starts_with("IOERR "),
+            "{form:?} {root}: {part}"
+        );
+    }
+    text
+}
+
+/// [`agree_on_mir_dump`] on `files`: in one process a side, or, when their
+/// names would pass [`HIR_ARGS_LIMIT`], in [`agree_in_parts`] side by side,
+/// each part under it.
+fn agree_on_mir_in(form: MirForm, dir: &Path, files: &[String], code: i32) -> String {
+    if files.is_empty() {
+        return String::new();
+    }
+    let bytes: usize = files.iter().map(|f| f.len() + 1).sum();
+    let parts = bytes.div_ceil(HIR_ARGS_LIMIT);
+    if parts <= 1 {
+        return agree_on_mir_dump(form, dir, files, code);
+    }
+    agree_in_parts(files, parts, |part| {
+        agree_on_mir_dump(form, dir, part, code)
+    })
+}
+
+/// `nova check`'s help names no `--dump-mir`, every `nova check` command
+/// line whose first argument after `check` is not the hidden flag is parsed
+/// as the base parses it, `--dump-mir=…` included, and the MIR dump's own
+/// parser takes `--before-async` and files and refuses everything else
+/// with one error under its own usage line, `--std`, `--target`, `--test`,
+/// `--help`, `-h` and the flag again among them; with no file, one line on
+/// stderr; each exiting 2 with nothing on stdout (5.4, spec §3.1).
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_dump_flag_is_hidden_and_refused_as_the_spec_says() {
+    let help = Command::new(nova())
+        .args(["check", "--help"])
+        .output()
+        .expect("run nova check --help");
+    let help = String::from_utf8_lossy(&help.stdout).into_owned();
+    assert!(help.contains("--target"), "{help}");
+    assert!(!help.contains("dump-mir"), "{help}");
+    assert!(!help.contains("before-async"), "{help}");
+    let out = Command::new(nova())
+        .args(["check", "--help", "--dump-mir"])
+        .output()
+        .expect("run nova check --help --dump-mir");
+    assert_eq!(out.status.code(), Some(0));
+    assert_eq!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout), help);
+    let (opts, file, target) = ("[OPTIONS] [FILE]", "<FILE>", "--target <TARGET> [FILE]");
+    for (args, named, usage) in [
+        (&["check", "--dump-mi", "a.nova"][..], "--dump-mi", opts),
+        (&["check", "--dump-mirr", "a.nova"], "--dump-mirr", opts),
+        (&["check", "--dump-mir=1", "a.nova"], "--dump-mir", opts),
+        (
+            &["check", "--dump-mir=before-async", "a.nova"],
+            "--dump-mir",
+            opts,
+        ),
+        (&["check", "--dump-mir=", "a.nova"], "--dump-mir", opts),
+        (
+            &["check", "--before-async", "a.nova"],
+            "--before-async",
+            opts,
+        ),
+        (&["check", "a.nova", "--dump-mir"], "--dump-mir", file),
+        (
+            &["check", "--target", "native", "--dump-mir", "a.nova"],
+            "--dump-mir",
+            target,
+        ),
+        (&["check", "--test", "--dump-mir", "a.nova"], "--test", opts),
+    ] {
+        let out = Command::new(nova())
+            .args(args)
+            .output()
+            .expect("run nova check");
+        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
+        let tip = format!("  tip: to pass '{named}' as a value, use '-- {named}'\n\n");
+        assert_eq!(out.status.code(), Some(2), "{args:?}: {stderr}");
+        assert_eq!(
+            stderr,
+            format!(
+                "error: unexpected argument '{named}' found\n\n{tip}\
+                 Usage: nova{EXE_SUFFIX} check {usage}\n\n\
+                 For more information, try '--help'.\n"
+            ),
+            "{args:?}"
+        );
+        assert!(out.stdout.is_empty(), "{args:?}");
+    }
+    for args in [
+        &["--std"][..],
+        &["--std", "a.nova"],
+        &["--target", "native", "a.nova"],
+        &["--target=wasm", "a.nova"],
+        &["--test", "a.nova"],
+        &["--help"],
+        &["-h"],
+        &["--dump-mir", "a.nova"],
+        &["--dump-hir", "a.nova"],
+        &["--before-async", "--before-async", "a.nova"],
+        &["--before-async=1", "a.nova"],
+        &["--before-asyn", "a.nova"],
+        &["--before-async"],
+        &[],
+    ] {
+        let out = Command::new(nova())
+            .args(MIR_S0)
+            .args(args)
+            .output()
+            .expect("run nova check --dump-mir");
+        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr);
+        assert_eq!(out.status.code(), Some(2), "{args:?}: {stderr}");
+        assert!(stderr.starts_with("error: "), "{args:?}: {stderr}");
+        assert_eq!(stderr.matches("error:").count(), 1, "{args:?}: {stderr}");
+        assert_eq!(
+            stderr.matches("Usage: ").count(),
+            stderr.matches("Usage: nova check --dump-mir ").count(),
+            "{args:?}: {stderr}"
+        );
+        // The form's own refusal, which is not clap's, names its flag.
+        if !stderr.contains("Usage: ") {
+            assert_eq!(stderr, "error: --dump-mir needs a FILE\n", "{args:?}");
+        }
+        assert!(out.stdout.is_empty(), "{args:?}");
+    }
+    // `--` before a root that starts with `-`.
+    let dir = fresh_dir("mir-flag");
+    std::fs::write(dir.join("-a.nova"), "fn main() {}\n").expect("write -a.nova");
+    let out = Command::new(nova())
+        .current_dir(&dir)
+        .args(MIR_BEFORE_S0)
+        .args(["--", "-a.nova"])
+        .output()
+        .expect("run nova check --dump-mir --before-async -- -a.nova");
+    assert_eq!(out.status.code(), Some(0));
+    assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout).starts_with(
+        "== -a.nova\n(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=false functions=1 "
+    ));
+}
+
+/// Each root's part follows a header with its path as given, in both
+/// forms: a root that cannot be read is `IOERR` and its kind, the rest are
+/// dumped, and the exit code is 2; a package's module that does not run is
+/// `runs=false`, before the loader, whether it can be read or not; a
+/// program that stops before MIR, in the parser, the resolver or the type
+/// checker, `checked=false` with no diagnostic; E0601 `lowered=false` with
+/// MIR's diagnostics, the first with no place; a clean program its
+/// functions, whose async ones the before form shows with their awaits
+/// (5.4, spec §4.1).
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_dump_has_a_header_per_root_and_says_how_far_each_got() {
+    let dir = fresh_dir("mir-headers");
+    let write = |name: &str, text: &[u8]| {
+        std::fs::write(dir.join(name), text).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("write {name}: {e}"))
+    };
+    write("a.nova", b"fn main() {}\n");
+    write("b.nova", b"fn g( {");
+    write("c.nova", b"fn f(x: Nope) {}\nfn f() {}\n");
+    write("d.nova", b"fn main() { let x: Int = \"s\" }\n");
+    write("e.nova", b"fn helper() {}\n");
+    write("f.nova", b"fn main(x: Int) {}\n");
+    write(
+        "g.nova",
+        b"async fn h() -> Int { 1 }\nasync fn k() -> Int { h().await + 1 }\n\
+          fn main() { println(\"${block_on(k())}\") }\n",
+    );
+    write("latin1.nova", b"fn \xe9() {}\n");
+    std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("pkg").join("src")).expect("create pkg/src");
+    write("pkg/nova.toml", MANIFEST.as_bytes());
+    write("pkg/src/main.nova", b"import shapes\nfn main() {}\n");
+    write("pkg/src/shapes.nova", b"pub type Shape =\n  | Dot\n");
+    let files: Vec<String> = [
+        "a.nova",
+        "missing.nova",
+        "b.nova",
+        "c.nova",
+        "d.nova",
+        "e.nova",
+        "f.nova",
+        "g.nova",
+        "latin1.nova",
+        "pkg/src/main.nova",
+        "pkg/src/shapes.nova",
+        "pkg/src/missing.nova",
+    ]
+    .iter()
+    .map(|f| f.to_string())
+    .collect();
+    let after = agree_on_mir_in(MirForm::After, &dir, &files, 2);
+    let before = agree_on_mir_in(MirForm::Before, &dir, &files, 2);
+    for (form, out) in [(MirForm::After, &after), (MirForm::Before, &before)] {
+        let parts: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String> =
+            parts_by_root(out).into_iter().collect();
+        let first = |root: &str| parts[root].lines().next().unwrap_or("").to_string();
+        assert_eq!(parts["missing.nova"], "IOERR NotFound\n", "{form:?}");
+        assert_eq!(parts["latin1.nova"], "IOERR InvalidData\n", "{form:?}");
+        for root in ["pkg/src/shapes.nova", "pkg/src/missing.nova"] {
+            assert_eq!(parts[root], "(Mir runs=false)\n(Diagnostics)\n", "{form:?}");
+        }
+        for root in ["b.nova", "c.nova", "d.nova"] {
+            assert_eq!(
+                parts[root], "(Mir runs=true checked=false)\n(Diagnostics)\n",
+                "{form:?} {root}"
+            );
+        }
+        assert_eq!(
+            parts["e.nova"],
+            "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=false)\n(Diagnostics\n  \
+             (Error \"E0601\" \"no `main` function found; `nova run` needs a `fn main()` \
+             entry point\"))\n",
+            "{form:?}"
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            parts["f.nova"],
+            "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=false)\n(Diagnostics\n  \
+             (Error \"E0601\" \"`main` cannot be generic or take parameters\"\n    \
+             (Primary \"f\":3..7 \"invalid `main` signature\")))\n",
+            "{form:?}"
+        );
+        let lowered = |n: usize| {
+            let flag = form == MirForm::After;
+            format!(
+                "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered={flag} functions={n} \
+                 externs=0"
+            )
+        };
+        assert_eq!(first("a.nova"), lowered(1), "{form:?}");
+        assert_eq!(first("pkg/src/main.nova"), lowered(1), "{form:?}");
+        assert!(
+            parts["a.nova"].ends_with(
+                "\n  (Function \"main\" params=0 takes_env=false captures=0 \
+                 ret=Unit async=false\n    (Temp t0 Unit)\n    (Block bb0\n      \
+                 (ConstUnit t0)\n      (Return _))))\n(Diagnostics)\n"
+            ),
+            "{form:?}: {}",
+            parts["a.nova"]
+        );
+        // `main`, `block_on`'s instance, `k` and `h`; after the transform
+        // each async function's poll function and wrapper.
+        let g = &parts["g.nova"];
+        let n = if form == MirForm::After { 6 } else { 4 };
+        assert_eq!(first("g.nova"), lowered(n), "{form:?}");
+        let awaits = g.matches("\n      (Await ").count();
+        let flagged = g.matches(" async=true\n").count();
+        match form {
+            MirForm::After => assert_eq!((awaits, flagged), (0, 0), "{g}"),
+            MirForm::Before => assert_eq!((awaits, flagged), (1, 2), "{g}"),
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// The MIR dump's runs are settled as the HIR dump's, in both forms, on
+/// both sides (5.4, spec §7.2): no MIR message names a path, but the dump
+/// runs the loader, whose E0004 does.
+#[test]
+fn every_run_of_a_mir_dump_settles_e0004s_paths_on_windows() {
+    let windows = b"(Diagnostics\n  (Error \"E0004\" \"`a` is both: app\\x5csrc\\x5ca.nova, and the entry at app\\x5cnova.toml:6\"\n".to_vec();
+    let settled = settled_stdout(RESOLUTION_S0, windows.clone(), true);
+    assert_ne!(settled, windows);
+    for form in [MirForm::After, MirForm::Before] {
+        let args: Vec<&str> = form.s0().iter().chain(["a.nova"].iter()).copied().collect();
+        assert_eq!(settled_stdout(&args, windows.clone(), true), settled);
+        assert_eq!(settled_stdout(&args, windows.clone(), false), windows);
+        assert_eq!(
+            settled_stdout(mir_in(form).n, windows.clone(), true),
+            settled
+        );
+    }
+}
````

+/// Which dumps of a root one check gives: the HIR dump's, and the MIR
+/// dump's after the async transform and before it (5.4).
+#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
+struct Wanted {
+    hir: bool,
+    mir: bool,
+    before: bool,
+}
+
+impl Wanted {
+    const HIR: Wanted = Wanted {
+        hir: true,
+        mir: false,
+        before: false,
+    };
+}
+
+/// One root's dumps, as [`Wanted`] asked for them.
+#[derive(Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
+struct RootDumps {
+    hir: Option<String>,
+    mir: Option<String>,
+    before: Option<String>,
+}
+
+impl RootDumps {
+    /// The MIR forms `wanted` names, each from `reached`, given whether it
+    /// is the before form.
+    fn mir_forms<'a>(
+        &mut self,
+        labels: &[(FileId, String)],
+        wanted: Wanted,
+        reached: impl Fn(bool) -> nova_mir::dump::Reached<'a>,
+    ) {
+        if wanted.mir {
+            self.mir = Some(nova_mir::dump::dump_program(labels, reached(false)));
+        }
+        if wanted.before {
+            self.before = Some(nova_mir::dump::dump_program(labels, reached(true)));
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// The MIR dumps of programs, natively (`nova_mir::dump`; spec
+/// `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §3.3, ADR
+/// 0057): for each root, a program that does not run, as `check` decides
+/// it, is `runs=false`, before the front end; otherwise [`HirDumps`]'s
+/// pipeline and check, std's shared, and, when neither gave an error, the
+/// entry `main` kept and the module lowered natively, after the async
+/// transform or, with `before_async`, before it. A user sees nothing of
+/// it: `check`, `build`, `run`, `test` and the language server keep their
+/// own paths.
+pub struct MirDumps {
+    hir: HirDumps,
+    before_async: bool,
+}
+
+impl MirDumps {
+    /// std's modules, natively, parsed, resolved, their items collected
+    /// and their bodies checked once, each program's dump after the async
+    /// transform or, with `before_async`, before it.
+    pub fn new(before_async: bool) -> MirDumps {
+        MirDumps {
+            hir: HirDumps::unshared(Target::Native, false),
+            before_async,
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// [`MirDumps::new`], each program resolving and checking std again,
+    /// as `check` does: the dumps are the same, which a test checks.
+    pub fn unshared(before_async: bool) -> MirDumps {
+        MirDumps {
+            hir: HirDumps::unshared(Target::Native, false),
+            before_async,
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// The roots whose check the shared path handed to the full one, in
+    /// the order they were dumped ([`HirDumps::fallbacks`]).
+    pub fn fallbacks(&self) -> Vec<std::path::PathBuf> {
+        self.hir.fallbacks()
+    }
+
+    /// The MIR dump of the program whose root is `path` (the type's docs).
+    /// `Err` when the root cannot be read.
+    pub fn dump(&self, path: &Path) -> std::io::Result<String> {
+        let wanted = Wanted {
+            hir: false,
+            mir: !self.before_async,
+            before: self.before_async,
+        };
+        let dumps = self.hir.dumps(path, wanted)?;
+        Ok(if self.before_async {
+            dumps.before
+        } else {
+            dumps.mir
+        }
+        .unwrap_or_default())
+    }
+}
+

 /// (`test`): the item tables and the functions of a program of no module of
 /// its own, which are std's in every program, and every diagnostic, the
@@ -751,7 +938,10 @@ fn runtime_impls(module: &hir::Module, definitions: &Definitions) -> nova_mir::R
 
 /// The options MIR lowering takes for this context's target.
 fn mir_options(runtime: Option<&nova_mir::RuntimeImpls>) -> nova_mir::Options<'_> {
-    nova_mir::Options { runtime }
+    nova_mir::Options {
+        runtime,
+        ..Default::default()
