````diff
 /// Rename every `main` declared outside the entry module, `ModuleId(0)`,
diff --git a/crates/nova-mir/src/dump.rs b/crates/nova-mir/src/dump.rs
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..8f5599af
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-mir/src/dump.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,902 @@
+//! The MIR dump: what monomorphization, lowering and the async transform
+//! built natively for one program, as text, one entry per line, which the
+//! self-hosted compiler (`selfhost/`) prints too, so that the two can be
+//! compared byte for byte (spec
+//! `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §4; ADR 0057
+//! writes the grammar out, with an example of every form). It carries no
+//! stability promise: it changes whenever MIR does, on both sides in one
+//! pull request. `nova check --dump-mir FILE…` prints a program's part
+//! after a `== FILE` header, and `--before-async` the module as lowering
+//! left it, before the async transform (spec §3.1).
+//!
+//! - It follows the earlier dumps' conventions (ADR 0053 §1): a line is its
+//!   indentation, two spaces per depth, `(` and its kind, its values and its
+//!   fields `key=value`; its children follow, one level deeper, and its `)`
+//!   closes it at the end of its last line. Strings and symbols are quoted
+//!   as the AST dump quotes strings; diagnostics print as the resolution
+//!   dump's, MIR's alone.
+//! - **The first line says how far the root got:** `(Mir runs=false)` for
+//!   a package module `check` does not lower; `checked=false` for a
+//!   program that stopped before MIR, with no diagnostic, which the HIR
+//!   dump prints; `lowered=false` for MIR's diagnostics; `lowered=true`,
+//!   `async_lowered=` whether the transform ran, the counts, then the
+//!   externs and every function.
+//! - **Order is MIR's own,** nothing sorted: the functions as the worklist
+//!   made them, the shim after them, the transform's wrappers after all;
+//!   the externs as collected; temps and blocks by index.
+//! - A statement is one line: its [`Stmt`] variant's name, then every field
+//!   in declaration order: `_` for an absent destination, a temp `t<n>`, a
+//!   block `bb<n>`, an integer in decimal, a float as the 16 lowercase hex
+//!   digits of `to_bits`, a string or a symbol quoted, an operator, a
+//!   class, a runtime function or a memory operation by its variant's name,
+//!   a list in parentheses, each capture's, field's or element's class
+//!   beside its temp (`t3:Ptr`), each switch arm's value beside its block
+//!   (`0:bb2`).
+//! - **Symbols print raw,** with absolute `DefId`s (`Vec_T.push.147`): MIR
+//!   is monomorphized per program from `main`, so it has no std part.
+
+use std::fmt::Write;
+
+use nova_diagnostics::Diagnostic;
+use nova_resolver::dump::{quote, write_diagnostics, Labels};
+
+use crate::{
+    BlockId, ExternAbi, Function, MemOp, MirTy, Module, OperandClass, Stmt, Temp, Terminator,
+};
+
+/// Every form the dump prints, as [`nova_resolver::dump::forms_on`] reads
+/// a line, beyond the fields whose values are numbers: the harness checks
+/// that the native dumps of the corpus and the cases print each of them but
+/// [`NOT_NATIVE`]'s and print nothing unlisted
+/// (`crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs`).
+pub const KINDS: &[&str] = &[
+    "(Mir",
+    "(Mir runs=true",
+    "(Mir runs=false",
+    "(Mir checked=true",
+    "(Mir checked=false",
+    "(Mir lowered=true",
+    "(Mir lowered=false",
+    "(Mir async_lowered=true",
+    "(Mir async_lowered=false",
+    "(Extern",
+    "abi=C",
+    "abi=Js",
+    "ret=I64",
+    "ret=F64",
+    "ret=I8",
+    "ret=Ptr",
+    "ret=Unit",
+    "(Function",
+    "(Function takes_env=true",
+    "(Function takes_env=false",
+    "(Function async=true",
+    "(Function async=false",
+    "(Temp",
+    "(Block",
+    "(ConstInt",
+    "(ConstFloat",
+    "(ConstBool",
+    "(ConstStr",
+    "(ConstUnit",
+    "(Copy",
+    "(Bin",
+    "(Neg",
+    "(Not",
+    "(BitNot",
+    "(Call",
+    "(CallIndirect",
+    "(Await",
+    "(CallRuntime",
+    "(MakeClosure",
+    "(MakeSum",
+    "(SumTag",
+    "(SumField",
+    "(MakeRecord",
+    "(RecordField",
+    "(SetField",
+    "(MakeArray",
+    "(ArrayAlloc",
+    "(ArrayLen",
+    "(ArrayGet",
+    "(ArraySet",
+    "(Mem",
+    "(Goto",
+    "(Branch",
+    "(Switch",
+    "(Return",
+    "(Trap",
+    "(Diagnostics",
+    "(Error",
+    "(Primary",
+    "(Note",
+];
+
+/// The forms of [`KINDS`] only a WASM build's MIR holds, which the native
+/// dump never prints: a memory access (`Stmt::Mem`) and a JS extern. The
+/// printer takes any module, so it prints them, and its unit test does.
+pub const NOT_NATIVE: &[&str] = &["(Mem", "abi=Js"];
+
+/// How far a root got, which its part's first line says (module docs).
+pub enum Reached<'a> {
+    /// A package module that `check` does not lower: no front end ran.
+    NotRun,
+    /// An error before MIR, which the HIR dump prints.
+    NotChecked,
+    /// MIR's result: its diagnostics, or the module, after the async
+    /// transform when `async_lowered`, as lowering left it otherwise.
+    Lowered {
+        result: &'a Result<Module, Vec<Diagnostic>>,
+        async_lowered: bool,
+    },
+}
+
+/// A program's part (module docs): its first line and, once lowered, the
+/// module; then `(Diagnostics)`, MIR's alone, each place under its file's
+/// label in `labels`.
+pub fn dump_program(labels: &Labels, reached: Reached<'_>) -> String {
+    let mut d = Dump::default();
+    d.open(0, "Mir");
+    let mut diagnostics: &[Diagnostic] = &[];
+    match reached {
+        Reached::NotRun => d.bool_field("runs", false),
+        Reached::NotChecked => {
+            d.bool_field("runs", true);
+            d.bool_field("checked", false);
+        }
+        Reached::Lowered {
+            result,
+            async_lowered,
+        } => {
+            d.bool_field("runs", true);
+            d.bool_field("checked", true);
+            d.bool_field("lowered", result.is_ok());
+            match result {
+                Ok(module) => {
+                    d.bool_field("async_lowered", async_lowered);
+                    d.module(module);
+                }
+                Err(errors) => diagnostics = errors,
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    d.close();
+    d.out.push('\n');
+    write_diagnostics(&mut d.out, labels, diagnostics);
+    d.out
+}
+
+/// The text being written.
+#[derive(Default)]
+struct Dump {
+    out: String,
+}
+
+impl Dump {
+    // --- Lines and values ---
+
+    /// A line, up to its kind.
+    fn open(&mut self, depth: usize, kind: &str) {
+        if !self.out.is_empty() && !self.out.ends_with('\n') {
+            self.out.push('\n');
+        }
+        for _ in 0..depth {
+            self.out.push_str("  ");
+        }
+        self.out.push('(');
+        self.out.push_str(kind);
+    }
+
+    fn close(&mut self) {
+        self.out.push(')');
+    }
+
+    /// ` key=value`.
+    fn field(&mut self, key: &str, value: &str) {
+        let _ = write!(self.out, " {key}={value}");
+    }
+
+    fn bool_field(&mut self, key: &str, value: bool) {
+        let _ = write!(self.out, " {key}={value}");
+    }
+
+    /// ` ` and a value as it is.
+    fn value(&mut self, text: &str) {
+        self.out.push(' ');
+        self.out.push_str(text);
+    }
+
+    /// ` ` and `text` quoted.
+    fn string(&mut self, text: &str) {
+        self.out.push(' ');
+        quote(&mut self.out, text);
+    }
+
+    /// ` ` and a list in parentheses, its items separated by one space.
+    fn list(&mut self, items: impl IntoIterator<Item = String>) {
+        let items: Vec<String> = items.into_iter().collect();
+        let _ = write!(self.out, " ({})", items.join(" "));
+    }
+
+    // --- The module ---
+
+    /// The counts, the externs and every function, after the first line's
+    /// fields.
+    fn module(&mut self, module: &Module) {
+        self.field("functions", &module.functions.len().to_string());
+        self.field("externs", &module.externs.len().to_string());
+        for e in &module.externs {
+            self.open(1, "Extern");
+            self.string(&e.symbol);
+            let abi = match e.abi {
+                ExternAbi::C => "C",
+                ExternAbi::Js => "Js",
+            };
+            self.field("abi", abi);
+            self.list(e.params.iter().map(|&c| class(c).to_string()));
+            self.field("ret", class(e.ret));
+            self.close();
+        }
+        for f in &module.functions {
+            self.function(f);
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A function: its line, its temps, one a line, and its blocks.
+    fn function(&mut self, f: &Function) {
+        self.open(1, "Function");
+        self.string(&f.name);
+        self.field("params", &f.params.to_string());
+        self.bool_field("takes_env", f.takes_env);
+        self.field("captures", &f.capture_count.to_string());
+        self.field("ret", class(f.ret));
+        self.bool_field("async", f.is_async);
+        for (i, &c) in f.temps.iter().enumerate() {
+            self.open(2, "Temp");
+            let _ = write!(self.out, " t{i} {}", class(c));
+            self.close();
+        }
+        for (i, block) in f.blocks.iter().enumerate() {
+            self.open(2, "Block");
+            let _ = write!(self.out, " bb{i}");
+            for stmt in &block.stmts {
+                self.stmt(stmt);
+            }
+            self.terminator(&block.term);
+            self.close();
+        }
+        self.close();
+    }
+
+    /// A statement, one line (module docs).
+    fn stmt(&mut self, stmt: &Stmt) {
+        let (kind, values): (&str, Vec<String>) = match stmt {
+            Stmt::ConstInt(dst, v) => ("ConstInt", vec![temp(*dst), v.to_string()]),
+            Stmt::ConstFloat(dst, v) => (
+                "ConstFloat",
+                vec![temp(*dst), format!("{:016x}", v.to_bits())],
+            ),
+            Stmt::ConstBool(dst, v) => ("ConstBool", vec![temp(*dst), v.to_string()]),
+            Stmt::ConstStr(dst, s) => ("ConstStr", vec![temp(*dst), quoted(s)]),
+            Stmt::ConstUnit(dst) => ("ConstUnit", vec![temp(*dst)]),
+            Stmt::Copy { dst, src } => ("Copy", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*src)]),
+            Stmt::Bin {
+                dst,
+                op,
+                class: c,
+                lhs,
+                rhs,
+            } => (
+                "Bin",
+                vec![
+                    temp(*dst),
+                    format!("{op:?}"),
+                    operand_class(*c).to_string(),
+                    temp(*lhs),
+                    temp(*rhs),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::Neg { dst, class: c, src } => (
+                "Neg",
+                vec![temp(*dst), operand_class(*c).to_string(), temp(*src)],
+            ),
+            Stmt::Not { dst, src } => ("Not", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*src)]),
+            Stmt::BitNot { dst, src } => ("BitNot", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*src)]),
+            Stmt::Call { dst, callee, args } => {
+                ("Call", vec![dest(*dst), quoted(callee), temps(args)])
+            }
+            Stmt::CallIndirect {
+                dst,
+                callee,
+                params,
+                ret,
+                args,
+            } => (
+                "CallIndirect",
+                vec![
+                    dest(*dst),
+                    temp(*callee),
+                    classes(params),
+                    class(*ret).to_string(),
+                    temps(args),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::Await { dst, future } => ("Await", vec![dest(*dst), temp(*future)]),
+            Stmt::CallRuntime { dst, func, args } => (
+                "CallRuntime",
+                vec![dest(*dst), format!("{func:?}"), temps(args)],
+            ),
+            Stmt::MakeClosure {
+                dst,
+                code,
+                captures,
+            } => (
+                "MakeClosure",
+                vec![temp(*dst), quoted(code), typed(captures)],
+            ),
+            Stmt::MakeSum { dst, tag, fields } => {
+                ("MakeSum", vec![temp(*dst), tag.to_string(), typed(fields)])
+            }
+            Stmt::SumTag { dst, sum } => ("SumTag", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*sum)]),
+            Stmt::SumField {
+                dst,
+                sum,
+                index,
+                ty,
+            } => (
+                "SumField",
+                vec![
+                    temp(*dst),
+                    temp(*sum),
+                    index.to_string(),
+                    class(*ty).to_string(),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::MakeRecord { dst, fields } => ("MakeRecord", vec![temp(*dst), typed(fields)]),
+            Stmt::RecordField {
+                dst,
+                record,
+                index,
+                ty,
+            } => (
+                "RecordField",
+                vec![
+                    temp(*dst),
+                    temp(*record),
+                    index.to_string(),
+                    class(*ty).to_string(),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::SetField {
+                record,
+                index,
+                value,
+                ty,
+            } => (
+                "SetField",
+                vec![
+                    temp(*record),
+                    index.to_string(),
+                    temp(*value),
+                    class(*ty).to_string(),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::MakeArray { dst, elems } => ("MakeArray", vec![temp(*dst), typed(elems)]),
+            Stmt::ArrayAlloc { dst, len } => ("ArrayAlloc", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*len)]),
+            Stmt::ArrayLen { dst, arr } => ("ArrayLen", vec![temp(*dst), temp(*arr)]),
+            Stmt::ArrayGet {
+                dst,
+                arr,
+                index,
+                ty,
+            } => (
+                "ArrayGet",
+                vec![temp(*dst), temp(*arr), temp(*index), class(*ty).to_string()],
+            ),
+            Stmt::ArraySet {
+                arr,
+                index,
+                value,
+                ty,
+            } => (
+                "ArraySet",
+                vec![
+                    temp(*arr),
+                    temp(*index),
+                    temp(*value),
+                    class(*ty).to_string(),
+                ],
+            ),
+            Stmt::Mem { dst, op, args } => (
+                "Mem",
+                vec![dest(*dst), mem_op(*op).to_string(), temps(args)],
+            ),
+        };
+        self.open(3, kind);
+        for v in &values {
+            self.value(v);
+        }
+        self.close();
+    }
+
+    /// A block's terminator, one line at its statements' depth.
+    fn terminator(&mut self, term: &Terminator) {
+        match term {
+            Terminator::Goto(to) => {
+                self.open(3, "Goto");
+                self.value(&block(*to));
+            }
+            Terminator::Branch { cond, then_, else_ } => {
+                self.open(3, "Branch");
+                self.value(&temp(*cond));
+                self.value(&block(*then_));
+                self.value(&block(*else_));
+            }
+            Terminator::Switch {
+                disc,
+                arms,
+                default,
+            } => {
+                self.open(3, "Switch");
+                self.value(&temp(*disc));
+                self.list(arms.iter().map(|(v, b)| format!("{v}:{}", block(*b))));
+                self.value(&block(*default));
+            }
+            Terminator::Return(value) => {
+                self.open(3, "Return");
+                self.value(&dest(*value));
+            }
+            Terminator::Trap => self.open(3, "Trap"),
+        }
+        self.close();
+    }
+}
+
+/// A temp, `t<n>`.
+fn temp(t: Temp) -> String {
+    format!("t{}", t.0)
+}
+
+/// A block, `bb<n>`.
+fn block(b: BlockId) -> String {
+    format!("bb{}", b.0)
+}
+
+/// An optional temp: the temp, or `_`.
+fn dest(t: Option<Temp>) -> String {
+    t.map_or_else(|| "_".to_string(), temp)
+}
+
+/// Temps, a list.
+fn temps(ts: &[Temp]) -> String {
+    let items: Vec<String> = ts.iter().map(|&t| temp(t)).collect();
+    format!("({})", items.join(" "))
+}
+
+/// Classes, a list.
+fn classes(cs: &[MirTy]) -> String {
+    let items: Vec<&str> = cs.iter().map(|&c| class(c)).collect();
+    format!("({})", items.join(" "))
+}
+
+/// Temps with their classes, a list of `t<n>:<class>`.
+fn typed(fields: &[(Temp, MirTy)]) -> String {
+    let items: Vec<String> = fields
+        .iter()
+        .map(|&(t, c)| format!("{}:{}", temp(t), class(c)))
+        .collect();
+    format!("({})", items.join(" "))
+}
+
+/// `text` quoted.
+fn quoted(text: &str) -> String {
+    let mut out = String::new();
+    quote(&mut out, text);
+    out
+}
+
+/// A class by its variant's name.
+fn class(c: MirTy) -> &'static str {
+    match c {
+        MirTy::I64 => "I64",
+        MirTy::F64 => "F64",
+        MirTy::I8 => "I8",
+        MirTy::Ptr => "Ptr",
+        MirTy::Unit => "Unit",
+    }
+}
+
+/// An operand class by its variant's name.
+fn operand_class(c: OperandClass) -> &'static str {
+    match c {
+        OperandClass::Int => "Int",
+        OperandClass::Float => "Float",
+        OperandClass::Bool => "Bool",
+    }
+}
+
+/// A memory operation by its variant's name.
+fn mem_op(op: MemOp) -> &'static str {
+    match op {
+        MemOp::Load8 => "Load8",
+        MemOp::Store8 => "Store8",
+        MemOp::Load64 => "Load64",
+        MemOp::Store64 => "Store64",
+        MemOp::Copy => "Copy",
+        MemOp::Fill => "Fill",
+        MemOp::Pages => "Pages",
+        MemOp::Grow => "Grow",
+        MemOp::HeapBase => "HeapBase",
+        MemOp::Poll => "Poll",
+        MemOp::Abort => "Abort",
+        MemOp::ClosureWord => "ClosureWord",
+        MemOp::ClosureCall => "ClosureCall",
+    }
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    use super::*;
+    use crate::{Block, ExternDecl, RtFunc};
+    use nova_diagnostics::{FileId, Span};
+    use nova_hir::BinOp;
+
+    fn t(n: u32) -> Temp {
+        Temp(n)
+    }
+
+    fn bb(n: u32) -> BlockId {
+        BlockId(n)
+    }
+
+    /// A function of no statement that returns its one temp.
+    fn leaf(name: &str, ret: MirTy) -> Function {
+        Function {
+            name: name.to_string(),
+            params: 1,
+            takes_env: false,
+            capture_count: 0,
+            temps: vec![ret],
+            ret,
+            is_async: false,
+            blocks: vec![Block {
+                stmts: Vec::new(),
+                term: Terminator::Return(Some(t(0))),
+            }],
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A module holding every statement, terminator, class and extern ABI
+    /// once, built by hand: a native build never holds a `Mem` or a JS
+    /// extern ([`NOT_NATIVE`]).
+    fn every_form() -> Module {
+        use MirTy::*;
+        let stmts = vec![
+            Stmt::ConstInt(t(0), -7),
+            Stmt::ConstFloat(t(1), 1.5),
+            Stmt::ConstBool(t(2), true),
+            Stmt::ConstStr(t(3), "a \"b\"\n".to_string()),
+            Stmt::ConstUnit(t(4)),
+            Stmt::Copy {
+                dst: t(0),
+                src: t(5),
+            },
+            Stmt::Bin {
+                dst: t(0),
+                op: BinOp::Shl,
+                class: OperandClass::Int,
+                lhs: t(0),
+                rhs: t(5),
+            },
+            Stmt::Neg {
+                dst: t(1),
+                class: OperandClass::Float,
+                src: t(1),
+            },
+            Stmt::Not {
+                dst: t(2),
+                src: t(2),
+            },
+            Stmt::BitNot {
+                dst: t(0),
+                src: t(0),
+            },
+            Stmt::Call {
+                dst: Some(t(3)),
+                callee: "f.1$i".to_string(),
+                args: vec![t(0), t(1)],
+            },
+            Stmt::Call {
+                dst: None,
+                callee: "g.2".to_string(),
+                args: Vec::new(),
+            },
+            Stmt::CallIndirect {
+                dst: Some(t(0)),
+                callee: t(3),
+                params: vec![I64, F64],
+                ret: I64,
+                args: vec![t(0), t(1)],
+            },
+            Stmt::Await {
+                dst: None,
+                future: t(3),
+            },
+            Stmt::CallRuntime {
+                dst: Some(t(3)),
+                func: RtFunc::IntToStr,
+                args: vec![t(0)],
+            },
+            Stmt::MakeClosure {
+                dst: t(3),
+                code: "closure$9.9".to_string(),
+                captures: vec![(t(0), I64), (t(3), Ptr)],
+            },
+            Stmt::MakeSum {
+                dst: t(3),
+                tag: 1,
+                fields: vec![(t(0), I64)],
+            },
+            Stmt::SumTag {
+                dst: t(0),
+                sum: t(3),
+            },
+            Stmt::SumField {
+                dst: t(0),
+                sum: t(3),
+                index: 0,
+                ty: I64,
+            },
+            Stmt::MakeRecord {
+                dst: t(3),
+                fields: vec![(t(1), F64)],
+            },
+            Stmt::RecordField {
+                dst: t(1),
+                record: t(3),
+                index: 0,
+                ty: F64,
+            },
+            Stmt::SetField {
+                record: t(3),
+                index: 0,
+                value: t(1),
+                ty: F64,
+            },
+            Stmt::MakeArray {
+                dst: t(3),
+                elems: Vec::new(),
+            },
+            Stmt::ArrayAlloc {
+                dst: t(3),
+                len: t(0),
+            },
+            Stmt::ArrayLen {
+                dst: t(0),
+                arr: t(3),
+            },
+            Stmt::ArrayGet {
+                dst: t(2),
+                arr: t(3),
+                index: t(0),
+                ty: I8,
+            },
+            Stmt::ArraySet {
+                arr: t(3),
+                index: t(0),
+                value: t(2),
+                ty: I8,
+            },
+            Stmt::Mem {
+                dst: Some(t(0)),
+                op: MemOp::Load8,
+                args: vec![t(0)],
+            },
+        ];
+        let main = Function {
+            name: "main".to_string(),
+            params: 0,
+            takes_env: false,
+            capture_count: 0,
+            temps: vec![I64, F64, I8, Ptr, Unit, I64],
+            ret: Unit,
+            is_async: false,
+            blocks: vec![
+                Block {
+                    stmts,
+                    term: Terminator::Branch {
+                        cond: t(2),
+                        then_: bb(1),
+                        else_: bb(2),
+                    },
+                },
+                Block {
+                    stmts: Vec::new(),
+                    term: Terminator::Switch {
+                        disc: t(0),
+                        arms: vec![(0, bb(2)), (-1, bb(3))],
+                        default: bb(4),
+                    },
+                },
+                Block {
+                    stmts: Vec::new(),
+                    term: Terminator::Goto(bb(5)),
+                },
+                Block {
+                    stmts: Vec::new(),
+                    term: Terminator::Return(None),
+                },
+                Block {
+                    stmts: Vec::new(),
+                    term: Terminator::Trap,
+                },
+                Block {
+                    stmts: Vec::new(),
+                    term: Terminator::Return(None),
+                },
+            ],
+        };
+        let closure = Function {
+            takes_env: true,
+            capture_count: 2,
+            is_async: true,
+            ..leaf("closure$9.9", I64)
+        };
+        Module {
+            functions: vec![main, closure, leaf("p.3", Ptr)],
+            externs: vec![
+                ExternDecl {
+                    symbol: "sqrt".to_string(),
+                    abi: ExternAbi::C,
+                    params: vec![F64],
+                    ret: F64,
+                },
+                ExternDecl {
+                    symbol: "js_flag".to_string(),
+                    abi: ExternAbi::Js,
+                    params: Vec::new(),
+                    ret: I8,
+                },
+            ],
+            runtime_alloc: None,
+            runtime_exports: Vec::new(),
+        }
+    }
+
+    const EVERY_FORM: &str = r#"(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=false functions=3 externs=2
+  (Extern "sqrt" abi=C (F64) ret=F64)
+  (Extern "js_flag" abi=Js () ret=I8)
+  (Function "main" params=0 takes_env=false captures=0 ret=Unit async=false
+    (Temp t0 I64)
+    (Temp t1 F64)
+    (Temp t2 I8)
+    (Temp t3 Ptr)
+    (Temp t4 Unit)
+    (Temp t5 I64)
+    (Block bb0
+      (ConstInt t0 -7)
+      (ConstFloat t1 3ff8000000000000)
+      (ConstBool t2 true)
+      (ConstStr t3 "a \x22b\x22\x0a")
+      (ConstUnit t4)
+      (Copy t0 t5)
+      (Bin t0 Shl Int t0 t5)
+      (Neg t1 Float t1)
+      (Not t2 t2)
+      (BitNot t0 t0)
+      (Call t3 "f.1$i" (t0 t1))
+      (Call _ "g.2" ())
+      (CallIndirect t0 t3 (I64 F64) I64 (t0 t1))
+      (Await _ t3)
+      (CallRuntime t3 IntToStr (t0))
+      (MakeClosure t3 "closure$9.9" (t0:I64 t3:Ptr))
+      (MakeSum t3 1 (t0:I64))
+      (SumTag t0 t3)
+      (SumField t0 t3 0 I64)
+      (MakeRecord t3 (t1:F64))
+      (RecordField t1 t3 0 F64)
+      (SetField t3 0 t1 F64)
+      (MakeArray t3 ())
+      (ArrayAlloc t3 t0)
+      (ArrayLen t0 t3)
+      (ArrayGet t2 t3 t0 I8)
+      (ArraySet t3 t0 t2 I8)
+      (Mem t0 Load8 (t0))
+      (Branch t2 bb1 bb2))
+    (Block bb1
+      (Switch t0 (0:bb2 -1:bb3) bb4))
+    (Block bb2
+      (Goto bb5))
+    (Block bb3
+      (Return _))
+    (Block bb4
+      (Trap))
+    (Block bb5
+      (Return _)))
+  (Function "closure$9.9" params=1 takes_env=true captures=2 ret=I64 async=true
+    (Temp t0 I64)
+    (Block bb0
+      (Return t0)))
+  (Function "p.3" params=1 takes_env=false captures=0 ret=Ptr async=false
+    (Temp t0 Ptr)
+    (Block bb0
+      (Return t0))))
+(Diagnostics)
+"#;
+
+    /// One example of every form of [`KINDS`] (spec 5.4 §3.2, ADR 0057):
+    /// every statement, terminator, class and extern ABI in a module built
+    /// by hand, the transform's flag both ways, MIR's diagnostics with and
+    /// without a place and with a note, a program that stopped before MIR
+    /// and one that does not run; each form listed, and nothing unlisted.
+    #[test]
+    fn the_dump_prints_one_example_of_every_form() {
+        let labels = [(FileId::DUMMY, "main".to_string())];
+        let module = every_form();
+        let before = Ok(module);
+        let text = dump_program(
+            &labels,
+            Reached::Lowered {
+                result: &before,
+                async_lowered: false,
+            },
+        );
+        assert_eq!(text, EVERY_FORM);
+        let after = Ok(every_form());
+        let text_after = dump_program(
+            &labels,
+            Reached::Lowered {
+                result: &after,
+                async_lowered: true,
+            },
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            text_after,
+            EVERY_FORM.replacen("async_lowered=false", "async_lowered=true", 1)
+        );
+        let errors = Err(vec![
+            Diagnostic::error("E0601", "no `main` function found"),
+            Diagnostic::error("E0013", "trait bound `(Int, Int): Eq` is not satisfied")
+                .with_primary_label(Span::new(4, 9, FileId::DUMMY), "required here")
+                .with_note("a tuple has no impls"),
+        ]);
+        let failed = dump_program(
+            &labels,
+            Reached::Lowered {
+                result: &errors,
+                async_lowered: true,
+            },
+        );
+        assert_eq!(
+            failed,
+            "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=false)\n(Diagnostics\n  \
+             (Error \"E0601\" \"no `main` function found\")\n  \
+             (Error \"E0013\" \"trait bound `(Int, Int): Eq` is not satisfied\"\n    \
+             (Primary \"main\":4..9 \"required here\")\n    \
+             (Note \"a tuple has no impls\")))\n"
+        );
+        let unchecked = dump_program(&labels, Reached::NotChecked);
+        assert_eq!(unchecked, "(Mir runs=true checked=false)\n(Diagnostics)\n");
+        let not_run = dump_program(&labels, Reached::NotRun);
+        assert_eq!(not_run, "(Mir runs=false)\n(Diagnostics)\n");
+
+        let mut found = std::collections::HashSet::new();
+        for part in [&text, &text_after, &failed, &unchecked, &not_run] {
+            for line in part.lines() {
+                found.extend(nova_resolver::dump::forms_on(line));
+            }
+        }
+        let missing: Vec<&str> = KINDS
+            .iter()
+            .copied()
+            .filter(|k| !found.contains(*k))
+            .collect();
+        assert!(missing.is_empty(), "{missing:?} printed nowhere");
+        let unlisted: Vec<&String> = found
+            .iter()
+            .filter(|f| !f.ends_with("=<n>") && !KINDS.contains(&f.as_str()))
+            .collect();
+        assert!(unlisted.is_empty(), "{unlisted:?} are in no list");
+        assert!(NOT_NATIVE.iter().all(|k| KINDS.contains(k)));
+    }
+}
diff --git a/crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs b/crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs
index aeeab321..a166fab9 100644
--- a/crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs
+++ b/crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs
@@ -15,6 +15,7 @@
 //! through the runtime. Strings and function values are opaque pointers.
````

````diff
+
+    /// std's checked functions, kept once and spliced into a program's
+    /// shared check, give the module the full check gives, by its `Debug`
+    /// text (5.4, spec 5.4 §5): over programs with no closure, one and
+    /// several with a wrapper, a method and a constant, against a std of
+    /// one more module holding a closure, a wrapper, a record, a sum, a
+    /// trait, an impl, a generic function and a constant, so that the
+    /// program's definitions and closures, `D` and `P`, both vary.
+    #[test]
+    fn shared_module_is_the_full_checks_module() {
+        let extra = (
+            "$std.zz",
+            "pub record Zr { n: Int }\npub type Zs = | Za(Int) | Zb\n\
+             pub trait Zt {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int\n}\n\
+             impl Zt for Zr {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int { self.n }\n}\n\
+             pub fn zz(x: Int) -> Int {\n    Some(x).map(|y| y + 1).unwrap_or(0)\n}\n\
+             pub fn zw(s: Zs) -> Int {\n    let h = zz\n    match s {\n        \
+             Za(n) => h(n)\n        Zb => Zr { n: 2 }.zt()\n    }\n}\n\
+             pub fn zg<T: Zt>(x: T) -> Int { x.zt() }\npub const ZC: Int = 3\n",
+        );
+        let std = std_with(Some(extra));
+        let shared_std = SharedStd::new(CompileTarget::Native, std_with(Some(extra)));
+        let alone = shared_std.alone();
+        let options = CheckOptions::default();
+        let items = SharedItems::new(&alone.file, &alone.definitions, &options);
+        assert!(items.shareable());
+        let std_lifted = items
+            .functions
+            .iter()
+            .filter(|f| f.def_id.0 >= items.defs)
+            .count();
+        assert!(std_lifted >= 2, "{std_lifted} of std's functions lifted");
+        let mut own_lifted = Vec::new();
+        for program in [
+            "fn main() {\n    println(\"${zz(1)} ${ZC}\")\n}\n",
+            "fn main() {\n    let f = |x: Int| x * 2\n    println(\"${f(zw(Zb))}\")\n}\n",
+            "record Mine { x: Int }\nimpl Mine {\n    \
+             fn m(self) -> Int {\n        let g = |y: Int| y + self.x\n        g(1)\n    }\n}\n\
+             impl Zt for Mine {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int { self.x }\n}\nconst K: Int = 4\n\
+             fn twice(f: fn(Int) -> Int, x: Int) -> Int { f(f(x)) }\n\
+             fn main() {\n    let a = |x: Int| x + K\n    let b = |x: Int| x * 2\n    \
+             let c = zz\n    let s = Za(1)\n    \
+             println(\"${twice(a, 1)} ${twice(b, 2)} ${c(3)} ${zg(Mine { x: 1 })} \
+             ${Mine { x: 2 }.m()} ${zw(s)}\")\n}\n",
+        ] {
+            let ast = parse(program);
+            let sources = [ModuleSource::new("main", &ast)];
+            let full =
+                nova_resolver::resolve_program_with_std(CompileTarget::Native, &sources, &std);
+            let all = check_with(&full.file, &full.definitions, &options);
+            assert!(
+                all.diagnostics.is_empty(),
+                "{program}: {:?}",
+                all.diagnostics
+            );
+            let shared =
+                nova_resolver::resolve_program_with_shared_std_for_check(&sources, &shared_std);
+            let checked = check_program_shared(&shared.file, &shared.definitions, &options, &items)
+                .expect("the shared path checks it");
+            own_lifted.push(checked.std_closures - checked.closures);
+            let spliced = shared_module(checked, &items);
+            assert_eq!(
+                format!("{spliced:?}"),
+                format!("{:?}", all.module),
+                "{program}"
+            );
+            // Each of std's lifted functions is named for its new id.
+            let defs = full.definitions.defs().len() as u32;
+            for f in spliced.functions.iter().filter(|f| f.def_id.0 >= defs) {
+                assert!(f.name.ends_with(&format!("${}", f.def_id.0)), "{}", f.name);
+            }
+        }
+        assert_eq!(own_lifted, [0, 1, 4]);
+    }
 }
````

````diff
+/// The module the full check builds ([`Checked::into_module`]), from a
+/// program's shared check `checked` ([`check_program_shared`]) and std's
+/// checked functions, which [`SharedItems::new`] kept, copied in with their
+/// ids moved to where the full check numbers them (5.4, spec
+/// `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §5, ADR
+/// 0057). With `S` std's definitions, `D` the program's and `P` its
+/// closures and wrappers ([`Shift`]): every id below `S` moves up by `D`,
+/// every closure's or wrapper's, `S` and up, by `D + P`, past the
+/// program's, and a lifted function's name `closure$N` or `fnval$N` takes
+/// its new id; nothing else changes. std's functions go after the
+/// program's functions, methods and constants, std's closures after the
+/// program's, as the full check orders them (`Checker::check_bodies`). The
+/// tables are the shared check's, std's parts rebased already.
+pub fn shared_module(checked: Checked, shared: &SharedItems) -> hir::Module {
+    let own_defs = checked.closures - shared.defs;
+    let shift = Shift {
+        std_defs: shared.defs,
+        defs: own_defs,
+        closures: own_defs + (checked.std_closures - checked.closures),
+    };
+    let Checked {
+        tables,
+        functions: own,
+        closures,
+        ..
+    } = checked;
+    let lifted = |fs: &[hir::Function], from: u32| {
+        fs.iter()
+            .position(|f| f.def_id.0 >= from)
+            .unwrap_or(fs.len())
+    };
+    let std = &shared.functions;
+    let std_lifted = lifted(std, shared.defs);
+    let own_lifted = lifted(&own, closures);
+    let mut own = own.into_iter();
+    let mut functions: Vec<hir::Function> = own.by_ref().take(own_lifted).collect();
+    functions.extend(std[..std_lifted].iter().map(|f| shift.function(f)));
+    functions.extend(own);
+    functions.extend(std[std_lifted..].iter().map(|f| shift.function(f)));
+    hir::Module {
+        sums: tables.sums,
+        records: tables.records,
+        traits: tables.traits,
+        impls: tables.impls,
+        functions,
+        externs: tables.externs,
+    }
+}
+
+/// How std's checked functions' ids move in a program ([`shared_module`]):
+/// a definition's, below `std_defs`, up by the program's `defs`; a closure's
+/// or wrapper's, from `std_defs` on, up by `closures`, the program's
+/// definitions and its own closures and wrappers.
+struct Shift {
+    std_defs: u32,
+    defs: u32,
+    closures: u32,
+}
+
+impl Shift {
+    fn id(&self, id: DefId) -> DefId {
+        if id.0 < self.std_defs {
+            DefId(id.0 + self.defs)
+        } else {
+            DefId(id.0 + self.closures)
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A copy of std's function `f`, its ids moved: its own and its
+    /// bounds', every type's on its locals, its return and its body, and
+    /// every id its body names. A closure's or wrapper's name holds its id
+    /// (`Checker::lift_closure`, `Checker::make_fn_wrapper`), so it is
+    /// written again; any other name is kept.
+    fn function(&self, f: &hir::Function) -> hir::Function {
+        let def_id = self.id(f.def_id);
+        let mut name = f.name.clone();
+        if f.def_id.0 >= self.std_defs {
+            for prefix in ["closure$", "fnval$"] {
+                if f.name == format!("{prefix}{}", f.def_id.0) {
+                    name = format!("{prefix}{}", def_id.0);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        let mut locals = f.locals.clone();
+        for local in &mut locals {
+            self.ty(&mut local.ty);
+        }
+        let mut ret_ty = f.ret_ty.clone();
+        self.ty(&mut ret_ty);
+        let mut body = f.body.clone();
+        self.expr(&mut body);
+        hir::Function {
+            def_id,
+            name,
+            generics: f.generics,
+            bounds: f
+                .bounds
+                .iter()
+                .map(|b| b.iter().map(|&t| self.id(t)).collect())
+                .collect(),
+            takes_env: f.takes_env,
+            capture_count: f.capture_count,
+            params: f.params,
+            locals,
+            ret_ty,
+            is_async: f.is_async,
+            body,
+            span: f.span,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn ty(&self, ty: &mut Ty) {
+        match ty {
+            Ty::Fn { params, ret } => {
+                self.tys(params);
+                self.ty(ret);
+            }
+            Ty::Future(inner) | Ty::Array(inner) => self.ty(inner),
+            Ty::Sum { def_id, args } | Ty::Record { def_id, args } => {
+                *def_id = self.id(*def_id);
+                self.tys(args);
+            }
+            Ty::Tuple(elems) => self.tys(elems),
+            Ty::Assoc { on, assoc } => {
+                self.ty(on);
+                *assoc = self.id(*assoc);
+            }
+            Ty::Int
+            | Ty::Float
+            | Ty::Bool
+            | Ty::Char
+            | Ty::String
+            | Ty::Bytes
+            | Ty::Unit
+            | Ty::Param(_)
+            | Ty::Var(_)
+            | Ty::Never
+            | Ty::Error => {}
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn tys(&self, tys: &mut [Ty]) {
+        for ty in tys {
+            self.ty(ty);
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn exprs(&self, exprs: &mut [hir::Expr]) {
+        for e in exprs {
+            self.expr(e);
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// `e`, its type and every node below it, each id moved.
+    fn expr(&self, e: &mut hir::Expr) {
+        use hir::ExprKind as K;
+        self.ty(&mut e.ty);
+        match &mut e.kind {
+            K::IntLit(_)
+            | K::FloatLit(_)
+            | K::BoolLit(_)
+            | K::StrLit(_)
+            | K::CharLit(_)
+            | K::Unit
+            | K::Local(_)
+            | K::Break
+            | K::Continue => {}
+            K::MakeClosure {
+                func,
+                type_args,
+                captures,
+            } => {
+                *func = self.id(*func);
+                self.tys(type_args);
+                self.exprs(captures);
+            }
+            K::Call {
+                func,
+                type_args,
+                args,
+            } => {
+                if let hir::Callee::Def(id) = func {
+                    *id = self.id(*id);
+                }
+                self.tys(type_args);
+                self.exprs(args);
+            }
+            K::MakeVariant { sum, args, .. } => {
+                *sum = self.id(*sum);
+                self.exprs(args);
+            }
+            K::MakeRecord { record, fields } => {
+                *record = self.id(*record);
+                self.exprs(fields);
+            }
+            K::MakeTuple { elems } | K::MakeArray { elems } | K::StrConcat(elems) => {
+                self.exprs(elems)
+            }
+            K::FieldGet { target, .. }
+            | K::ArrayLen { target }
+            | K::Unary { expr: target, .. }
+            | K::Let { init: target, .. }
+            | K::Assign { value: target, .. }
+            | K::ToStr(target)
+            | K::Await(target) => self.expr(target),
+            K::FieldSet { target, value, .. } => {
+                self.expr(target);
+                self.expr(value);
+            }
+            K::ArrayRepeat { init, len } => {
+                self.expr(init);
+                self.expr(len);
+            }
+            K::Index { target, index } => {
+                self.expr(target);
+                self.expr(index);
+            }
+            K::IndexSet {
+                target,
+                index,
+                value,
+            } => {
+                self.expr(target);
+                self.expr(index);
+                self.expr(value);
+            }
+            K::TraitCall {
+                trait_id,
+                self_ty,
+                type_args,
+                receiver,
+                args,
+                ..
+            } => {
+                *trait_id = self.id(*trait_id);
+                self.ty(self_ty);
+                self.tys(type_args);
+                if let Some(r) = receiver {
+                    self.expr(r);
+                }
+                self.exprs(args);
+            }
+            K::Binary { lhs, rhs, .. } | K::LogicalAnd { lhs, rhs } | K::LogicalOr { lhs, rhs } => {
+                self.expr(lhs);
+                self.expr(rhs);
+            }
+            K::Block { stmts, trailing } => {
+                self.exprs(stmts);
+                if let Some(t) = trailing {
+                    self.expr(t);
+                }
+            }
+            K::If { cond, then, else_ } => {
+                self.expr(cond);
+                self.expr(then);
+                if let Some(e) = else_ {
+                    self.expr(e);
+                }
+            }
+            K::While { cond, body } => {
+                self.expr(cond);
+                self.expr(body);
+            }
+            K::Match { scrutinee, arms } => {
+                self.expr(scrutinee);
+                for arm in arms {
+                    self.pattern(&mut arm.pattern);
+                    if let Some(g) = &mut arm.guard {
+                        self.expr(g);
+                    }
+                    self.expr(&mut arm.body);
+                }
+            }
+            K::Return(value) => {
+                if let Some(v) = value {
+                    self.expr(v);
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn pattern(&self, p: &mut hir::Pattern) {
+        match p {
+            hir::Pattern::Variant { sum, fields, .. } => {
+                *sum = self.id(*sum);
+                for f in fields {
+                    self.pattern(f);
+                }
+            }
+            hir::Pattern::Tuple(ps) | hir::Pattern::Or(ps) => {
+                for q in ps {
+                    self.pattern(q);
+                }
+            }
+            hir::Pattern::Wildcard
+            | hir::Pattern::Bind(_)
+            | hir::Pattern::LitInt(_)
+            | hir::Pattern::LitBool(_)
+            | hir::Pattern::LitStr(_)
+            | hir::Pattern::LitChar(_)
+            | hir::Pattern::Range { .. } => {}
+        }
+    }
+}
+
 /// Whether the program implements anything on a type it does not own
 /// (5.3b, spec 5.3 §4.5): an impl, a trait's or an inherent one, whose
 /// self type is a primitive, an array or one of std's records or sums,
diff --git a/crates/nova-typeck/src/lib.rs b/crates/nova-typeck/src/lib.rs
index c560531a..802b2540 100644
--- a/crates/nova-typeck/src/lib.rs
+++ b/crates/nova-typeck/src/lib.rs
@@ -20,7 +20,7 @@ mod usefulness;
 
 pub use check::{
     check, check_items, check_items_shared, check_program, check_program_shared, check_with,
-    implements_on_a_type_it_does_not_own, Checked, ItemTables, SharedItems,
+    implements_on_a_type_it_does_not_own, shared_module, Checked, ItemTables, SharedItems,
 };
 
 use nova_hir::Ty;
````

````diff
diff --git a/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs
index 9f43bf6c..82128b8f 100644
--- a/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs
+++ b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs
@@ -73,6 +73,16 @@
 //! given a std whose items or bodies give a diagnostic checks std for each
 //! program.
 //!
+//! 5.4 (spec
+//! `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §3, §7; ADR
+//! 0057): S0's hidden `nova check --dump-mir FILE…`, absent from `--help`,
+//! prints each root's MIR natively, after the async transform or, with
+//! `--before-async`, before it. Until N prints MIR (`N_MIR`), the native
+//! corpus's roots that run, the HIR cases that reach MIR and MIR's cases
+//! (`cases/mir/`), with both line endings, go through S0 alone, whose dumps
+//! must be well formed and hold every native form and every code MIR gives
+//! from source.
+//!
 //! `NOVA_SELFHOST_FUZZ=SEED:COUNT` runs a longer search locally, never in
 //! CI. Every input that finds a difference becomes a fixed case.
 //!
@@ -5096,3 +5106,265 @@ fn every_run_of_a_mir_dump_settles_e0004s_paths_on_windows() {
         );
     }
 }
+
+/// The codes MIR gives natively from source (5.4, spec §7.3), each with
+/// its site's words, which the cases print: E0601 with no `main` and with
+/// one that is generic or takes parameters (`mono.rs`'s two), E0013 at the
+/// bound check of monomorphization, E0075, E0078 at both of its limits and
+/// E0079. E0013's other site, an unresolvable trait call in lowering
+/// (`lower.rs`), is unreachable from source, as monomorphization's E0011
+/// is, and E0091 is the WASM target's (ADR 0057).
+const MIR_CODES: [(&str, &str); 7] = [
+    ("E0601", "no `main` function found"),
+    ("E0601", "`main` cannot be generic or take parameters"),
+    ("E0013", "is not satisfied when instantiating"),
+    ("E0075", "is declared with conflicting signatures"),
+    ("E0078", "the chain of bindings is more than"),
+    ("E0078", "it resolves to more than"),
+    ("E0079", "is still an unresolved associated type"),
+];
+
+/// MIR's cases (5.4, spec §7.1): `cases/mir/`, the sources the native tests
+/// of `nova-mir` lower, extracted, and by hand a case for each of MIR's
+/// codes reachable from source, one holding every statement a native build
+/// prints, and a package whose module that does not run is `runs=false`;
+/// laid out as the resolver's ([`case_roots`]).
+fn mir_cases_dir() -> PathBuf {
+    cases_dir().join("mir")
+}
+
+/// The native corpus's roots that run (spec 5.4 §7.1): every loose program
+/// and each package's `src/main.nova`, as `check` lowers them.
+fn mir_corpus() -> (Vec<String>, Vec<String>) {
+    let packages = package_corpus()
+        .into_iter()
+        .filter(|f| f.ends_with("/src/main.nova"))
+        .collect();
+    (loose_corpus(), packages)
+}
+
+/// The HIR cases that reach MIR (spec 5.4 §7.1): the roots of
+/// `cases/check/` whose part in the HIR test's run ([`hir_cases`]) holds no
+/// error, the loose programs' and the packages' names, and the directories
+/// of those run apart; each part is read in the order the run dumped them.
+fn mir_hir_cases() -> &'static (Vec<String>, Vec<PathBuf>) {
+    static CASES: OnceLock<(Vec<String>, Vec<PathBuf>)> = OnceLock::new();
+    CASES.get_or_init(|| {
+        let roots = case_roots(&hir_cases_dir(Mode::Check));
+        let from_cases: Vec<String> = roots.loose.into_iter().chain(roots.packages).collect();
+        let (dump, _) = hir_cases(Mode::Check);
+        let parts = parts_by_root(dump);
+        assert_eq!(parts.len(), from_cases.len() + roots.apart.len());
+        let clean: Vec<bool> = parts
+            .iter()
+            .map(|(_, part)| !part.contains("\n  (Error \""))
+            .collect();
+        let loose = from_cases
+            .iter()
+            .zip(&clean)
+            .filter(|(_, c)| **c)
+            .map(|(r, _)| r.clone())
+            .collect();
+        let apart = roots
+            .apart
+            .iter()
+            .zip(&clean[from_cases.len()..])
+            .filter(|(_, c)| **c)
+            .map(|(d, _)| d.clone())
+            .collect();
+        (loose, apart)
+    })
+}
+
+/// [`agree_on_mir_in`] `form` on the cases of `check` (in `check`, or a
+/// copy of it) that reach MIR ([`mir_hir_cases`]) and on MIR's cases (in
+/// `mir`, or a copy): each program apart from its own directory after the
+/// rest. Returns S0's output and how many programs it holds.
+fn agree_on_mir_cases(form: MirForm, check: &Path, mir: &Path) -> (String, usize) {
+    let (loose, apart) = mir_hir_cases();
+    let roots = case_roots(mir);
+    assert!(roots.apart.is_empty());
+    let mut all = agree_on_mir_in(form, check, loose, 0);
+    for dir in apart {
+        let name = dir.file_name().expect("a case's directory");
+        let copy = check.join(name);
+        all.push_str(&agree_on_mir_in(form, &copy, &["main.nova".to_string()], 0));
+    }
+    let own: Vec<String> = roots.loose.into_iter().chain(roots.packages).collect();
+    all.push_str(&agree_on_mir_in(form, mir, &own, 0));
+    (all, loose.len() + apart.len() + own.len())
+}
+
+/// [`agree_on_mir_cases`] in `form` on the cases as they are, once a run.
+fn mir_cases(form: MirForm) -> &'static (String, usize) {
+    static CASES: [OnceLock<(String, usize)>; 2] = [OnceLock::new(), OnceLock::new()];
+    CASES[form as usize]
+        .get_or_init(|| agree_on_mir_cases(form, &hir_cases_dir(Mode::Check), &mir_cases_dir()))
+}
+
+/// The MIR dump, in both forms, of the native corpus's roots that run,
+/// the HIR cases that reach MIR and MIR's cases (5.4, spec §7.1 to §7.3):
+/// compared with N's once N prints MIR ([`N_MIR`]), S0's alone before;
+/// each root's part is there, well formed; every form of `KINDS` occurs
+/// but those a native build never prints, and nothing unlisted; `Await` and
+/// `async=true` in the before form alone, which equals the after form on
+/// every root that holds no poll function; and each of MIR's codes from
+/// source at its site occurs among the cases ([`MIR_CODES`]).
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_corpus_and_the_cases() {
+    let root = root();
+    let (loose, packages) = mir_corpus();
+    assert!(loose.len() >= 240, "only {} loose programs", loose.len());
+    assert!(
+        packages.len() >= 8,
+        "only {} packages' src/main.nova",
+        packages.len()
+    );
+    let runs: Vec<(MirForm, String, String, usize)> = std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+        let runs: Vec<_> = [MirForm::After, MirForm::Before]
+            .into_iter()
+            .map(|form| {
+                let (root, loose, packages) = (&root, &loose, &packages);
+                scope.spawn(move || {
+                    let mut corpus = agree_on_mir_in(form, root, loose, 0);
+                    corpus.push_str(&agree_on_mir_in(form, root, packages, 0));
+                    let (cases, count) = mir_cases(form).clone();
+                    (form, corpus, cases, count)
+                })
+            })
+            .collect();
+        joined(runs)
+    });
+    let mut forms: [std::collections::HashSet<String>; 2] = Default::default();
+    for (form, corpus, cases, count) in &runs {
+        // Most corpus roots lower; the HIR cases that reach MIR, most of
+        // them clean, and MIR's cases.
+        assert!(*count >= 400, "{form:?}: only {count} cases");
+        let first = |dump: &str, head: &str| {
+            parts_by_root(dump)
+                .iter()
+                .filter(|(_, p)| p.starts_with(head))
+                .count()
+        };
+        let lowered = first(corpus, "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true ");
+        assert!(lowered >= 200, "{form:?}: {lowered} corpus roots lower");
+        assert!(
+            first(cases, "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true ") >= 380,
+            "{form:?}: too few cases lower"
+        );
+        let flag = format!(" async_lowered={} ", *form == MirForm::After);
+        for dump in [corpus, cases] {
+            for (r, part) in parts_by_root(dump) {
+                let line = part.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
+                if line.contains(" lowered=true ") {
+                    assert!(line.contains(&flag), "{form:?} {r}: {line}");
+                }
+            }
+            forms[*form as usize].extend(hir_forms(dump));
+        }
+        eprintln!(
+            "the MIR dump {form:?}: {} loose programs and {} packages' mains, {} lines, {} \
+             bytes; {count} cases, {} lines, {} bytes",
+            loose.len(),
+            packages.len(),
+            corpus.lines().count(),
+            corpus.len(),
+            cases.lines().count(),
+            cases.len()
+        );
+    }
+    let kinds = nova_driver::mir_dump::KINDS;
+    let not_native = nova_driver::mir_dump::NOT_NATIVE;
+    let all: std::collections::HashSet<&String> = forms[0].iter().chain(&forms[1]).collect();
+    let unlisted: Vec<&&String> = all
+        .iter()
+        .filter(|f| !f.ends_with("=<n>") && !kinds.contains(&f.as_str()))
+        .collect();
+    assert!(unlisted.is_empty(), "{unlisted:?} are in no list");
+    let missing: Vec<&str> = kinds
+        .iter()
+        .copied()
+        .filter(|k| !not_native.contains(k) && !all.iter().any(|f| f == k))
+        .collect();
+    assert!(missing.is_empty(), "no native MIR dump holds {missing:?}");
+    let native: Vec<&str> = not_native
+        .iter()
+        .copied()
+        .filter(|k| all.iter().any(|f| f == k))
+        .collect();
+    assert!(native.is_empty(), "{native:?} in a native dump");
+    for before_only in ["(Await", "(Function async=true"] {
+        assert!(!forms[0].contains(before_only), "{before_only} after");
+        assert!(forms[1].contains(before_only), "no {before_only} before");
+    }
+    // Where the after form holds no poll function the transform changed
+    // nothing (spec §7.2).
+    let (after, before) = (&runs[0], &runs[1]);
+    for (a, b) in [(&after.1, &before.1), (&after.2, &before.2)] {
+        let (a, b) = (parts_by_root(a), parts_by_root(b));
+        assert_eq!(a.len(), b.len());
+        let mut changed = 0;
+        for ((ra, pa), (rb, pb)) in a.iter().zip(&b) {
+            assert_eq!(ra, rb);
+            if pa.contains("$poll\" ") {
+                changed += 1;
+                continue;
+            }
+            let same = pa.replacen(" async_lowered=true ", " async_lowered=false ", 1);
+            assert_eq!(&same, pb, "{ra}: the forms differ with no poll function");
+        }
+        eprintln!("{changed} roots hold a poll function after the transform");
+    }
+    let codes = codes_in(&after.2);
+    assert_eq!(codes, codes_in(&before.2));
+    let absent: Vec<&(&str, &str)> = MIR_CODES
+        .iter()
+        .filter(|(code, words)| {
+            !after
+                .2
+                .lines()
+                .any(|l| l.starts_with(&format!("  (Error \"{code}\" \"")) && l.contains(words))
+        })
+        .collect();
+    assert!(absent.is_empty(), "{absent:?} occur in no case; {codes:?}");
+    eprintln!("the MIR cases' codes: {codes:?}");
+}
+
+/// MIR's cases and the HIR cases that reach MIR as a Windows checkout has
+/// them ([`crlf`]), in both forms (spec 5.4 §7.2): each part is there and
+/// the codes are those of the cases as they are, though E0601's and
+/// E0013's places are byte offsets.
+/// A copy that changes no file, as on a Windows checkout, which already
+/// has each `\r\n`, is not compared again:
+/// `the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_corpus_and_the_cases` has compared those
+/// bytes, as the resolution and HIR dumps' tests do (ADR 0054 §9).
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_cases_with_windows_line_endings() {
+    let dir = fresh_dir("crlf-mir");
+    let (check, mir) = (dir.join("check"), dir.join("mir"));
+    let (held, changed_check) = copy_crlf(&hir_cases_dir(Mode::Check), &check);
+    let (held_mir, changed) = copy_crlf(&mir_cases_dir(), &mir);
+    assert!(held > 700 && held_mir > 70, "{held} {held_mir}");
+    if changed_check + changed == 0 {
+        return;
+    }
+    assert!(changed > 70, "{changed}");
+    std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+        let forms: Vec<_> = [MirForm::After, MirForm::Before]
+            .into_iter()
+            .map(|form| {
+                let (check, mir) = (&check, &mir);
+                scope.spawn(move || {
+                    let crlf = agree_on_mir_cases(form, check, mir);
+                    (form, crlf, mir_cases(form))
+                })
+            })
+            .collect();
+        for (form, (dumps, count), (lf, lf_count)) in joined(forms) {
+            assert_eq!(count, *lf_count, "{form:?}");
+            assert_eq!(parts_by_root(&dumps).len(), count, "{form:?}");
+            assert_eq!(codes_in(&dumps), codes_in(lf), "{form:?}");
+            assert_ne!(&dumps, lf, "{form:?}: no place moved");
+        }
+    });
+}

+trait A { fn a(self) -> Int }
+trait B: A { fn b(self) -> Int }
+record R { v: Int }
+record W<T> { inner: T }
+impl A for R { fn a(self) -> Int { 1 } }
+impl B for R { fn b(self) -> Int { 2 } }
+impl<T: B> A for W<T> { fn a(self) -> Int { self.inner.a() } }
+impl<T: B> B for W<T> { fn b(self) -> Int { self.inner.b() } }
+fn sum<U: B>(x: U) -> Int { x.a() + x.b() }
+fn main() {
+let w = W { inner: R { v: 0 } }
+println("${sum(w)}")
+}
\ No newline at end of file

diff --git a/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_every_native_form.nova b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_every_native_form.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..80bfde59
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_every_native_form.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,46 @@
+extern "C" {
+    fn labs(x: Int) -> Int
+}
+
+record P {
+    x: Int
+    y: Float
+}
+
+type S =
+  | A(Int)
+  | B
+
+fn pick(s: S) -> Int {
+    match s {
+        A(n) => n
+        B => 0
+    }
+}
+
+fn flip(b: Bool) -> Bool {
+    !b
+}
+
+fn half(f: Float) -> Float {
+    -f / 2.0
+}
+
+async fn one() -> Int {
+    1
+}
+
+async fn two() -> Int {
+    one().await + 1
+}
+
+fn main() {
+    let mut p = P { x: ~1, y: half(3.0) }
+    p.x = -p.x
+    let mut a = [0; 3]
+    a[1] = a.len() + [5, 6][1]
+    let k = 2
+    let f = |n: Int| n * k
+    let g = pick
+    println("${f(a[1])} ${g(A(4))} ${flip(true)} ${p.y} ${labs(-3)} ${block_on(two())}")
+}
diff --git a/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_std_bodies_see_an_inherent_flush_on_stdout.nova b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_std_bodies_see_an_inherent_flush_on_stdout.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..0084f7f9
--- /dev/null
+++ b/crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir/mir_std_bodies_see_an_inherent_flush_on_stdout.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,15 @@
+import ui::html::{text}
+import ui::ssr::{page, serve, Page, Site}
+
+async fn mine() -> Result<(), IoError> { Ok(()) }
+
+impl Stdout {
+    fn flush(self) -> Future<Result<(), IoError>> { mine() }
+}
+
+fn render(location: String) -> Page { page(location, || text("hi")) }
+
+fn main() {
+    let site = Site { address: "127.0.0.1:0", dist: "dist", routes: [], render: render }
+    let _ = block_on(serve(site))
+}
````

### Task 4: N's MIR, lowering and monomorphization, compared before the async transform

Spec §4.3, §6, §7.2, §9; decisions 2, 7, 16, 17, 18, 19 and 25.

**Files:**
- Create: `selfhost/src/mir.nova`, `selfhost/src/lower.nova`,
  `selfhost/src/lower_call.nova`, `selfhost/src/lower_match.nova`,
  `selfhost/src/mono.nova`, `selfhost/src/splice.nova`,
  `selfhost/src/mir_dump.nova`, `selfhost/src/mir_tests.nova`
- Modify: `selfhost/src/hir.nova`, `selfhost/src/main.nova`,
  `crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost.rs`,
  `crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/compare.rs`

- [ ] **Step 1: The tests.** `patch_block t4-tests`:
  - `mir_tests.nova`, N's 4 `@test`s: `mangle_ty`'s codes, the lowering's
    runtime functions by name, a generic function instantiated twice with
    its own nodes unchanged, and a splice done twice with std's shared
    functions unchanged; imported by `main.nova`;
  - `selfhost.rs`: N's before form compared on every input, its after
    form refused (`N_MIR_AFTER = false`, `MirForm::n_prints`, decision
    17); `both_sides_refuse_the_mir_dumps_shapes_alike`;
    `n_refuses_the_after_form_until_it_ports_the_async_transform`; the
    manifest test runs N's `mir` too; `HIR_PACKAGE_PARTS` 6 and
    `HIR_TEST_PARTS` 2 (decision 18);
  - `selfhost.rs`'s
    `the_mir_mismatch_report_names_the_function_block_and_statement`,
    beside the earlier report tests (decision 25): two MIR dumps written
    by hand, two functions in one root and a second root, each rule's
    report asserted, a statement's whole.
- [ ] **Step 2: Run, and see them fail.** First the build, which ends with
  `Finished`. Then `cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost
  --no-run`, and `SH` (8 s). Expected: `21 passed; 42 failed`: N does not
  build, `error[E0001]: cannot find module` `lower_call`, `mir`, `mono`
  and `splice` (imported by `mir_tests.nova`), so every test that builds
  N fails, 41; and
  `the_mir_mismatch_report_names_the_function_block_and_statement` fails
  at its first assertion, since `compare.rs` is still the base's, whose
  report names no function (`left: "the dumps differ in a.nova, at line
  18 of its dump\n…`, where the right names `function \"g.1\", block
  bb1, statement 1`). The 21 that pass run S0 alone or no N.
- [ ] **Step 3: The code.** `patch_block t4-code`: N's native MIR (ADR
  0057 §10): `mir.nova` (the types as `Int` codes, `mir_ty`, `mangle`,
  `mangle_ty`, `RT_FUNC_VARIANTS`); `lower.nova`, `lower_call.nova` and
  `lower_match.nova` (`lower.rs` whole: functions, expressions, calls and
  the builtin table, trait calls, operators, `if`, array repeat, `match`
  by switches and chains); `mono.nova` (`lower_module_with` from `main`,
  E0601, the worklist, externs and E0075, names, E0011, bounds and E0013,
  `Specializer` building new nodes with E0078 and E0079, the shim,
  `type_name`, `keep_the_entry_main`); `splice.nova` (`full_module`,
  `shared_module`, `Shift`); `mir_dump.nova`; `hir.nova`'s `HirModule`
  with its index map, lookups, `resolve_method_full`, `has_params` and
  `is_flat`; `main.nova`'s `selfhost mir --std DIR [--before-async]
  FILE…`, which refuses the after form with exit 3 until Task 5; and
  `compare.rs`'s MIR mismatch report (decision 25): `Mismatch::place`,
  `MirWalk`'s `step` and `place`, `report` giving the place where other
  dumps give a source line.
- [ ] **Step 4: Run.** First the build, which ends with `Finished`.
  - `cargo test --locked --workspace --test selfhost --no-run`, then `SH`
    (186 s). Expected: `63 passed; 0 failed`.
  - `(cd selfhost && ../target/debug/nova test) 2>&1 | tail -n 1` (7 s).
    Expected: `test result: ok. 100 passed; 0 failed; 0 trapped; 100
    total`.
  - `cargo test --locked --workspace --no-run`, then `S` (320 s).
    Expected: `2835 1 10`, as Task 3's: no Rust changes outside the
    tests.
- [ ] **Step 5: The checks,** as the conventions list them.
- [ ] **Step 6: Commit** `selfhost: N's MIR, lowering and
      monomorphization, compared before the async transform`.

````diff
+/// A MIR dump's line has no span, so for a MIR dump the report names the
+/// root, the function and the block above the first line that differs and
+/// the statement's index there (spec 5.4 §4.3), the nearest function's of
+/// two; a function's or a block's header names the one it heads, a temp
+/// its function, the root's first line the root alone, and a line outside
+/// any function, an extern or a diagnostic, says so.
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_mismatch_report_names_the_function_block_and_statement() {
+    const MIR: Dump = Dump {
+        s0: &["check", "--dump-mir"],
+        n: &["mir"],
+    };
+    let s0 = [
+        "== a.nova",
+        "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=true functions=2 externs=1",
+        "  (Extern \"sqrt\" abi=C (F64) ret=F64)",
+        "  (Function \"main\" params=0 takes_env=false captures=0 ret=Unit async=false",
+        "    (Temp t0 Unit)",
+        "    (Block bb0",
+        "      (Call _ \"g.1\" ())",
+        "      (Goto bb1))",
+        "    (Block bb1",
+        "      (ConstUnit t0)",
+        "      (Return _)))",
+        "  (Function \"g.1\" params=0 takes_env=false captures=0 ret=I64 async=false",
+        "    (Temp t0 I64)",
+        "    (Temp t1 I64)",
+        "    (Block bb0",
+        "      (Goto bb1))",
+        "    (Block bb1",
+        "      (ConstInt t0 1)",
+        "      (ConstInt t1 2)",
+        "      (Return t1)))",
+        "(Diagnostics)",
+        "== b.nova",
+        "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=false)",
+        "(Diagnostics",
+        "  (Error \"E0601\" \"`main` function not found\"))",
+        "",
+    ]
+    .join("\n");
+    let reported = |from: &str, to: &str| {
+        let n = s0.replacen(from, to, 1);
+        assert_ne!(s0, n, "{from} is not in:\n{s0}");
+        let m = first_difference(s0.as_bytes(), n.as_bytes()).expect("a difference");
+        report(
+            &m,
+            &MIR,
+            Path::new("."),
+            Path::new("nova"),
+            Path::new("selfhost"),
+        )
+    };
+    assert_eq!(
+        reported("(ConstInt t1 2)", "(ConstInt t1 3)"),
+        "the dumps differ in a.nova, at line 18 of its dump, function \"g.1\", block bb1, \
+         statement 1\n\
+         \x20 S0 (nova check --dump-mir):       (ConstInt t1 2)\n\
+         \x20 N  (selfhost mir):                (ConstInt t1 3)\n\
+         reproduce, in .:\n\
+         \x20 nova check --dump-mir a.nova > s0.txt\n\
+         \x20 selfhost mir a.nova > n.txt\n\
+         \x20 diff s0.txt n.txt\n\
+         and add the file to crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/ once it is fixed"
+    );
+    for (from, to, at) in [
+        (
+            "(Return t1)",
+            "(Return t0)",
+            "a.nova, at line 19 of its dump, function \"g.1\", block bb1, statement 2",
+        ),
+        (
+            "captures=0 ret=I64",
+            "captures=1 ret=I64",
+            "a.nova, at line 11 of its dump, function \"g.1\", at its header",
+        ),
+        (
+            "(Block bb1\n      (ConstInt",
+            "(Block bb2\n      (ConstInt",
+            "a.nova, at line 16 of its dump, function \"g.1\", block bb1, at its header",
+        ),
+        (
+            "(Temp t1 I64)",
+            "(Temp t1 F64)",
+            "a.nova, at line 13 of its dump, function \"g.1\", at its temps",
+        ),
+        (
+            "(Temp t0 Unit)",
+            "(Temp t0 I64)",
+            "a.nova, at line 4 of its dump, function \"main\", at its temps",
+        ),
+        (
+            "functions=2",
+            "functions=3",
+            "a.nova, at line 1 of its dump, the root's first line",
+        ),
+        (
+            "(F64) ret=F64",
+            "(I64) ret=F64",
+            "a.nova, at line 2 of its dump, outside any function",
+        ),
+        (
+            "(Diagnostics)",
+            "(Diagnostics",
+            "a.nova, at line 20 of its dump, outside any function",
+        ),
+        (
+            "not found",
+            "missing",
+            "b.nova, at line 3 of its dump, outside any function",
+        ),
+    ] {
+        let text = reported(from, to);
+        assert_eq!(
+            text.lines().next(),
+            Some(format!("the dumps differ in {at}").as_str()),
+            "{text}"
+        );
+    }
+}

+// N's own tests of MIR (5.4, spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §6.3): each
+// type's code in a symbol (`mangle_ty`, mono.rs's tests' `mangle_ty`
+// cases), the lowering's runtime functions by name, a generic function
+// instantiated twice with its own nodes unchanged, and std's functions
+// spliced into a program twice with std's shared functions unchanged. The
+// differential compares everything else with S0.
+//
+// They live in `src/`, imported by `main.nova`, for the reason
+// lexer_tests.nova gives.
+
+import builtins
+import hir
+import lexer
+import lower_call
+import mir
+import mono
+import parser
+import resolver
+import splice
+import typeck
+import typeck_body
+import typeck_body_tests
+
+fn tys(list: [Ty]) -> Vec<Ty> { Vec::from_array(list) }
+
+@test
+fn mangle_ty_gives_each_type_its_code() {
+    let cases = [
+        (TyInt, "i"),
+        (TyFloat, "f"),
+        (TyBool, "b"),
+        (TyChar, "c"),
+        (TyString, "s"),
+        (TyBytes, "y"),
+        (TyUnit, "u"),
+        (TyNever, "n"),
+        (TyFn(tys([TyInt, TyString]), TyBool), "FisRbE"),
+        (TyFn(tys([]), TyUnit), "FRuE"),
+        (TySum(7, tys([])), "S7"),
+        (TySum(7, tys([TyInt, TyArray(TyChar)])), "S7LiAcEE"),
+        (TyRecord(12, tys([])), "R12"),
+        (TyRecord(12, tys([TyFuture(TyFloat)])), "R12LUfEE"),
+        (TyArray(TyArray(TyInt)), "AAiEE"),
+        (TyTuple(tys([TyInt, TyTuple(tys([TyInt, TyInt]))])), "TiTiiEE"),
+        (TyTuple(tys([TyTuple(tys([TyInt, TyInt])), TyInt])), "TTiiEiE"),
+        (TyFuture(TyUnit), "UuE"),
+        (TyParam(0), "X"),
+        (TyVar(3), "X"),
+        (TyError, "X"),
+        (TyAssoc(TyInt, 4), "X"),
+    ]
+    for (ty, code) in cases.iter() { assert_eq(mangle_ty(ty), code) }
+    assert_eq(mangle(9, "f", tys([])), "f.9")
+    assert_eq(mangle(9, "f", tys([TyInt, TyString])), "f.9" + "$" + "i_s")
+}
+
+@test
+fn the_lowerings_runtime_functions_are_named_as_s0_names_them() {
+    assert(RT_FUNC_VARIANTS.len() == 111, "${RT_FUNC_VARIANTS.len()} runtime functions")
+    let named = [
+        (RT_STR_CONCAT, "StrConcat"),
+        (RT_STR_CONCAT_N, "StrConcatN"),
+        (RT_INT_TO_STR, "IntToStr"),
+        (RT_FLOAT_TO_STR, "FloatToStr"),
+        (RT_BOOL_TO_STR, "BoolToStr"),
+        (RT_CHAR_TO_STR, "CharToStr"),
+        (RT_STR_EQ, "StrEq"),
+        (RT_CHECK_BOUNDS, "CheckBounds"),
+        (RT_PANIC, "Panic"),
+        (RT_TASK_BLOCK_ON, "TaskBlockOn"),
+    ]
+    for (index, name) in named.iter() { assert_eq(RT_FUNC_VARIANTS[index], name) }
+    assert(BUILTIN_LOWERING.len() == BUILTIN_VARIANTS.len(), "one lowering a builtin")
+    let lowerings = builtin_lowerings()
+    assert(lowerings.len() == BUILTIN_VARIANTS.len(), "one lowering a builtin")
+}
+
+// --- A generic function instantiated twice ---
+
+// `ty` as text, a parameter `P<i>`, so that a substitution written into
+// a type shows.
+fn ty_text(ty: Ty) -> String {
+    match ty {
+        TyParam(i) => "P${i}"
+        TyFn(params, ret) => "F(${tys_text(params)})${ty_text(ret)}"
+        TySum(d, args) => "S${d}(${tys_text(args)})"
+        TyRecord(d, args) => "R${d}(${tys_text(args)})"
+        TyArray(e) => "A(${ty_text(e)})"
+        TyTuple(es) => "T(${tys_text(es)})"
+        TyFuture(o) => "U(${ty_text(o)})"
+        TyAssoc(on, assoc) => "<${ty_text(on)}>::${assoc}"
+        other => mangle_ty(other)
+    }
+}
+
+fn tys_text(list: Vec<Ty>) -> String {
+    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for t in list.iter() { parts.push(ty_text(t)) }
+    ",".join(parts.to_array())
+}
+
+// Every type of `f` (its locals, its return, its body's nodes, in order),
+// its id and name, as text.
+fn function_text(f: HirFunction) -> String {
+    let mut out = StringBuilder::new()
+    out.push("${f.def_id} ${f.name} generics=${f.generics}")
+    for l in f.locals.iter() { out.push(" ${ty_text(l.ty)}") }
+    out.push(" -> ${ty_text(f.ret_ty)}:")
+    push_expr_types(out, f.body)
+    out.to_string()
+}
+
+fn push_expr_types(mut out: StringBuilder, e: HirExpr) {
+    out.push(" ${ty_text(e.ty)}")
+    for child in child_exprs(e).iter() { push_expr_types(out, child) }
+    match e.kind {
+        EkCall(_, type_args, _) => out.push(" <${tys_text(type_args)}>")
+        _ => ()
+    }
+}
+
+@test
+fn a_generic_function_instantiated_twice_keeps_its_own_nodes() {
+    let c = check_bodies_of(
+        "fn pair<T>(x: T) -> (T, T) {\n    let y = x\n    (y, x)\n}\nfn main() {\n    let a = pair(1)\n    let b = pair(\"s\")\n    let c = pair(2)\n}\n",
+    )
+    assert(c.diagnostics.is_empty(), "the source checks")
+    let program = full_module(c)
+    let generic = program.function(0).unwrap()
+    let before = function_text(generic)
+    assert(before.contains("P0"), before)
+    let lowered = match lower_module_with(program, true) {
+        Ok(m) => m
+        Err(_) => panic("the source lowers")
+    }
+    // `main`, then `pair` at `Int`, then at `String`, as the worklist pops
+    // them, last pushed first; the first call at `Int` is the last's
+    // instance.
+    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in lowered.functions.iter() { names.push(f.name) }
+    let pair = "pair.0" + "$"
+    assert_eq(" ".join(names.to_array()), "main ${pair}i ${pair}s")
+    assert_eq(MIR_TY_NAMES[lowered.functions.at(1).temps.at(0)], "I64")
+    assert_eq(MIR_TY_NAMES[lowered.functions.at(2).temps.at(0)], "Ptr")
+    // The generic function, which both instances read, is as it was.
+    assert_eq(function_text(program.function(0).unwrap()), before)
+    assert(program.function(0).unwrap().generics == 1, "still generic")
+}
+
+// --- std's functions spliced in ---
+
+// A std of one module, `$std.zz`, holding a function with a closure and
+// a generic one, shared as `share_items` shares it, and the program
+// `source` checked on the shared path against it.
+fn shared_std_and_program(source: String) -> (SharedItems, Checker) {
+    let zz = std_module(
+        "$std.zz",
+        std_label("$std.zz"),
+        "pub record Zr { n: Int }\npub fn zz(x: Int) -> Int {\n    let r = Zr { n: x }\n    let f = |y: Int| y + r.n\n    f(1)\n}\npub fn zid<T>(t: T) -> T { t }\n",
+    )
+    let std_set = StdSet {
+        modules: Vec::from_array([zz]),
+        natives: Vec::new(),
+        builtins: builtin_table(),
+    }
+    let shared = share_std(CompileTargetNative, std_set)
+    if !shared.shareable { panic("std does not resolve alone") }
+    let mut std_texts: Vec<Option<String>> = Vec::new()
+    std_texts.push(None)
+    let alone = shared.alone
+    let items = share_items(alone.items, alone.definitions, Vec::from_array([zz.label]), std_texts)
+    if !items.shareable { panic("std's items or bodies give a diagnostic") }
+    let (tokens, _) = lex(source)
+    let (file, errors) = parse(tokens)
+    if !errors.is_empty() { panic("a test source does not parse") }
+    let mut m = module_source("main", "main", file)
+    m.text = Some(source)
+    let r = resolve_program_shared_for_check(Vec::from_array([m]), shared)
+    if !r.diagnostics.is_empty() {
+        panic("a test source does not resolve: ${r.diagnostics.at(0).message}")
+    }
+    let mut texts: Vec<Option<String>> = Vec::new()
+    texts.push(Some(source))
+    texts.push(None)
+    let mut c = check_items_shared(
+        r.items,
+        r.definitions,
+        Vec::from_array(["main", zz.label]),
+        texts,
+        items,
+    )
+    c.check_bodies(r.newline_splits)
+    (items, c)
+}
+
+fn module_text(program: HirModule) -> String {
+    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in program.functions.iter() { parts.push(function_text(f)) }
+    "\n".join(parts.to_array())
+}
+
+@test
+fn a_splice_done_twice_leaves_stds_shared_functions_as_they_were() {
+    let (items, c) = shared_std_and_program(
+        "record Mine { k: Int }\nfn main() {\n    let g = |z: Int| z\n    let a = zz(g(1))\n    let b = zid(Mine { k: 1 })\n}\n",
+    )
+    assert(c.diagnostics.is_empty(), "the program checks")
+    let mut std_before: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in items.tables.functions.iter() { std_before.push(function_text(f)) }
+    let first = shared_module(c, items)
+    let second = shared_module(c, items)
+    assert_eq(module_text(first), module_text(second))
+    let mut std_after: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in items.tables.functions.iter() { std_after.push(function_text(f)) }
+    assert_eq("\n".join(std_after.to_array()), "\n".join(std_before.to_array()))
+    // std's definitions moved past the program's (`D` of them), std's
+    // closure past the program's own (`D + P`), and renamed.
+    let d = c.closures - items.defs
+    let p = c.std_closures - c.closures
+    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in first.functions.iter() { names.push("${f.def_id}:${f.name}") }
+    let std_closure = items.defs
+    let moved = std_closure + d + p
+    assert(
+        " ".join(names.to_array()).contains("${moved}:closure$" + "${moved}"),
+        " ".join(names.to_array()),
+    )
+    // Every copy is a new record: renaming one leaves std's as it was.
+    let mut copy = first.functions.at(first.functions.len() - 1)
+    copy.name = "renamed"
+    let mut std_last: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for f in items.tables.functions.iter() { std_last.push(function_text(f)) }
+    assert_eq("\n".join(std_last.to_array()), "\n".join(std_before.to_array()))
+}
````

````diff

 use std::path::Path;
@@ -38,6 +39,10 @@ pub struct Mismatch {

 }
````
