 }
 
+// `selfhost mir --std DIR [--before-async] FILE...`: std's modules are
+// read from DIR, parsed, resolved, their items collected and their bodies
+// checked once, then each program's MIR dump after its header, natively,
+// as S0's `check --dump-mir` prints it (spec 5.4 §6.3). Each option is
+// taken once, and `--` before a root that starts with `-`. With no `--std
+// DIR`, with a second `--std` or `--before-async`, `--std-part`,
+// `--target`, `--test` or another option, or with no file, nothing is
+// printed and the exit code is 2, as S0's form refuses its shapes. Until
+// N's async transform is ported, the dump after it is refused with the
+// exit code 3 and one line on stderr: only `--before-async` is printed. A
+// root whose package's manifest N's reader refuses stops the run with the
+// exit code 1, as `selfhost hir`.
+async fn mir_command(argv: Vec<String>) {
+    let usage = "usage: selfhost mir --std DIR [--before-async] FILE..."
+    let mut std_dir = ""
+    let mut before = false
+    let mut bad = false
+    let mut only_files = false
+    let mut files: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    let mut i = 2
+    while i < argv.len() {
+        let a = argv.at(i)
+        if only_files {
+            files.push(a)
+        } else if a == "--" {
+            only_files = true
+        } else if a == "--std" && i + 1 < argv.len() {
+            if std_dir != "" { bad = true }
+            std_dir = argv.at(i + 1)
+            if std_dir == "" { bad = true }
+            i += 1
+        } else if a == "--before-async" {
+            if before { bad = true }
+            before = true
+        } else if a.starts_with("-") {
+            // `--std` with no value, `--std-part`, `--target`, `--test`,
+            // `--before-async=…` and every other option.
+            bad = true
+        } else {
+            files.push(a)
+        }
+        i += 1
+    }
+    if bad || std_dir == "" || files.is_empty() {
+        eprintln(usage)
+        exit(2)
+        return
+    }
+    if !before {
+        eprintln(
+            "error: selfhost mir prints only --before-async until N's async transform is ported",
+        )
+        exit(3)
+        return
+    }
+    let std_set = match load_std(std_dir, CompileTargetNative, false).await {
+        Ok(s) => s
+        Err(message) => {
+            eprintln(message)
+            exit(2)
+            return
+        }
+    }
+    // std is resolved once, its items collected and its bodies checked
+    // once, and every root reads them (ADR 0055, ADR 0056); its checked
+    // functions are spliced into each program (spec 5.4 §5).
+    let shared = share_std(CompileTargetNative, std_set)
+    let mut items: Option<SharedItems> = None
+    let (labels, texts) = labels_and_texts(Vec::new(), std_set)
+    let alone = shared.alone
+    let s = share_items(alone.items, alone.definitions, labels, texts)
+    if shared.shareable && s.shareable { items = Some(s) }
+    let mut failed = false
+    for path in files.iter() {
+        match dump_mir(path, shared, items, before).await {
+            DumpedText(out) => print("== ${path}\n${out}")
+            DumpedIoError(e) => {
+                print("== ${path}\nIOERR ${io_kind_name(e.kind)}\n")
+                failed = true
+            }
+            DumpedRefused(why) => {
+                eprintln("error: selfhost mir cannot dump ${path}: ${why}")
+                exit(1)
+                return
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    if failed { exit(2) }
+}
+
+// The MIR dump of the program whose root is `path` (nova-driver's
+// `MirDumps::dump`): a package's module that does not run, as `check`
+// decides it (`Program::for_file_with`: a module of a package runs as its
+// `src/main.nova` alone), is `runs=false`, before its root or manifests
+// are read; otherwise `dump_hir`'s pipeline and check, shared when it can
+// be and in full otherwise, and, when neither gave an error, the full
+// check's module, std's functions spliced in, its entry `main` kept and
+// the module lowered natively, before the async transform when `before`.
+async fn dump_mir(
+    path: String,
+    shared: SharedStd,
+    items: Option<SharedItems>,
+    before: Bool,
+) -> Dumped {
+    let mut graph: Option<Graph> = None
+    let mut kind = RootLoose
+    match package_of(path).await {
+        Some((dir, k)) => {
+            let runs = match k {
+                RootSrc => module_name(path) == "main"
+                _ => false
+            }
+            if !runs { return DumpedText(dump_mir_program(ReachedNotRun)) }
+            match read_graph(dir).await {
+                Ok(g) => graph = Some(g)
+                Err(why) => return DumpedRefused(why)
+            }
+            kind = k
+        }
+        None => ()
+    }
+    let load = match load_program(graph, path, kind).await {
+        Ok(l) => l
+        Err(e) => return DumpedIoError(e)
+    }
+    let modules = load.modules
+    let mut failed = false
+    for d in load.diagnostics.iter() { if d.is_error() { failed = true } }
+    if failed { return DumpedText(dump_mir_program(ReachedNotChecked)) }
+    strip_test_functions(modules, false)
+    let mut sources: Vec<ModuleSource> = Vec::new()
+    for m in modules.iter() {
+        sources.push(
+            ModuleSource {
+                name: m.name,
+                label: m.name,
+                file: m.ast,
+                imports: m.imports,
+                text: Some(m.text),
+                package: m.package,
+                importable: m.importable,
+            },
+        )
+    }
+    if items.is_some() {
+        match mir_of(sources, shared, items, before) {
+            Some(d) => return d
+            None => ()
+        }
+    }
+    mir_of(sources, shared, None, before).unwrap()
+}
+
+// `dump_mir`'s part from the resolver on, as `hir_of`'s: with std's
+// tables `items`, the program resolved with std's definitions after its
+// own, its bodies alone checked and std's checked functions spliced in
+// (`shared_module`); without, resolved and checked in full. An error
+// before MIR is `checked=false`. `None` when the shared path cannot check
+// the program, because it implements anything on a type it does not own,
+// which the full path then checks (ADR 0056 §4).
+fn mir_of(
+    sources: Vec<ModuleSource>,
+    shared: SharedStd,
+    items: Option<SharedItems>,
+    before: Bool,
+) -> Option<Dumped> {
+    let std_set = shared.std_set
+    let resolved = match items {
+        Some(_) => resolve_program_shared_for_check(sources, shared)
+        None => resolve_program_for(CompileTargetNative, sources, std_set)
+    }
+    for d in resolved.diagnostics.iter() {
+        if d.is_error() { return Some(DumpedText(dump_mir_program(ReachedNotChecked))) }
+    }
+    let (labels, texts) = labels_and_texts(sources, std_set)
+    let mut tables = match items {
+        Some(s) => check_items_shared(resolved.items, resolved.definitions, labels, texts, s)
+        None => check_items(resolved.items, resolved.definitions, labels, texts)
+    }
+    if items.is_some() && tables.implements_on_a_type_it_does_not_own() { return None }
+    tables.check_bodies(resolved.newline_splits)
+    for d in tables.diagnostics.iter() {
+        if d.is_error() { return Some(DumpedText(dump_mir_program(ReachedNotChecked))) }
+    }
+    let program = match items {
+        Some(s) => shared_module(tables, s)
+        None => full_module(tables)
+    }
+    keep_the_entry_main(program, resolved.definitions)
+    let result = lower_module_with(program, before)
+    Some(DumpedText(dump_mir_program(ReachedLowered(result, !before))))
+}
+
 // What `dump_resolution` gives for one root: its part; the error that
 // kept its root from being read, `IOERR`; or why N refuses its package.
 type Dumped = | DumpedText(String) | DumpedIoError(IoError) | DumpedRefused(String)
diff --git a/selfhost/src/mir.nova b/selfhost/src/mir.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..860232ab
--- /dev/null
+++ b/selfhost/src/mir.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,390 @@
+// MIR's types and names: a port of crates/nova-mir/src/lib.rs's native
+// part, function by function and with the same names (spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §6, ADR 0057):
+// `MirTy`, `Temp`, `BlockId`, `Module`, `ExternDecl`, `Function`, `Block`,
+// `OperandClass`, `RtFunc`'s variant names in `RtFunc::ALL`'s order, `Stmt`
+// but its WASM-only `Mem`, `Terminator`, `mir_ty`, `mangle` and
+// `mangle_ty`. `RtFunc::symbol` and `signature` come with the emitter in
+// 5.5 (decision 7); the WASM code, `on_wasm` and `MemOp`, is S0's alone
+// (decision 4).
+//
+// The Rust's idioms, beyond hir.nova's (ADR 0057):
+//
+// - A class (`MirTy`), an operand class (`OperandClass`) and a runtime
+//   function (`RtFunc`) are `Int` codes, where S0 compares them with `==`:
+//   a `MirTy` one of `MIR_I64` to `MIR_UNIT`, an `OperandClass` one of
+//   `OC_INT` to `OC_BOOL`, an `RtFunc` its index in `RT_FUNC_VARIANTS`.
+// - A `Temp` and a `BlockId` are `Int`s, an `Option<Temp>` an `Int`, -1
+//   for none.
+// - The names ast.nova or hir.nova hold too carry `Mir` (`MirModule`,
+//   `MirFunction`, `MirBlock`, `MirStmt`, `MirTerm`); a statement's
+//   variant is `S` and its Rust name (`SCall`), a terminator's `Term` and
+//   its Rust name (`TermGoto`).
+// - `module` and `record` are keywords in Nova: S0's `module` is `program`
+//   wherever it names a `hir::Module` or MIR's, and `Module::record` is
+//   `record_type`.
+
+import hir
+
+/// `MirTy::I64`: 64-bit integer (Int, Char).
+pub const MIR_I64: Int = 0
+
+/// `MirTy::F64`: 64-bit float.
+pub const MIR_F64: Int = 1
+
+/// `MirTy::I8`: boolean (byte-sized).
+pub const MIR_I8: Int = 2
+
+/// `MirTy::Ptr`: opaque pointer (String, Bytes, sum values, function
+/// values).
+pub const MIR_PTR: Int = 3
+
+/// `MirTy::Unit`: no runtime value (unit / diverging results).
+pub const MIR_UNIT: Int = 4
+
+/// Each class's variant name, by its code.
+pub const MIR_TY_NAMES: [String] = ["I64", "F64", "I8", "Ptr", "Unit"]
+
+/// `OperandClass::Int`.
+pub const OC_INT: Int = 0
+
+/// `OperandClass::Float`.
+pub const OC_FLOAT: Int = 1
+
+/// `OperandClass::Bool`.
+pub const OC_BOOL: Int = 2
+
+/// Each operand class's variant name, by its code.
+pub const OPERAND_CLASS_NAMES: [String] = ["Int", "Float", "Bool"]
+
+/// The largest array length `ArrayAlloc` takes (`MAX_ARRAY_LEN`):
+/// `(i64::MAX - 8) / 8`.
+pub const MAX_ARRAY_LEN: Int = 1152921504606846974
+
+/// The future value's slot holding its state object
+/// (`async_lower::FUTURE_SLOT_STATE`).
+pub const FUTURE_SLOT_STATE: Int = 1
+
+/// The state object's slot holding the finished output
+/// (`async_lower::STATE_SLOT_OUTPUT`).
+pub const STATE_SLOT_OUTPUT: Int = 1
+
+/// `RtFunc`'s variants, in `RtFunc::ALL`'s order: a runtime function is
+/// its index here.
+pub const RT_FUNC_VARIANTS: [String] = [
+    "Println",
+    "Print",
+    "EPrint",
+    "EPrintln",
+    "StrConcat",
+    "StrConcatN",
+    "IntToStr",
+    "FloatToStr",
+    "FloatFixed",
+    "StrToFloat",
+    "StrIsFloat",
+    "FloatToBits",
+    "BoolToStr",
+    "CharToStr",
+    "StrEq",
+    "StrCmp",
+    "StrHash",
+    "StrLenChars",
+    "StrChars",
+    "StrFromChars",
+    "StrJoin",
+    "StrIndexOf",
+    "JsonQuote",
+    "StrToUpper",
+    "StrToLower",
+    "Alloc",
+    "CheckBounds",
+    "Panic",
+    "TestSelector",
+    "TaskSpawn",
+    "TaskBlockOn",
+    "TaskIsDone",
+    "TaskTakeOutput",
+    "TaskRelease",
+    "TaskYieldFuture",
+    "TaskSleepFutureNanos",
+    "TaskJoinFuture",
+    "TaskTimeoutFuture",
+    "FsReadToString",
+    "FsWriteString",
+    "FsTakeString",
+    "FsLastErrorMessage",
+    "FsTempDir",
+    "FsExists",
+    "FsCreateDir",
+    "FsCreateDirAll",
+    "FsRemoveFile",
+    "FsRemoveDirAll",
+    "FsRename",
+    "FsReadDir",
+    "FsTakeStringArray",
+    "FsKind",
+    "FsRead",
+    "FsTakeBytes",
+    "FsWrite",
+    "FileOpen",
+    "FileClose",
+    "FileRead",
+    "FileWrite",
+    "FileFlush",
+    "IoStdinRead",
+    "IoStdoutWrite",
+    "IoStderrWrite",
+    "IoStdoutFlush",
+    "IoStderrFlush",
+    "NetConnect",
+    "NetClose",
+    "NetRead",
+    "NetWrite",
+    "NetReadTimeout",
+    "NetListen",
+    "NetLocalPort",
+    "NetAccept",
+    "BytesLen",
+    "BytesFromString",
+    "BytesIsUtf8",
+    "BytesToStringUnchecked",
+    "BytesAt",
+    "BytesSlice",
+    "BytesConcat",
+    "BytesToInts",
+    "BytesFromInts",
+    "BytesEq",
+    "IntHashSeed",
+    "HttpParseRequest",
+    "CryptoHash",
+    "CryptoRandomBytes",
+    "CryptoRandomInt",
+    "CryptoPbkdf2Sha256",
+    "CryptoPbkdf2Sha256Verify",
+    "TimeNowNanos",
+    "TimeNowEpochNanos",
+    "LogConfigLevel",
+    "LogConfigToStderr",
+    "LogSetConfig",
+    "ShutdownRequested",
+    "ProcessArgCount",
+    "ProcessArg",
+    "ProcessExit",
+    "ProcessRun",
+    "ProcessTakeStatus",
+    "ProcessTakeStdout",
+    "ProcessTakeStderr",
+    "ProcessEnv",
+    "ProcessCurrentExe",
+    "UiStatePresent",
+    "UiStateLoad",
+    "UiStateStore",
+    "RouterStatePresent",
+    "RouterStateLoad",
+    "RouterStateStore",
+]
+
+/// `RtFunc::StrConcat`, by its index (the lowering's own runtime
+/// functions, beside the builtins' table; `mir_tests.nova` holds each to
+/// its name).
+pub const RT_STR_CONCAT: Int = 4
+
+/// `RtFunc::StrConcatN`.
+pub const RT_STR_CONCAT_N: Int = 5
+
+/// `RtFunc::IntToStr`.
+pub const RT_INT_TO_STR: Int = 6
+
+/// `RtFunc::FloatToStr`.
+pub const RT_FLOAT_TO_STR: Int = 7
+
+/// `RtFunc::BoolToStr`.
+pub const RT_BOOL_TO_STR: Int = 12
+
+/// `RtFunc::CharToStr`.
+pub const RT_CHAR_TO_STR: Int = 13
+
+/// `RtFunc::StrEq`.
+pub const RT_STR_EQ: Int = 14
+
+/// `RtFunc::CheckBounds`.
+pub const RT_CHECK_BOUNDS: Int = 26
+
+/// `RtFunc::Panic`.
+pub const RT_PANIC: Int = 27
+
+/// `RtFunc::TaskBlockOn`.
+pub const RT_TASK_BLOCK_ON: Int = 30
+
+/// The runtime function named `name`, its index in `RT_FUNC_VARIANTS`
+/// (`RtFunc::Name`); a name that is none of them is a bug in N.
+pub fn rt_func(name: String) -> Int {
+    let variants = RT_FUNC_VARIANTS
+    let mut i = 0
+    while i < variants.len() {
+        if variants[i] == name { return i }
+        i += 1
+    }
+    panic("no runtime function is named `${name}`")
+}
+
+/// A monomorphized module ready for codegen (`Module`): natively
+/// `runtime_alloc` is `None` and `runtime_exports` empty, so neither is
+/// held.
+pub record MirModule { pub functions: Vec<MirFunction>, pub externs: Vec<ExternDecl> }
+
+/// An imported external function (`ExternDecl`): its raw symbol, ABI and
+/// classes.
+pub record ExternDecl { pub symbol: String, pub abi: ExternAbi, pub params: Vec<Int>, pub ret: Int }
+
+/// A monomorphized function (`Function`).
+pub record MirFunction {
+    pub name: String,
+    pub params: Int,
+    pub takes_env: Bool,
+    pub capture_count: Int,
+    pub temps: Vec<Int>,
+    pub ret: Int,
+    pub is_async: Bool,
+    pub blocks: Vec<MirBlock>,
+}
+
+/// A basic block: straight-line statements plus one terminator (`Block`).
+pub record MirBlock { pub stmts: Vec<MirStmt>, pub term: MirTerm }
+
+/// A single 3-address statement (`Stmt`), but `Mem`, a WASM build's alone;
+/// the comment after a variant names its fields.
+pub type MirStmt =
+    // dst, value
+    | SConstInt(Int, Int)
+    | SConstFloat(Int, Float)
+    | SConstBool(Int, Bool)
+    | SConstStr(Int, String)
+    | SConstUnit(Int)
+    // dst, src
+    | SCopy(Int, Int)
+    // dst, op, class, lhs, rhs
+    | SBin(Int, HirBinOp, Int, Int, Int)
+    // dst, class, src
+    | SNeg(Int, Int, Int)
+    // dst, src
+    | SNot(Int, Int)
+    // dst, src
+    | SBitNot(Int, Int)
+    // dst, callee, args
+    | SCall(Int, String, Vec<Int>)
+    // dst, callee, params, ret, args
+    | SCallIndirect(Int, Int, Vec<Int>, Int, Vec<Int>)
+    // dst, future
+    | SAwait(Int, Int)
+    // dst, func, args
+    | SCallRuntime(Int, Int, Vec<Int>)
+    // dst, code, captures
+    | SMakeClosure(Int, String, Vec<(Int, Int)>)
+    // dst, tag, fields
+    | SMakeSum(Int, Int, Vec<(Int, Int)>)
+    // dst, sum
+    | SSumTag(Int, Int)
+    // dst, sum, index, ty
+    | SSumField(Int, Int, Int, Int)
+    // dst, fields
+    | SMakeRecord(Int, Vec<(Int, Int)>)
+    // dst, record, index, ty
+    | SRecordField(Int, Int, Int, Int)
+    // record, index, value, ty
+    | SSetField(Int, Int, Int, Int)
+    // dst, elems
+    | SMakeArray(Int, Vec<(Int, Int)>)
+    // dst, len
+    | SArrayAlloc(Int, Int)
+    // dst, arr
+    | SArrayLen(Int, Int)
+    // dst, arr, index, ty
+    | SArrayGet(Int, Int, Int, Int)
+    // arr, index, value, ty
+    | SArraySet(Int, Int, Int, Int)
+
+/// Block terminators (`Terminator`).
+pub type MirTerm =
+    | TermGoto(Int)
+    // cond, then_, else_
+    | TermBranch(Int, Int, Int)
+    // disc, arms, default
+    | TermSwitch(Int, Vec<(Int, Int)>, Int)
+    // the temp, -1 for none
+    | TermReturn(Int)
+    | TermTrap
+
+/// Map a concrete HIR type to its machine class (`mir_ty`). A type that
+/// should not occur after type checking maps to `MIR_UNIT`, as S0's
+/// defensive arm does.
+pub fn mir_ty(ty: Ty) -> Int {
+    match ty {
+        TyInt => MIR_I64
+        TyChar => MIR_I64
+        TyFloat => MIR_F64
+        TyBool => MIR_I8
+        TyString => MIR_PTR
+        TyBytes => MIR_PTR
+        TyFn(_, _) => MIR_PTR
+        TySum(_, _) => MIR_PTR
+        TyRecord(_, _) => MIR_PTR
+        TyArray(_) => MIR_PTR
+        TyTuple(_) => MIR_PTR
+        TyFuture(_) => MIR_PTR
+        TyUnit => MIR_UNIT
+        TyNever => MIR_UNIT
+        TyParam(_) => MIR_UNIT
+        TyVar(_) => MIR_UNIT
+        TyError => MIR_UNIT
+        TyAssoc(_, _) => MIR_UNIT
+    }
+}
+
+/// Mangle a function instance into a unique symbol name (`mangle`):
+/// `name.<def>`, or `name.<def>$<args>` for an instance of a generic
+/// function.
+pub fn mangle(def_id: Int, name: String, type_args: Vec<Ty>) -> String {
+    if type_args.is_empty() { return "${name}.${def_id}" }
+    let mut args: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for t in type_args.iter() { args.push(mangle_ty(t)) }
+    let joined = "_".join(args.to_array())
+    "${name}.${def_id}" + "$" + joined
+}
+
+/// A type's part of a symbol (`mangle_ty`): each a prefix-free code, `X`
+/// for a type that should not occur after monomorphization.
+pub fn mangle_ty(ty: Ty) -> String {
+    match ty {
+        TyInt => "i"
+        TyFloat => "f"
+        TyBool => "b"
+        TyChar => "c"
+        TyString => "s"
+        TyBytes => "y"
+        TyUnit => "u"
+        TyNever => "n"
+        TyFn(params, ret) => "F${mangle_tys(params, "")}R${mangle_ty(ret)}E"
+        TySum(d, args) => if args.is_empty() { "S${d}" } else { "S${d}L${mangle_tys(args, "")}E" }
+        TyRecord(d, args) => if args.is_empty() {
+            "R${d}"
+        } else {
+            "R${d}L${mangle_tys(args, "")}E"
+        }
+        TyArray(e) => "A${mangle_ty(e)}E"
+        TyTuple(es) => "T${mangle_tys(es, "")}E"
+        TyFuture(o) => "U${mangle_ty(o)}E"
+        TyParam(_) => "X"
+        TyVar(_) => "X"
+        TyError => "X"
+        TyAssoc(_, _) => "X"
+    }
+}
+
+// Each of `tys` mangled, joined by `sep`.
+fn mangle_tys(tys: Vec<Ty>, sep: String) -> String {
+    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for t in tys.iter() { parts.push(mangle_ty(t)) }
+    sep.join(parts.to_array())
+}
diff --git a/selfhost/src/mir_dump.nova b/selfhost/src/mir_dump.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..80cc3f02
--- /dev/null
+++ b/selfhost/src/mir_dump.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,415 @@
+// The MIR dump: what monomorphization and lowering built natively for one
+// program, as text, one entry per line, exactly as S0's `nova check
+// --dump-mir` prints it: a port of crates/nova-mir/src/dump.rs, function
+// by function (spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §4). ADR 0057
+// writes the grammar out, with an example of every form.
+//
+// - The first line says how far the root got: `(Mir runs=false)`,
+//   `checked=false`, `lowered=false` with MIR's diagnostics, or
+//   `lowered=true`, `async_lowered=` whether the transform ran, the
+//   counts, the externs and every function.
+// - Order is MIR's own, nothing sorted.
+// - A statement is one line: its variant's name, then every field in
+//   declaration order: `_` for an absent destination, `t<n>`, `bb<n>`,
+//   integers in decimal, a float as the 16 hex digits of its bits, strings
+//   and symbols quoted, operators, classes and runtime functions by name,
+//   lists in parentheses (`t3:Ptr`, `0:bb2`).
+// - One printer for both forms: the before form is the module before the
+//   async transform.
+
+import diagnostic
+import dump
+import hir
+import mir
+import resolution_dump
+
+/// How far a root got, which its part's first line says (`Reached`).
+pub type Reached =
+    // A package module that `check` does not lower.
+    | ReachedNotRun
+    // An error before MIR, which the HIR dump prints.
+    | ReachedNotChecked
+    // MIR's result, after the async transform when the flag says so.
+    | ReachedLowered(Result<MirModule, Vec<Diagnostic>>, Bool)
+
+/// A program's part (`dump_program`): its first line and, once lowered,
+/// the module; then `(Diagnostics)`, MIR's alone.
+pub fn dump_mir_program(reached: Reached) -> String {
+    let mut d = MirDump { out: StringBuilder::new(), fresh: true }
+    d.open(0, "Mir")
+    let mut diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic> = Vec::new()
+    match reached {
+        ReachedNotRun => d.bool_field("runs", false)
+        ReachedNotChecked => {
+            d.bool_field("runs", true)
+            d.bool_field("checked", false)
+        }
+        ReachedLowered(result, async_lowered) => {
+            d.bool_field("runs", true)
+            d.bool_field("checked", true)
+            match result {
+                Ok(program) => {
+                    d.bool_field("lowered", true)
+                    d.bool_field("async_lowered", async_lowered)
+                    d.module_text(program)
+                }
+                Err(errors) => {
+                    d.bool_field("lowered", false)
+                    diagnostics = errors
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    d.close()
+    d.out.push("\n")
+    d.out.push(diagnostics_text(diagnostics))
+    d.out.to_string()
+}
+
+// The text being written, and whether it is empty or ends a line
+// (`Dump`).
+record MirDump { out: StringBuilder, fresh: Bool }
+
+impl MirDump {
+    // A line, up to its kind (`open`).
+    fn open(mut self, depth: Int, kind: String) {
+        if !self.fresh { self.out.push("\n") }
+        self.fresh = false
+        for _ in 0..depth { self.out.push("  ") }
+        self.out.push("(")
+        self.out.push(kind)
+    }
+
+    fn close(mut self) { self.out.push(")") }
+
+    // ` key=value`.
+    fn field(mut self, key: String, value: String) { self.out.push(" ${key}=${value}") }
+
+    fn bool_field(mut self, key: String, value: Bool) { self.out.push(" ${key}=${value}") }
+
+    // ` ` and a value as it is.
+    fn value(mut self, text: String) {
+        self.out.push(" ")
+        self.out.push(text)
+    }
+
+    // ` ` and `text` quoted.
+    fn string(mut self, text: String) {
+        self.out.push(" \"")
+        push_escaped(self.out, text, true)
+        self.out.push("\"")
+    }
+
+    // ` t<n>`.
+    fn temp(mut self, t: Int) { self.out.push(" t${t}") }
+
+    // ` bb<n>`.
+    fn block(mut self, b: Int) { self.out.push(" bb${b}") }
+
+    // ` ` and an optional temp, the temp or `_`.
+    fn dest(mut self, t: Int) { if t < 0 { self.out.push(" _") } else { self.out.push(" t${t}") } }
+
+    // ` ` and a class by its variant's name.
+    fn class(mut self, c: Int) {
+        self.out.push(" ")
+        self.out.push(MIR_TY_NAMES[c])
+    }
+
+    // ` (t0 t1)`: temps, a list.
+    fn temps(mut self, ts: Vec<Int>) {
+        self.out.push(" (")
+        for i in 0..ts.len() {
+            if i > 0 { self.out.push(" ") }
+            self.out.push("t${ts.at(i)}")
+        }
+        self.out.push(")")
+    }
+
+    // ` (I64 F64)`: classes, a list.
+    fn classes(mut self, cs: Vec<Int>) {
+        self.out.push(" (")
+        for i in 0..cs.len() {
+            if i > 0 { self.out.push(" ") }
+            self.out.push(MIR_TY_NAMES[cs.at(i)])
+        }
+        self.out.push(")")
+    }
+
+    // ` (t0:I64 t3:Ptr)`: temps with their classes, a list.
+    fn typed(mut self, fields: Vec<(Int, Int)>) {
+        self.out.push(" (")
+        let mut first = true
+        for i in 0..fields.len() {
+            let (t, c) = fields.at(i)
+            if !first { self.out.push(" ") }
+            first = false
+            self.out.push("t${t}:")
+            self.out.push(MIR_TY_NAMES[c])
+        }
+        self.out.push(")")
+    }
+
+    // The counts, the externs and every function, after the first line's
+    // fields (`module`).
+    fn module_text(mut self, program: MirModule) {
+        self.field("functions", "${program.functions.len()}")
+        self.field("externs", "${program.externs.len()}")
+        for e in program.externs.iter() {
+            self.open(1, "Extern")
+            self.string(e.symbol)
+            let abi = match e.abi {
+                ExternAbiC => "C"
+                ExternAbiJs => "Js"
+            }
+            self.field("abi", abi)
+            self.classes(e.params)
+            self.field("ret", MIR_TY_NAMES[e.ret])
+            self.close()
+        }
+        for i in 0..program.functions.len() { self.function(program.functions.at(i)) }
+    }
+
+    // A function: its line, its temps, one a line, and its blocks
+    // (`function`).
+    fn function(mut self, f: MirFunction) {
+        self.open(1, "Function")
+        self.string(f.name)
+        self.field("params", "${f.params}")
+        self.bool_field("takes_env", f.takes_env)
+        self.field("captures", "${f.capture_count}")
+        self.field("ret", MIR_TY_NAMES[f.ret])
+        self.bool_field("async", f.is_async)
+        for i in 0..f.temps.len() {
+            self.open(2, "Temp")
+            self.temp(i)
+            self.class(f.temps.at(i))
+            self.close()
+        }
+        for i in 0..f.blocks.len() {
+            let b = f.blocks.at(i)
+            self.open(2, "Block")
+            self.block(i)
+            for k in 0..b.stmts.len() { self.stmt(b.stmts.at(k)) }
+            self.terminator(b.term)
+            self.close()
+        }
+        self.close()
+    }
+
+    // A statement, one line (`stmt`).
+    fn stmt(mut self, stmt: MirStmt) {
+        match stmt {
+            SConstInt(dst, v) => {
+                self.open(3, "ConstInt")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value("${v}")
+            }
+            SConstFloat(dst, v) => {
+                self.open(3, "ConstFloat")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value(hex16(v.to_bits()))
+            }
+            SConstBool(dst, v) => {
+                self.open(3, "ConstBool")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value("${v}")
+            }
+            SConstStr(dst, s) => {
+                self.open(3, "ConstStr")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.string(s)
+            }
+            SConstUnit(dst) => {
+                self.open(3, "ConstUnit")
+                self.temp(dst)
+            }
+            SCopy(dst, src) => {
+                self.open(3, "Copy")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(src)
+            }
+            SBin(dst, op, c, lhs, rhs) => {
+                self.open(3, "Bin")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value(bin_op_name(op))
+                self.value(OPERAND_CLASS_NAMES[c])
+                self.temp(lhs)
+                self.temp(rhs)
+            }
+            SNeg(dst, c, src) => {
+                self.open(3, "Neg")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value(OPERAND_CLASS_NAMES[c])
+                self.temp(src)
+            }
+            SNot(dst, src) => {
+                self.open(3, "Not")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(src)
+            }
+            SBitNot(dst, src) => {
+                self.open(3, "BitNot")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(src)
+            }
+            SCall(dst, callee, args) => {
+                self.open(3, "Call")
+                self.dest(dst)
+                self.string(callee)
+                self.temps(args)
+            }
+            SCallIndirect(dst, callee, params, ret, args) => {
+                self.open(3, "CallIndirect")
+                self.dest(dst)
+                self.temp(callee)
+                self.classes(params)
+                self.class(ret)
+                self.temps(args)
+            }
+            SAwait(dst, future) => {
+                self.open(3, "Await")
+                self.dest(dst)
+                self.temp(future)
+            }
+            SCallRuntime(dst, func, args) => {
+                self.open(3, "CallRuntime")
+                self.dest(dst)
+                self.value(RT_FUNC_VARIANTS[func])
+                self.temps(args)
+            }
+            SMakeClosure(dst, code, captures) => {
+                self.open(3, "MakeClosure")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.string(code)
+                self.typed(captures)
+            }
+            SMakeSum(dst, tag, fields) => {
+                self.open(3, "MakeSum")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.value("${tag}")
+                self.typed(fields)
+            }
+            SSumTag(dst, sum) => {
+                self.open(3, "SumTag")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(sum)
+            }
+            SSumField(dst, sum, index, ty) => {
+                self.open(3, "SumField")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(sum)
+                self.value("${index}")
+                self.class(ty)
+            }
+            SMakeRecord(dst, fields) => {
+                self.open(3, "MakeRecord")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.typed(fields)
+            }
+            SRecordField(dst, rec, index, ty) => {
+                self.open(3, "RecordField")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(rec)
+                self.value("${index}")
+                self.class(ty)
+            }
+            SSetField(rec, index, value, ty) => {
+                self.open(3, "SetField")
+                self.temp(rec)
+                self.value("${index}")
+                self.temp(value)
+                self.class(ty)
+            }
+            SMakeArray(dst, elems) => {
+                self.open(3, "MakeArray")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.typed(elems)
+            }
+            SArrayAlloc(dst, len) => {
+                self.open(3, "ArrayAlloc")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(len)
+            }
+            SArrayLen(dst, arr) => {
+                self.open(3, "ArrayLen")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(arr)
+            }
+            SArrayGet(dst, arr, index, ty) => {
+                self.open(3, "ArrayGet")
+                self.temp(dst)
+                self.temp(arr)
+                self.temp(index)
+                self.class(ty)
+            }
+            SArraySet(arr, index, value, ty) => {
+                self.open(3, "ArraySet")
+                self.temp(arr)
+                self.temp(index)
+                self.temp(value)
+                self.class(ty)
+            }
+        }
+        self.close()
+    }
+
+    // A block's terminator, one line at its statements' depth
+    // (`terminator`).
+    fn terminator(mut self, term: MirTerm) {
+        match term {
+            TermGoto(to) => {
+                self.open(3, "Goto")
+                self.block(to)
+            }
+            TermBranch(cond, then_, else_) => {
+                self.open(3, "Branch")
+                self.temp(cond)
+                self.block(then_)
+                self.block(else_)
+            }
+            TermSwitch(disc, arms, default) => {
+                self.open(3, "Switch")
+                self.temp(disc)
+                self.out.push(" (")
+                let mut first = true
+                for i in 0..arms.len() {
+                    let (v, b) = arms.at(i)
+                    if !first { self.out.push(" ") }
+                    first = false
+                    self.out.push("${v}:bb${b}")
+                }
+                self.out.push(")")
+                self.block(default)
+            }
+            TermReturn(value) => {
+                self.open(3, "Return")
+                self.dest(value)
+            }
+            TermTrap => self.open(3, "Trap")
+        }
+        self.close()
+    }
+}
+
+/// A binary operator by its variant's name, as Rust's `Debug` prints it.
+pub fn bin_op_name(op: HirBinOp) -> String {
+    match op {
+        OpAdd => "Add"
+        OpSub => "Sub"
+        OpMul => "Mul"
+        OpDiv => "Div"
+        OpRem => "Rem"
+        OpEq => "Eq"
+        OpNe => "Ne"
+        OpLt => "Lt"
+        OpLe => "Le"
+        OpGt => "Gt"
+        OpGe => "Ge"
+        OpBitAnd => "BitAnd"
+        OpBitOr => "BitOr"
+        OpBitXor => "BitXor"
+        OpShl => "Shl"
+        OpShr => "Shr"
+    }
+}
diff --git a/selfhost/src/mono.nova b/selfhost/src/mono.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..2b0bd244
--- /dev/null
+++ b/selfhost/src/mono.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,586 @@
+// Monomorphization: a port of crates/nova-mir/src/mono.rs's native path,
+// function by function and with the same names (spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §6.1, ADR
+// 0057): `lower_module_with` from `main` (E0601), the worklist, externs
+// (E0075), names, E0011, bounds (E0013), `Specializer` (E0078, E0079),
+// the async `main`'s shim, `impl_satisfies` and `type_name`; and the
+// driver's `keep_the_entry_main` (crates/nova-driver/src/lib.rs). The
+// WASM build's roots and routing (`RuntimeImpls`, `route_runtime`,
+// `unavailable_on_wasm`, E0091) are S0's alone (decision 4).
+//
+// - The worklist is a `Vec` used as a stack; `done` a `Map<String, Bool>`
+//   read by lookup only, so no map's order reaches the output.
+// - Records alias in N, so `Specializer` builds every node of an instance
+//   afresh, and never writes into the generic function, which every
+//   instance reads (spec §6.2).
+// - `Options` is its native field alone, `stop_before_async`.
+
+import builtins
+import diagnostic
+import hir
+import lower
+import lower_call
+import mir
+import resolver
+import token
+
+/// Lower a typed module to monomorphized MIR natively
+/// (`lower_module_with`): instances are found from `main` outward. With
+/// `stop_before_async`, the module as lowering left it, the async `main`'s
+/// shim pushed, before the async transform.
+pub fn lower_module_with(
+    program: HirModule,
+    stop_before_async: Bool,
+) -> Result<MirModule, Vec<Diagnostic>> {
+    let mut diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic> = Vec::new()
+    let mut found: Option<HirFunction> = None
+    for f in program.functions.iter() {
+        if f.name == "main" {
+            found = Some(f)
+            break
+        }
+    }
+    let main = match found {
+        Some(f) => f
+        None => {
+            diagnostics.push(
+                Diagnostic::error(
+                    "E0601",
+                    "no `main` function found; `nova run` needs a `fn main()` entry point",
+                ),
+            )
+            return Err(diagnostics)
+        }
+    }
+    if main.generics != 0 || main.params != 0 {
+        diagnostics.push(
+            error_at(
+                "E0601",
+                "`main` cannot be generic or take parameters",
+                main.span.file,
+                main.span.span,
+                "invalid `main` signature",
+            ),
+        )
+        return Err(diagnostics)
+    }
+    let entry = main.def_id
+    let mut functions: Vec<MirFunction> = Vec::new()
+    let mut externs: Vec<ExternDecl> = Vec::new()
+    let mut done: Map<String, Bool> = Map::new()
+    let mut worklist: Vec<(Int, Vec<Ty>)> = Vec::new()
+    worklist.push((entry, Vec::new()))
+    // The symbol an `async fn main` was lowered under, once it has been:
+    // the future-building wrapper the shim calls.
+    let mut async_entry: Option<String> = None
+    let builtins = builtin_lowerings()
+    while !worklist.is_empty() {
+        let (def_id, type_args) = worklist.pop().unwrap()
+        let func = match program.function(def_id) {
+            Some(f) => f
+            None => {
+                // Not a Nova function: an `extern` declaration is recorded,
+                // deduplicated by symbol through `done`; two that share a
+                // symbol but disagree on the signature are E0075.
+                match program.extern_fn(def_id) {
+                    Some(ext) => {
+                        let mut params: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+                        for p in ext.params.iter() { params.push(mir_ty(p)) }
+                        let ret = mir_ty(ext.ret)
+                        if !done.contains_key(ext.symbol) {
+                            let _ = done.insert(ext.symbol, true)
+                            externs.push(
+                                ExternDecl {
+                                    symbol: ext.symbol,
+                                    abi: ext.abi,
+                                    params: params,
+                                    ret: ret,
+                                },
+                            )
+                        } else {
+                            match first_extern(externs, ext.symbol) {
+                                Some(prev) => {
+                                    if !ints_eq(prev.params, params) || prev.ret != ret {
+                                        diagnostics.push(
+                                            error_at(
+                                                "E0075",
+                                                "extern symbol `${ext.symbol}` is declared with conflicting signatures",
+                                                ext.span.file,
+                                                ext.span.span,
+                                                "conflicting declaration",
+                                            ),
+                                        )
+                                    }
+                                }
+                                None => ()
+                            }
+                        }
+                    }
+                    None => ()
+                }
+                continue
+            }
+        }
+        // The entry point keeps the bare symbol `main`; every other
+        // function, an `async fn main` too, is mangled with its id.
+        let name = if def_id == entry && !func.is_async {
+            "main"
+        } else {
+            mangle(def_id, func.name, type_args)
+        }
+        if def_id == entry && func.is_async { async_entry = Some(name) }
+        if done.contains_key(name) { continue }
+        let _ = done.insert(name, true)
+        let mut concrete = true
+        for i in 0..type_args.len() { if has_params(type_args.at(i)) { concrete = false } }
+        for i in 0..type_args.len() { if has_vars(type_args.at(i)) { concrete = false } }
+        if !concrete {
+            diagnostics.push(
+                error_at(
+                    "E0011",
+                    "cannot monomorphize `${func.name}` with non-concrete type arguments",
+                    func.span.file,
+                    func.span.span,
+                    "instantiated here",
+                ),
+            )
+            continue
+        }
+        // Each concrete type argument must satisfy its generic parameter's
+        // trait bounds.
+        let mut bounds_ok = true
+        for i in 0..func.bounds.len() {
+            if i >= type_args.len() { continue }
+            let arg = type_args.at(i)
+            for trait_id in func.bounds.at(i).iter() {
+                if !impl_satisfies(program, arg, trait_id) {
+                    bounds_ok = false
+                    let trait_name = match program.trait_def(trait_id) {
+                        Some(t) => t.name
+                        None => "trait#${trait_id}"
+                    }
+                    let mut d = error_at(
+                        "E0013",
+                        "trait bound `${type_name(arg, program)}: ${trait_name}` is not satisfied when instantiating `${func.name}`",
+                        func.span.file,
+                        func.span.span,
+                        "required by this generic parameter",
+                    )
+                    match arg {
+                        TyTuple(_) => d.add_note(TUPLE_HAS_NO_IMPLS)
+                        _ => ()
+                    }
+                    diagnostics.push(d)
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        // An instance with unsatisfied bounds is not lowered: its body
+        // would fail to resolve the very trait methods the bound
+        // guarantees.
+        if !bounds_ok { continue }
+        // The body specialized for these type arguments, every projection
+        // they make resolvable resolved.
+        let mut spec = Specializer {
+            args: type_args,
+            impls: program.impls,
+            unresolved: None,
+            overflow: None,
+        }
+        let specialized = spec.function(func)
+        let mut resolved_ok = true
+        match spec.overflow {
+            Some((at, limit)) => {
+                resolved_ok = false
+                let detail = match limit {
+                    NormalizeLimitDepth => "the chain of bindings is more than ${NORMALIZE_DEPTH_LIMIT} deep, or does not terminate"
+                    NormalizeLimitSteps => "it resolves to more than ${NORMALIZE_STEP_LIMIT} type nodes"
+                }
+                diagnostics.push(
+                    error_at(
+                        "E0078",
+                        "could not resolve the associated types in `${type_name(at, program)}` when instantiating `${func.name}`: ${detail}",
+                        func.span.file,
+                        func.span.span,
+                        "instantiated here",
+                    ),
+                )
+            }
+            None => ()
+        }
+        match spec.unresolved {
+            Some(at) => {
+                resolved_ok = false
+                diagnostics.push(
+                    error_at(
+                        "E0079",
+                        "`${type_name(at, program)}` is still an unresolved associated type after instantiating `${func.name}`; no impl in scope binds it for this Self type",
+                        func.span.file,
+                        func.span.span,
+                        "instantiated here",
+                    ),
+                )
+            }
+            None => ()
+        }
+        // An instance whose types are not what they claim is not lowered.
+        if !resolved_ok { continue }
+        match lower_function(specialized, name, program, entry, worklist, builtins) {
+            Ok(f) => functions.push(f)
+            Err(ds) => for d in ds.iter() { diagnostics.push(d) }
+        }
+    }
+    if !diagnostics.is_empty() { return Err(diagnostics) }
+    // Before the transform, so the shim is in place whichever order the
+    // two are read in: it is not async, so the transform walks past it.
+    match async_entry {
+        Some(wrapper) => functions.push(async_main_shim(wrapper))
+        None => ()
+    }
+    let mir = MirModule { functions: functions, externs: externs }
+    // The hidden MIR dump's before form.
+    if stop_before_async { return Ok(mir) }
+    panic(
+        "the async transform is not ported to N yet (5.4's next part): only the before form is lowered",
+    )
+}
+
+// The first extern of `externs` whose symbol is `symbol`.
+fn first_extern(externs: Vec<ExternDecl>, symbol: String) -> Option<ExternDecl> {
+    for e in externs.iter() { if e.symbol == symbol { return Some(e) } }
+    None
+}
+
+/// Element-wise `==` of two lists of codes (`Vec<MirTy>`'s `==`).
+pub fn ints_eq(a: Vec<Int>, b: Vec<Int>) -> Bool {
+    if a.len() != b.len() { return false }
+    for i in 0..a.len() { if a.at(i) != b.at(i) { return false } }
+    true
+}
+
+/// The `main` an `async fn main` gets (`async_main_shim`): call the
+/// future-building wrapper `wrapper`, then hand the future to the
+/// executor, discarding its output.
+pub fn async_main_shim(wrapper: String) -> MirFunction {
+    let future = 0
+    let mut stmts: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+    stmts.push(SCall(future, wrapper, Vec::new()))
+    stmts.push(SCallRuntime(-1, RT_TASK_BLOCK_ON, Vec::from_array([future])))
+    let mut blocks: Vec<MirBlock> = Vec::new()
+    blocks.push(MirBlock { stmts: stmts, term: TermReturn(-1) })
+    MirFunction {
+        name: "main",
+        params: 0,
+        takes_env: false,
+        capture_count: 0,
+        temps: Vec::from_array([MIR_PTR]),
+        ret: MIR_UNIT,
+        is_async: false,
+        blocks: blocks,
+    }
+}
+
+/// Whether the concrete type `arg` implements `trait_id`
+/// (`impl_satisfies`): an impl of the trait whose self type fits `arg`,
+/// and whose own generic bounds hold for the arguments recovered from it.
+pub fn impl_satisfies(program: HirModule, arg: Ty, trait_id: Int) -> Bool {
+    let h = match head(arg) {
+        Some(h) => h
+        None => return false
+    }
+    for imp in program.impls.iter() {
+        if imp.trait_id != trait_id || !head_eq(imp.self_head, h) { continue }
+        match match_args(imp, arg) {
+            Some(impl_args) => {
+                let mut all = true
+                for i in 0..imp.bounds.len() {
+                    for bound in imp.bounds.at(i).iter() {
+                        let holds = if i < impl_args.len() {
+                            impl_satisfies(program, impl_args.at(i), bound)
+                        } else {
+                            false
+                        }
+                        if !holds {
+                            all = false
+                            break
+                        }
+                    }
+                    if !all { break }
+                }
+                if all { return true }
+            }
+            None => ()
+        }
+    }
+    false
+}
+
+/// A short display name for a type in monomorphization's diagnostics
+/// (`type_name`), its names read from the module; a projection is
+/// `<on>::Name`.
+pub fn type_name(ty: Ty, program: HirModule) -> String {
+    match ty {
+        TyInt => "Int"
+        TyFloat => "Float"
+        TyBool => "Bool"
+        TyChar => "Char"
+        TyString => "String"
+        TyBytes => "Bytes"
+        TyUnit => "()"
+        TySum(d, _) => {
+            match program.sum(d) {
+                Some(s) => s.name
+                None => "?"
+            }
+        }
+        TyRecord(d, _) => {
+            match program.record_type(d) {
+                Some(r) => r.name
+                None => "?"
+            }
+        }
+        TyArray(e) => "[${type_name(e, program)}]"
+        TyTuple(es) => {
+            let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+            for e in es.iter() { names.push(type_name(e, program)) }
+            if names.len() == 1 { "(${names.at(0)},)" } else { "(${", ".join(names.to_array())})" }
+        }
+        TyFuture(o) => "Future<${type_name(o, program)}>"
+        TyAssoc(on, assoc) => {
+            let mut name = "?"
+            for t in program.traits.iter() {
+                let mut hit = false
+                for (n, d) in t.assoc_types.iter() {
+                    if d == assoc {
+                        name = n
+                        hit = true
+                        break
+                    }
+                }
+                if hit { break }
+            }
+            "${type_name(on, program)}::${name}"
+        }
+        _ => "?"
+    }
+}
+
+/// `subst` then normalization, over every type of an instance
+/// (`Specializer`): the first projection left unresolved and the first
+/// normalization out of budget, with the type it was asked about, are
+/// kept for one diagnostic each.
+pub record Specializer {
+    pub args: Vec<Ty>,
+    pub impls: Vec<ImplInfo>,
+    pub unresolved: Option<Ty>,
+    pub overflow: Option<(Ty, NormalizeLimit)>,
+}
+
+impl Specializer {
+    /// `ty` substituted and normalized (`Specializer::ty`).
+    pub fn ty(mut self, ty: Ty) -> Ty {
+        let substituted = subst(ty, self.args)
+        if !has_assoc(substituted) { return substituted }
+        match normalize_ty(substituted, self.impls) {
+            Ok(resolved) => {
+                if has_assoc(resolved) && self.unresolved.is_none() {
+                    self.unresolved = Some(resolved)
+                }
+                resolved
+            }
+            Err(limit) => {
+                if self.overflow.is_none() { self.overflow = Some((substituted, limit)) }
+                substituted
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A new function, every type substituted and normalized: locals,
+    /// return type, then body (`Specializer::function`).
+    pub fn function(mut self, func: HirFunction) -> HirFunction {
+        let mut locals: Vec<Local> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..func.locals.len() {
+            let l = func.locals.at(i)
+            locals.push(Local { name: l.name, ty: self.ty(l.ty), is_mut: l.is_mut, span: l.span })
+        }
+        let ret_ty = self.ty(func.ret_ty)
+        let body = self.expr(func.body)
+        HirFunction {
+            def_id: func.def_id,
+            name: func.name,
+            generics: 0,
+            bounds: Vec::new(),
+            takes_env: func.takes_env,
+            capture_count: func.capture_count,
+            params: func.params,
+            locals: locals,
+            ret_ty: ret_ty,
+            is_async: func.is_async,
+            body: body,
+            span: func.span,
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn tys(mut self, tys: Vec<Ty>) -> Vec<Ty> {
+        let mut out: Vec<Ty> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..tys.len() { out.push(self.ty(tys.at(i))) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    fn exprs(mut self, exprs: Vec<HirExpr>) -> Vec<HirExpr> {
+        let mut out: Vec<HirExpr> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..exprs.len() { out.push(self.expr(exprs.at(i))) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    fn opt(mut self, e: Option<HirExpr>) -> Option<HirExpr> {
+        match e {
+            Some(x) => Some(self.expr(x))
+            None => None
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A new node for `e` and every node below it, in the order S0's
+    /// struct literals evaluate them, then its type
+    /// (`Specializer::expr`).
+    pub fn expr(mut self, e: HirExpr) -> HirExpr {
+        let kind = match e.kind {
+            EkIntLit(v) => EkIntLit(v)
+            EkFloatLit(v) => EkFloatLit(v)
+            EkBoolLit(v) => EkBoolLit(v)
+            EkStrLit(v) => EkStrLit(v)
+            EkCharLit(v) => EkCharLit(v)
+            EkUnit => EkUnit
+            EkBreak => EkBreak
+            EkContinue => EkContinue
+            EkLocal(l) => EkLocal(l)
+            EkMakeClosure(func, type_args, captures) => {
+                let ta = self.tys(type_args)
+                EkMakeClosure(func, ta, self.exprs(captures))
+            }
+            EkCall(func, type_args, args) => {
+                let ta = self.tys(type_args)
+                EkCall(func, ta, self.exprs(args))
+            }
+            EkMakeVariant(sum, variant, args) => EkMakeVariant(sum, variant, self.exprs(args))
+            EkMakeRecord(rec, fields) => EkMakeRecord(rec, self.exprs(fields))
+            EkMakeTuple(elems) => EkMakeTuple(self.exprs(elems))
+            EkFieldGet(target, index) => EkFieldGet(self.expr(target), index)
+            EkFieldSet(target, index, value) => {
+                let t = self.expr(target)
+                EkFieldSet(t, index, self.expr(value))
+            }
+            EkMakeArray(elems) => EkMakeArray(self.exprs(elems))
+            EkArrayRepeat(init, len) => {
+                let i = self.expr(init)
+                EkArrayRepeat(i, self.expr(len))
+            }
+            EkIndex(target, index) => {
+                let t = self.expr(target)
+                EkIndex(t, self.expr(index))
+            }
+            EkIndexSet(target, index, value) => {
+                let t = self.expr(target)
+                let i = self.expr(index)
+                EkIndexSet(t, i, self.expr(value))
+            }
+            EkArrayLen(target) => EkArrayLen(self.expr(target))
+            EkTraitCall(trait_id, method, self_ty, type_args, receiver, args) => {
+                let st = self.ty(self_ty)
+                let ta = self.tys(type_args)
+                // `None` for a trait associated function: substitution must
+                // not invent a receiver for one.
+                let r = self.opt(receiver)
+                EkTraitCall(trait_id, method, st, ta, r, self.exprs(args))
+            }
+            EkBinary(op, lhs, rhs) => {
+                let l = self.expr(lhs)
+                EkBinary(op, l, self.expr(rhs))
+            }
+            EkLogicalAnd(lhs, rhs) => {
+                let l = self.expr(lhs)
+                EkLogicalAnd(l, self.expr(rhs))
+            }
+            EkLogicalOr(lhs, rhs) => {
+                let l = self.expr(lhs)
+                EkLogicalOr(l, self.expr(rhs))
+            }
+            EkUnary(op, inner) => EkUnary(op, self.expr(inner))
+            EkLet(local, init) => EkLet(local, self.expr(init))
+            EkAssign(local, value) => EkAssign(local, self.expr(value))
+            EkBlock(stmts, trailing) => {
+                let s = self.exprs(stmts)
+                EkBlock(s, self.opt(trailing))
+            }
+            EkIf(cond, then, else_) => {
+                let c = self.expr(cond)
+                let t = self.expr(then)
+                EkIf(c, t, self.opt(else_))
+            }
+            EkWhile(cond, body) => {
+                let c = self.expr(cond)
+                EkWhile(c, self.expr(body))
+            }
+            EkMatch(scrutinee, arms) => {
+                let s = self.expr(scrutinee)
+                let mut out: Vec<HirArm> = Vec::new()
+                for i in 0..arms.len() {
+                    let a = arms.at(i)
+                    let guard = self.opt(a.guard)
+                    let body = self.expr(a.body)
+                    out.push(HirArm { pattern: a.pattern, guard: guard, body: body, span: a.span })
+                }
+                EkMatch(s, out)
+            }
+            EkReturn(v) => EkReturn(self.opt(v))
+            EkToStr(inner) => EkToStr(self.expr(inner))
+            EkStrConcat(parts) => EkStrConcat(self.exprs(parts))
+            EkAwait(inner) => EkAwait(self.expr(inner))
+        }
+        HirExpr { kind: kind, ty: self.ty(e.ty), span: e.span }
+    }
+}
+
+/// The name a `main` declared outside the entry module takes
+/// (`NOT_THE_ENTRY_MAIN`).
+pub const NOT_THE_ENTRY_MAIN: String = "main.not_the_entry"
+
+/// Rename every `main` declared outside the entry module, module 0, so
+/// that `lower_module_with`'s search by name finds the entry's own `main`
+/// or reports E0601 (the driver's `keep_the_entry_main`). A call reaches
+/// its function by id, so the rename breaks no call. The renamed function
+/// is a new record, in the old one's place.
+pub fn keep_the_entry_main(mut program: HirModule, definitions: Definitions) {
+    for i in 0..program.functions.len() {
+        let f = program.functions.at(i)
+        if f.name != "main" { continue }
+        if f.def_id < 0 || f.def_id >= definitions.defs.len() { continue }
+        let item = match definitions.defs.at(f.def_id).kind {
+            DefKindFn(item) => item
+            _ => continue
+        }
+        let m = if item >= 0 && item < definitions.item_module.len() {
+            definitions.item_module.at(item)
+        } else {
+            0
+        }
+        if m != 0 {
+            program.functions.set(
+                i,
+                HirFunction {
+                    def_id: f.def_id,
+                    name: NOT_THE_ENTRY_MAIN,
+                    generics: f.generics,
+                    bounds: f.bounds,
+                    takes_env: f.takes_env,
+                    capture_count: f.capture_count,
+                    params: f.params,
+                    locals: f.locals,
+                    ret_ty: f.ret_ty,
+                    is_async: f.is_async,
+                    body: f.body,
+                    span: f.span,
+                },
+            )
+        }
+    }
+}
diff --git a/selfhost/src/splice.nova b/selfhost/src/splice.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..52c7fc2d
--- /dev/null
+++ b/selfhost/src/splice.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,259 @@
+// std's checked functions spliced into a program: a port of
+// crates/nova-typeck/src/check/items.rs's `shared_module` and `Shift`
+// (5.4, spec docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md
+// §5, ADR 0057), beside `Checked::into_module` (`full_module`). N's
+// `SharedItems` keeps its whole checker, std's checked functions with it
+// (5.3b), so each program's MIR reads the module the full check builds:
+// the shared check's tables and functions, std's functions copied in with
+// their ids moved.
+//
+// - Records alias in N, so a copy is made of new functions, nodes, types
+//   and patterns, as S0's clone is, and never writes into std's shared
+//   functions, which every program reads (spec §6.2).
+
+import hir
+import typeck
+
+/// The module the full check builds (`Checked::into_module`, 5.4): its
+/// tables and its functions.
+pub fn full_module(tables: Checker) -> HirModule {
+    hir_module(
+        tables.sums,
+        tables.records,
+        tables.traits,
+        tables.impls,
+        tables.functions,
+        tables.externs,
+    )
+}
+
+/// The module the full check builds, from a program's shared check
+/// `checked` and std's checked functions, which `share_items` kept, copied
+/// in with their ids moved to where the full check numbers them
+/// (`shared_module`; spec
+/// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §5, ADR
+/// 0057). With `S` std's definitions, `D` the program's and `P` its
+/// closures and wrappers (`Shift`): every id below `S` moves up by `D`,
+/// every closure's or wrapper's, `S` and up, by `D + P`, past the
+/// program's, and a lifted function's name `closure$N` or `fnval$N` takes
+/// its new id; nothing else changes. std's functions go after the
+/// program's functions, methods and constants, std's closures after the
+/// program's, as the full check orders them (`check_bodies`). Each copy is
+/// made of new nodes: std's shared functions are never written. The
+/// tables are the shared check's, std's parts rebased already.
+pub fn shared_module(checked: Checker, shared: SharedItems) -> HirModule {
+    let own_defs = checked.closures - shared.defs
+    let shift = Shift {
+        std_defs: shared.defs,
+        defs: own_defs,
+        closures: own_defs + (checked.std_closures - checked.closures),
+    }
+    let std = shared.tables.functions
+    let own = checked.functions
+    let std_lifted = first_lifted(std, shared.defs)
+    let own_lifted = first_lifted(own, checked.closures)
+    let mut functions: Vec<HirFunction> = Vec::new()
+    for i in 0..own_lifted { functions.push(own.at(i)) }
+    for i in 0..std_lifted { functions.push(shift.function(std.at(i))) }
+    for i in own_lifted..own.len() { functions.push(own.at(i)) }
+    for i in std_lifted..std.len() { functions.push(shift.function(std.at(i))) }
+    hir_module(
+        checked.sums,
+        checked.records,
+        checked.traits,
+        checked.impls,
+        functions,
+        checked.externs,
+    )
+}
+
+// The position of the first of `fs` whose id is `from` or past it: the
+// first closure or wrapper.
+fn first_lifted(fs: Vec<HirFunction>, from: Int) -> Int {
+    for i in 0..fs.len() { if fs.at(i).def_id >= from { return i } }
+    fs.len()
+}
+
+/// How std's checked functions' ids move in a program (`Shift`): a
+/// definition's, below `std_defs`, up by the program's `defs`; a closure's
+/// or wrapper's, from `std_defs` on, up by `closures`, the program's
+/// definitions and its own closures and wrappers.
+pub record Shift { pub std_defs: Int, pub defs: Int, pub closures: Int }
+
+impl Shift {
+    pub fn id(self, id: Int) -> Int {
+        if id < self.std_defs { id + self.defs } else { id + self.closures }
+    }
+
+    /// A copy of std's function `f`, its ids moved: its own and its
+    /// bounds', every type's on its locals, its return and its body, and
+    /// every id its body names; a closure's or wrapper's name, which holds
+    /// its id, written again (`Shift::function`).
+    pub fn function(self, f: HirFunction) -> HirFunction {
+        let def_id = self.id(f.def_id)
+        let mut name = f.name
+        if f.def_id >= self.std_defs {
+            for prefix in ["closure$", "fnval$"].iter() {
+                if f.name == prefix + "${f.def_id}" { name = prefix + "${def_id}" }
+            }
+        }
+        let mut locals: Vec<Local> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..f.locals.len() {
+            let l = f.locals.at(i)
+            locals.push(Local { name: l.name, ty: self.ty(l.ty), is_mut: l.is_mut, span: l.span })
+        }
+        let mut bounds: Vec<Vec<Int>> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..f.bounds.len() {
+            let b = f.bounds.at(i)
+            let mut ids: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+            for k in 0..b.len() { ids.push(self.id(b.at(k))) }
+            bounds.push(ids)
+        }
+        HirFunction {
+            def_id: def_id,
+            name: name,
+            generics: f.generics,
+            bounds: bounds,
+            takes_env: f.takes_env,
+            capture_count: f.capture_count,
+            params: f.params,
+            locals: locals,
+            ret_ty: self.ty(f.ret_ty),
+            is_async: f.is_async,
+            body: self.expr(f.body),
+            span: f.span,
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// `ty`, every id in it moved (`Shift::ty`).
+    pub fn ty(self, ty: Ty) -> Ty {
+        match ty {
+            TyFn(params, ret) => TyFn(self.tys(params), self.ty(ret))
+            TyFuture(o) => TyFuture(self.ty(o))
+            TyArray(e) => TyArray(self.ty(e))
+            TySum(d, args) => TySum(self.id(d), self.tys(args))
+            TyRecord(d, args) => TyRecord(self.id(d), self.tys(args))
+            TyTuple(es) => TyTuple(self.tys(es))
+            TyAssoc(on, assoc) => TyAssoc(self.ty(on), self.id(assoc))
+            other => other
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn tys(self, tys: Vec<Ty>) -> Vec<Ty> {
+        let mut out: Vec<Ty> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..tys.len() { out.push(self.ty(tys.at(i))) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    fn exprs(self, exprs: Vec<HirExpr>) -> Vec<HirExpr> {
+        let mut out: Vec<HirExpr> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..exprs.len() { out.push(self.expr(exprs.at(i))) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    fn opt(self, e: Option<HirExpr>) -> Option<HirExpr> {
+        match e {
+            Some(x) => Some(self.expr(x))
+            None => None
+        }
+    }
+
+    /// A new node for `e`, its type and every node below it, each id moved
+    /// (`Shift::expr`).
+    pub fn expr(self, e: HirExpr) -> HirExpr {
+        let kind = match e.kind {
+            EkMakeClosure(func, type_args, captures) => EkMakeClosure(
+                self.id(func),
+                self.tys(type_args),
+                self.exprs(captures),
+            )
+            EkCall(func, type_args, args) => {
+                let callee = match func {
+                    CalleeDef(id) => CalleeDef(self.id(id))
+                    other => other
+                }
+                EkCall(callee, self.tys(type_args), self.exprs(args))
+            }
+            EkMakeVariant(sum, variant, args) => EkMakeVariant(
+                self.id(sum),
+                variant,
+                self.exprs(args),
+            )
+            EkMakeRecord(rec, fields) => EkMakeRecord(self.id(rec), self.exprs(fields))
+            EkMakeTuple(elems) => EkMakeTuple(self.exprs(elems))
+            EkMakeArray(elems) => EkMakeArray(self.exprs(elems))
+            EkStrConcat(parts) => EkStrConcat(self.exprs(parts))
+            EkFieldGet(target, index) => EkFieldGet(self.expr(target), index)
+            EkArrayLen(target) => EkArrayLen(self.expr(target))
+            EkUnary(op, inner) => EkUnary(op, self.expr(inner))
+            EkLet(local, init) => EkLet(local, self.expr(init))
+            EkAssign(local, value) => EkAssign(local, self.expr(value))
+            EkToStr(inner) => EkToStr(self.expr(inner))
+            EkAwait(inner) => EkAwait(self.expr(inner))
+            EkFieldSet(target, index, value) => EkFieldSet(
+                self.expr(target),
+                index,
+                self.expr(value),
+            )
+            EkArrayRepeat(init, len) => EkArrayRepeat(self.expr(init), self.expr(len))
+            EkIndex(target, index) => EkIndex(self.expr(target), self.expr(index))
+            EkIndexSet(target, index, value) => EkIndexSet(
+                self.expr(target),
+                self.expr(index),
+                self.expr(value),
+            )
+            EkTraitCall(trait_id, method, self_ty, type_args, receiver, args) => EkTraitCall(
+                self.id(trait_id),
+                method,
+                self.ty(self_ty),
+                self.tys(type_args),
+                self.opt(receiver),
+                self.exprs(args),
+            )
+            EkBinary(op, lhs, rhs) => EkBinary(op, self.expr(lhs), self.expr(rhs))
+            EkLogicalAnd(lhs, rhs) => EkLogicalAnd(self.expr(lhs), self.expr(rhs))
+            EkLogicalOr(lhs, rhs) => EkLogicalOr(self.expr(lhs), self.expr(rhs))
+            EkBlock(stmts, trailing) => EkBlock(self.exprs(stmts), self.opt(trailing))
+            EkIf(cond, then, else_) => EkIf(self.expr(cond), self.expr(then), self.opt(else_))
+            EkWhile(cond, body) => EkWhile(self.expr(cond), self.expr(body))
+            EkMatch(scrutinee, arms) => {
+                let mut out: Vec<HirArm> = Vec::new()
+                for i in 0..arms.len() {
+                    let a = arms.at(i)
+                    out.push(
+                        HirArm {
+                            pattern: self.pattern(a.pattern),
+                            guard: self.opt(a.guard),
+                            body: self.expr(a.body),
+                            span: a.span,
+                        },
+                    )
+                }
+                EkMatch(self.expr(scrutinee), out)
+            }
+            EkReturn(value) => EkReturn(self.opt(value))
+            other => other
+        }
+        HirExpr { kind: kind, ty: self.ty(e.ty), span: e.span }
+    }
+
+    /// `p`, a variant's sum moved, at any depth (`Shift::pattern`).
+    pub fn pattern(self, p: HirPattern) -> HirPattern {
+        match p {
+            PatVariant(sum, variant, fields) => PatVariant(
+                self.id(sum),
+                variant,
+                self.patterns(fields),
+            )
+            PatTuple(ps) => PatTuple(self.patterns(ps))
+            PatOr(ps) => PatOr(self.patterns(ps))
+            other => other
+        }
+    }
+
+    fn patterns(self, ps: Vec<HirPattern>) -> Vec<HirPattern> {
+        let mut out: Vec<HirPattern> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..ps.len() { out.push(self.pattern(ps.at(i))) }
+        out
+    }
+}
````

+/// Each root's part of `out`, its header line first, in order; anything
+/// before the first header is a part of its own.
+fn headed_parts(out: &str) -> std::collections::VecDeque<String> {
+    let mut roots = std::collections::VecDeque::new();
+    for line in out.split_inclusive('\n') {
+        if line.starts_with("== ") || roots.is_empty() {
+            roots.push_back(String::new());
+        }
+        roots.back_mut().expect("a root's part").push_str(line);
+    }
+    roots
+}
+

     if files.is_empty() {

+/// Whether a root's part of the MIR dump holds a poll function, which the
+/// async transform made of an async function (spec 5.4 §2 item 9).
+fn holds_a_poll_function(part: &str) -> bool {
+    part.contains("$poll\" ")
+}
+
+/// The MIR dump in both forms on `files`, relative to `dir`, each a
+/// program's root, each side exiting with `code` (spec 5.4 §7.2): the
+/// after form compared with N's on every root; the before form compared
+/// on the roots whose part in S0's after form holds a poll function, so
+/// that a difference the transform makes is placed, and S0's alone on the
+/// rest, where the transform changed nothing and the forms are equal,
+/// which [`the_mir_dumps_agree_on_the_corpus_and_the_cases`] checks. A root
+/// that cannot be read holds no poll function, so the roots compared in
+/// the before form exit 0. Returns S0's two outputs, after then before,
+/// each with every root's part in the roots' order.
+fn agree_on_mir_forms(dir: &Path, files: &[String], code: i32) -> (String, String) {
+    let after = mir_dump_in(MirForm::After, true, dir, files, code);
+    let polls: Vec<bool> = parts_by_root(&after)
+        .iter()
+        .map(|(_, part)| holds_a_poll_function(part))
+        .collect();
+    let pick = |poll: bool| -> Vec<String> {
+        files
+            .iter()
+            .zip(&polls)
+            .filter(|(_, p)| **p == poll)
+            .map(|(f, _)| f.clone())
+            .collect()
+    };
+    let (with, without) = (pick(true), pick(false));
+    let (compared, alone) = std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+        let alone = scope.spawn(|| mir_dump_in(MirForm::Before, false, dir, &without, code));
+        let compared = mir_dump_in(MirForm::Before, true, dir, &with, 0);
+        (compared, joined(vec![alone]).remove(0))
+    });
+    let (mut compared, mut alone) = (headed_parts(&compared), headed_parts(&alone));
+    let mut before = String::new();
+    for (root, poll) in files.iter().zip(&polls) {
+        let part = if *poll {
+            compared.pop_front()
+        } else {
+            alone.pop_front()
+        };
+        let part = part.expect("each root's part");
+        assert!(part.starts_with(&format!("== {root}\n")), "{root}: {part}");
+        before.push_str(&part);
+    }
+    assert!(
+        compared.is_empty() && alone.is_empty(),
+        "a part no root was dealt"
+    );
+    (after, before)
+}
+

-                })
-            })
-            .collect();
-        for (form, (dumps, count), (lf, lf_count)) in joined(forms) {
-            assert_eq!(count, *lf_count, "{form:?}");
-            assert_eq!(parts_by_root(&dumps).len(), count, "{form:?}");
-            assert_eq!(codes_in(&dumps), codes_in(lf), "{form:?}");
-            assert_ne!(&dumps, lf, "{form:?}: no place moved");
-        }
+    let (crlf, lf) = std::thread::scope(|scope| {
+        let lf = scope.spawn(mir_cases);
+        (agree_on_mir_cases(&check, &mir), joined(vec![lf]).remove(0))
     });
+    assert_eq!((crlf.hir, crlf.count), (lf.hir, lf.count));
+    for (form, dumps, lf) in [
+        (MirForm::After, &crlf.after, &lf.after),
+        (MirForm::Before, &crlf.before, &lf.before),
+    ] {
+        assert_eq!(parts_by_root(dumps).len(), crlf.count, "{form:?}");
+        assert_eq!(codes_in(dumps), codes_in(lf), "{form:?}");
+        assert_ne!(dumps, lf, "{form:?}: no place moved");
+    }
 }
diff --git a/selfhost/src/mir_tests.nova b/selfhost/src/mir_tests.nova
index 4ef1bb89..3f4edffe 100644
--- a/selfhost/src/mir_tests.nova
+++ b/selfhost/src/mir_tests.nova
@@ -2,13 +2,15 @@
 // docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §6.3): each
 // type's code in a symbol (`mangle_ty`, mono.rs's tests' `mangle_ty`
 // cases), the lowering's runtime functions by name, a generic function
-// instantiated twice with its own nodes unchanged, and std's functions
-// spliced into a program twice with std's shared functions unchanged. The
-// differential compares everything else with S0.
+// instantiated twice with its own nodes unchanged, std's functions
+// spliced into a program twice with std's shared functions unchanged, and
+// the async transform's state slots for values live across two awaits.
+// The differential compares everything else with S0.
 //
 // They live in `src/`, imported by `main.nova`, for the reason
 // lexer_tests.nova gives.
 
+import async_lower
 import builtins
 import hir
 import lexer
@@ -66,6 +68,7 @@ fn the_lowerings_runtime_functions_are_named_as_s0_names_them() {
         (RT_BOOL_TO_STR, "BoolToStr"),
         (RT_CHAR_TO_STR, "CharToStr"),
         (RT_STR_EQ, "StrEq"),
+        (RT_ALLOC, "Alloc"),
         (RT_CHECK_BOUNDS, "CheckBounds"),
         (RT_PANIC, "Panic"),
         (RT_TASK_BLOCK_ON, "TaskBlockOn"),
@@ -233,3 +236,89 @@ fn a_splice_done_twice_leaves_stds_shared_functions_as_they_were() {
     for f in items.tables.functions.iter() { std_last.push(function_text(f)) }
     assert_eq("\n".join(std_last.to_array()), "\n".join(std_before.to_array()))
 }
+
+// --- The async transform's state slots ---
+
+// Each block's accesses to the state object `state`, in order: `R<slot>`
+// a load, `W<slot>` a store.
+fn state_slots_text(blocks: Vec<MirBlock>, state: Int) -> String {
+    let mut lines: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for i in 0..blocks.len() {
+        let mut line = "bb${i}"
+        for s in blocks.at(i).stmts.iter() {
+            match s {
+                SRecordField(_, rec, index, _) => if rec == state { line = line + " R${index}" }
+                SSetField(rec, index, _, _) => if rec == state { line = line + " W${index}" }
+                _ => ()
+            }
+        }
+        lines.push(line)
+    }
+    " | ".join(lines.to_array())
+}
+
+@test
+fn the_async_transform_keeps_values_live_across_two_awaits_in_their_slots() {
+    // `async fn f(x: Int) -> Int { let a = g(x).await; let b = g(a).await;
+    // x + b }` as lowering leaves it: `x` t0, `g(x)` t1, `a` t2, `g(a)` t3,
+    // `b` t4, `x + b` t5. `x` is live across both awaits, `a` across the
+    // second's call.
+    let mut stmts: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+    stmts.push(SCall(1, "g.1", Vec::from_array([0])))
+    stmts.push(SAwait(2, 1))
+    stmts.push(SCall(3, "g.1", Vec::from_array([2])))
+    stmts.push(SAwait(4, 3))
+    stmts.push(SBin(5, OpAdd, OC_INT, 0, 4))
+    let f = MirFunction {
+        name: "f.0",
+        params: 1,
+        takes_env: false,
+        capture_count: 0,
+        temps: Vec::from_array([MIR_I64, MIR_PTR, MIR_I64, MIR_PTR, MIR_I64, MIR_I64]),
+        ret: MIR_I64,
+        is_async: true,
+        blocks: Vec::from_array([MirBlock { stmts: stmts, term: TermReturn(5) }]),
+    }
+    let program = MirModule { functions: Vec::from_array([f]), externs: Vec::new() }
+    let out = transform(program)
+    let mut names: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for g in out.functions.iter() { names.push(g.name) }
+    assert_eq(" ".join(names.to_array()), "f.0" + "$" + "poll f.0")
+    // The poll function: the state object t0, task_ctx t1, then the
+    // dispatch on the tag, the trap, the piece before the first await and
+    // a poll, suspend and continuation per await. Each original temp `i`
+    // is slot `2 + i`: `x` slot 2, stored by the wrapper, read after the
+    // second await; `a` slot 4 and `b` slot 6, stored by their
+    // continuations; the output slot 1 last.
+    let poll = out.functions.at(0)
+    assert(poll.takes_env && poll.params == 1 && !poll.is_async, "the poll function's ABI")
+    assert_eq(MIR_TY_NAMES[poll.ret], "I64")
+    assert(poll.temps.len() == 26, "${poll.temps.len()} temps in the poll function")
+    assert_eq(
+        state_slots_text(poll.blocks, 0),
+        "bb0 R0 | bb1 | bb2 R2 W3 | bb3 R3 | bb4 W0 | bb5 R3 W4 R4 W5 | bb6 R5 | bb7 W0 | bb8 R5 W6 R2 R6 W7 R7 W1",
+    )
+    match poll.blocks.at(0).term {
+        TermSwitch(_, arms, default) => {
+            let mut text: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+            for (tag, b) in arms.iter() { text.push("${tag}:bb${b}") }
+            assert_eq(
+                " ".join(text.to_array()) + " else bb${default}",
+                "0:bb2 1:bb3 2:bb6 else bb1",
+            )
+        }
+        _ => panic("the poll function enters at a switch on the tag")
+    }
+    // The wrapper: 8 slots, 64 bytes, its state t2, the tag and `x` seeded.
+    let wrapper = out.functions.at(1)
+    assert(!wrapper.is_async && wrapper.params == 1, "the wrapper keeps the signature")
+    match wrapper.blocks.at(0).stmts.at(0) {
+        SConstInt(1, bytes) => assert(bytes == 64, "${bytes} bytes")
+        _ => panic("the wrapper sizes its state object first")
+    }
+    assert_eq(state_slots_text(wrapper.blocks, 2), "bb0 W0 W2")
+    // The module lowering left is as it was.
+    let before = program.functions.at(0)
+    assert(before.is_async && before.temps.len() == 6, "the async function is unchanged")
+    assert(before.blocks.len() == 1 && before.blocks.at(0).stmts.len() == 5, "its body too")
+}
````
