````diff
diff --git a/selfhost/src/async_lower.nova b/selfhost/src/async_lower.nova
new file mode 100644
index 00000000..ec8d71e6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/selfhost/src/async_lower.nova
@@ -0,0 +1,692 @@
+// The async state-machine transform: a port of
+// crates/nova-mir/src/async_lower.rs's `transform` and everything it
+// calls, function by function and with the same names (spec
+// docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md §6.1, ADR
+// 0057). It runs on the finished module, after the async `main`'s shim:
+// each async function becomes `<name>$poll`, `(state, task_ctx) -> i64`,
+// its body split at its awaits and every value it names kept in a state
+// object; a wrapper under the old symbol allocates the state object, seeds
+// it with the arguments and returns the `{ poll_code, state }` future; the
+// wrappers follow every function, in their async functions' order. S0's
+// module comment gives the reasons (the split, the spill of every value,
+// no unwind through a poll function); the layout is the runtime's
+// (`nova-runtime/src/task.rs`).
+//
+// The Rust's idioms, beyond mir.nova's (ADR 0057):
+//
+// - S0 rewrites each async function in place; here every node is new: the
+//   module, the poll function, each block and each statement, its temps
+//   substituted. The module lowering left is never written into (spec
+//   §6.2).
+// - `visit_temps` gives each temp a statement mentions to `SpillVisit`'s
+//   `visit`, S0's callback, in the order S0's match does, reads before the
+//   write: the scratch temps are numbered in that order.
+// - `split_at_awaits`'s segments are read by index, and a block's
+//   original terminator is held in a `TermSlot` until a piece takes it.
+// - S0's `debug_assert!`s and its four `expect`s are `panic`s with S0's
+//   words, never reached on a valid input (spec §6.2).
+
+import mir
+
+/// The resume tag's state slot, at byte offset 0 (`STATE_SLOT_TAG`):
+/// which of a poll function's states to enter.
+pub const STATE_SLOT_TAG: Int = 0
+
+/// The first per-temp state slot, at byte offset 16
+/// (`STATE_SLOT_TEMPS`): original temp `i` lives at slot
+/// `STATE_SLOT_TEMPS + i`.
+pub const STATE_SLOT_TEMPS: Int = 2
+
+/// The smallest state object the transform may emit, the tag and output
+/// slots (`STATE_MIN_SIZE`, `STATE_SLOT_TEMPS * 8`).
+pub const STATE_MIN_SIZE: Int = 16
+
+/// A poll function stopped at an await whose future was not ready
+/// (`POLL_PENDING`).
+pub const POLL_PENDING: Int = 0
+
+/// A poll function wrote its final value to the output slot
+/// (`POLL_READY`).
+pub const POLL_READY: Int = 1
+
+// The state object pointer inside a poll function, its environment
+// parameter, temp 0 (`STATE`).
+const STATE: Int = 0
+
+// The task context pointer inside a poll function, its one real
+// parameter, temp 1 (`TASK_CTX`).
+const TASK_CTX: Int = 1
+
+// The block a resumable poll function enters at, which switches on the
+// resume tag (`DISPATCH`).
+const DISPATCH: Int = 0
+
+// The one block every unrecognized resume tag and poll status goes to,
+// a trap (`BAD_DISCRIMINANT`).
+const BAD_DISCRIMINANT: Int = 1
+
+// How many blocks a resumable poll function reserves ahead of the
+// body's own (`RESERVED_BLOCKS`).
+const RESERVED_BLOCKS: Int = 2
+
+// How many blocks one await expands to beyond the piece before it: the
+// poll, the suspend and the continuation (`BLOCKS_PER_AWAIT`).
+const BLOCKS_PER_AWAIT: Int = 3
+
+// The lowest tag a suspend may store; tag 0 is the entry state
+// (`FIRST_RESUME_TAG`).
+const FIRST_RESUME_TAG: Int = 1
+
+/// Every async function of `program` rewritten into a poll function plus
+/// a wrapper (`transform`): a new module, each poll function in its async
+/// function's place and the wrappers after every function. A function
+/// that is not async is kept as it is, so a module with none comes out
+/// with the same functions.
+pub fn transform(program: MirModule) -> MirModule {
+    let mut functions: Vec<MirFunction> = Vec::new()
+    let mut wrappers: Vec<MirFunction> = Vec::new()
+    for i in 0..program.functions.len() {
+        let f = program.functions.at(i)
+        if !f.is_async {
+            functions.push(f)
+            continue
+        }
+        let (poll, wrapper) = split_into_poll_and_wrapper(f)
+        functions.push(poll)
+        wrappers.push(wrapper)
+    }
+    for i in 0..wrappers.len() { functions.push(wrappers.at(i)) }
+    MirModule { functions: functions, externs: program.externs }
+}
+
+// `f`'s `$poll` function, new, and the wrapper that takes over its old
+// symbol (`split_into_poll_and_wrapper`).
+fn split_into_poll_and_wrapper(f: MirFunction) -> (MirFunction, MirFunction) {
+    // The precondition the output store depends on: a returned temp's
+    // class is the class the body produces (`lower::body_return_ty`).
+    for b in f.blocks.iter() {
+        match b.term {
+            TermReturn(t) => {
+                if t >= 0 && f.temps.at(t) != f.ret {
+                    panic(
+                        "`${f.name}` returns a temp whose class is not its declared return class ${MIR_TY_NAMES[f.ret]}; see `lower::body_return_ty`",
+                    )
+                }
+            }
+            _ => ()
+        }
+    }
+    let wrapper = build_wrapper(f)
+    // Every original temp becomes a state slot, so the rewritten body is
+    // indexed against the old temps, while the poll function's own start
+    // fresh: the state pointer, task_ctx, then a scratch per load and per
+    // result.
+    let mut sp = Spiller { temps: Vec::from_array([MIR_PTR, MIR_PTR]), orig: f.temps }
+    let blocks = sp.body(f.blocks)
+    // Every await must have been consumed by the split.
+    for b in blocks.iter() {
+        for s in b.stmts.iter() {
+            match s {
+                SAwait(_, _) => panic(
+                    "`${f.name}$poll` still holds an await marker after the split",
+                )
+                _ => ()
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    // The two halves of the state object's size must agree: the highest
+    // slot any access names, against the slots the wrapper allocated.
+    let highest = highest_state_slot(blocks, STATE)
+    if highest >= wrapper.state_slots {
+        panic(
+            "`${f.name}$poll` addresses state slot ${highest}, which is past the ${wrapper.state_slots} slots (${wrapper.state_slots * 8} bytes) its wrapper allocated: a slot added to the rewrite must be added to `state_slot_count` in the same edit",
+        )
+    }
+    let seeded = highest_state_slot(wrapper.function.blocks, wrapper.state)
+    if seeded >= wrapper.state_slots {
+        panic(
+            "`${f.name}` seeds state slot ${seeded}, which is past the ${wrapper.state_slots} slots it allocated",
+        )
+    }
+    let poll = MirFunction {
+        name: f.name + "$poll",
+        params: 1,
+        takes_env: true,
+        capture_count: 0,
+        temps: sp.temps,
+        ret: MIR_I64,
+        is_async: false,
+        blocks: blocks,
+    }
+    (poll, wrapper.function)
+}
+
+// The highest state slot any field access in `blocks` addresses through
+// `state`, `STATE_SLOT_TAG` when there is none (`highest_state_slot`).
+fn highest_state_slot(blocks: Vec<MirBlock>, state: Int) -> Int {
+    let mut highest = -1
+    for b in blocks.iter() {
+        for s in b.stmts.iter() {
+            match s {
+                SRecordField(_, rec, index, _) => {
+                    if rec == state && index > highest { highest = index }
+                }
+                SSetField(rec, index, _, _) => {
+                    if rec == state && index > highest { highest = index }
+                }
+                _ => ()
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    if highest < 0 { STATE_SLOT_TAG } else { highest }
+}
+
+// How many 8-byte slots a state object needs for a body with `n_temps`
+// temps (`state_slot_count`), the one place it is decided.
+fn state_slot_count(n_temps: Int) -> Int { STATE_SLOT_TEMPS + n_temps }
+
+// A wrapper, the slot count its allocation used and the temp holding the
+// state pointer (`Wrapper`).
+record Wrapper { function: MirFunction, state_slots: Int, state: Int }
+
+// The function that keeps `f`'s symbol: allocate the state object, seed
+// it, and return the `{ poll_code, state }` future (`build_wrapper`).
+// Reads `f` as lowering left it.
+fn build_wrapper(f: MirFunction) -> Wrapper {
+    // The temps the ABI hands the function, the environment pointer if it
+    // has one, then the real parameters, numbered as `lower_function`
+    // numbered them.
+    let env = if f.takes_env { 1 } else { 0 }
+    let abi_count = f.params + env
+    let mut temps: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+    for i in 0..abi_count { temps.push(f.temps.at(i)) }
+    let mut stmts: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+    // Sized from the temps before the rewrite chooses the slots;
+    // `split_into_poll_and_wrapper` checks the two against each other.
+    let state_slots = state_slot_count(f.temps.len())
+    let bytes = state_slots * 8
+    if bytes < STATE_MIN_SIZE {
+        panic(
+            "a state object must hold at least the tag and output slots, since the executor reads the output slot on completion even for a future with no temps at all",
+        )
+    }
+    let size = push_temp(temps, MIR_I64)
+    stmts.push(SConstInt(size, bytes))
+    let state = push_temp(temps, MIR_PTR)
+    stmts.push(SCallRuntime(state, RT_ALLOC, Vec::from_array([size])))
+    // The resume tag, the entry state.
+    let zero = push_temp(temps, MIR_I64)
+    stmts.push(SConstInt(zero, 0))
+    stmts.push(SSetField(state, STATE_SLOT_TAG, zero, MIR_I64))
+    // The incoming ABI temps, each into its slot, a unit one too.
+    for i in 0..abi_count { stmts.push(SSetField(state, STATE_SLOT_TEMPS + i, i, temps.at(i))) }
+    // The future value: a closure of no captures over the poll function,
+    // its word 1 then the state object itself.
+    let future = push_temp(temps, MIR_PTR)
+    stmts.push(SMakeClosure(future, f.name + "$poll", Vec::new()))
+    stmts.push(SSetField(future, FUTURE_SLOT_STATE, state, MIR_PTR))
+    let mut blocks: Vec<MirBlock> = Vec::new()
+    blocks.push(MirBlock { stmts: stmts, term: TermReturn(future) })
+    Wrapper {
+        function: MirFunction {
+            name: f.name,
+            params: f.params,
+            takes_env: f.takes_env,
+            // The wrapper loads nothing from an environment record.
+            capture_count: 0,
+            temps: temps,
+            ret: MIR_PTR,
+            is_async: false,
+            blocks: blocks,
+        },
+        state_slots: state_slots,
+        state: state,
+    }
+}
+
+// Append a temp of class `ty` to `temps` and give its id, the list's
+// length before (`push_temp`).
+fn push_temp(mut temps: Vec<Int>, ty: Int) -> Int {
+    let t = temps.len()
+    temps.push(ty)
+    t
+}
+
+// Rewrites a pre-transform body into the poll function's (`Spiller`):
+// every value it names moves into the state object, and every suspend
+// point becomes the blocks that poll, suspend and resume.
+record Spiller {
+    // The poll function's temps, one more per load and per result.
+    temps: Vec<Int>,
+    // The pre-transform temps' classes, by original temp, which is also
+    // the temp's state slot past `STATE_SLOT_TEMPS`.
+    orig: Vec<Int>,
+}
+
+// One suspend point lifted out of a block's statements (`AwaitPoint`):
+// where the awaited value goes, -1 when the awaited output is unit, and
+// the pre-transform temp holding the awaited future.
+record AwaitPoint { dst: Int, future: Int }
+
+// A block's statements split at its awaits (`split_at_awaits`): the runs
+// between them, always one more than the awaits, and the awaits, in
+// order.
+fn split_at_awaits(stmts: Vec<MirStmt>) -> (Vec<Vec<MirStmt>>, Vec<AwaitPoint>) {
+    let mut segments: Vec<Vec<MirStmt>> = Vec::new()
+    segments.push(Vec::new())
+    let mut awaits: Vec<AwaitPoint> = Vec::new()
+    for i in 0..stmts.len() {
+        match stmts.at(i) {
+            SAwait(dst, future) => {
+                awaits.push(AwaitPoint { dst: dst, future: future })
+                segments.push(Vec::new())
+            }
+            other => {
+                if segments.is_empty() {
+                    panic("segments starts with one entry and only ever grows")
+                }
+                let mut last = segments.at(segments.len() - 1)
+                last.push(other)
+            }
+        }
+    }
+    (segments, awaits)
+}
+
+// The run of `segments` at `k`, the `k`-th taken (S0's `segments.next()`).
+fn next_segment(segments: Vec<Vec<MirStmt>>, k: Int) -> Vec<MirStmt> {
+    if k >= segments.len() { panic("one segment more than awaits") }
+    segments.at(k)
+}
+
+// How many suspend points `b` holds (`await_count`).
+fn await_count(b: MirBlock) -> Int {
+    let mut n = 0
+    for s in b.stmts.iter() {
+        match s {
+            SAwait(_, _) => { n += 1 }
+            _ => ()
+        }
+    }
+    n
+}
+
+// Whether a statement's operand is consumed or produced (`Role`): a load
+// goes before the statement, a store after it.
+type Role = | RoleRead | RoleWrite
+
+// A block's original terminator, until the piece that ends the block
+// takes it (S0's `Option<Terminator>` taken once).
+record TermSlot { term: Option<MirTerm> }
+
+impl Spiller {
+    fn scratch(mut self, ty: Int) -> Int { push_temp(self.temps, ty) }
+
+    fn orig_ty(self, t: Int) -> Int { self.orig.at(t) }
+
+    // Field `index` of `record` loaded into a fresh scratch temp, one per
+    // load site (`load`).
+    fn load(mut self, mut out: Vec<MirStmt>, rec: Int, index: Int, ty: Int) -> Int {
+        let dst = self.scratch(ty)
+        out.push(SRecordField(dst, rec, index, ty))
+        dst
+    }
+
+    // Original temp `t` loaded out of its state slot into a fresh scratch
+    // temp (`reload`).
+    fn reload(mut self, out: Vec<MirStmt>, t: Int) -> Int {
+        let ty = self.orig_ty(t)
+        self.load(out, STATE, slot(t), ty)
+    }
+
+    // A fresh scratch temp holding the i64 constant `v` (`const_i64`).
+    fn const_i64(mut self, mut out: Vec<MirStmt>, v: Int) -> Int {
+        let t = self.scratch(MIR_I64)
+        out.push(SConstInt(t, v))
+        t
+    }
+
+    // A whole pre-transform body rewritten (`body`): split at its suspend
+    // points, every value it names spilled, and a resumable body given its
+    // entry dispatch. An await-free body reserves no block, so its blocks
+    // keep their ids.
+    fn body(mut self, orig: Vec<MirBlock>) -> Vec<MirBlock> {
+        let mut per_block: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+        let mut total = 0
+        for i in 0..orig.len() {
+            let a = await_count(orig.at(i))
+            per_block.push(a)
+            total += a
+        }
+        let reserved = if total == 0 { 0 } else { RESERVED_BLOCKS }
+        // Where each original block's leading piece lands, decided before
+        // anything is emitted: a terminator can name a block not rewritten
+        // yet.
+        let mut next = reserved
+        let mut starts: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..per_block.len() {
+            starts.push(next)
+            next += 1 + BLOCKS_PER_AWAIT * per_block.at(i)
+        }
+        let mut out: Vec<MirBlock> = Vec::new()
+        // Placeholders: `BAD_DISCRIMINANT` is already what it needs to be,
+        // `DISPATCH` is overwritten below.
+        for _ in 0..reserved { out.push(MirBlock { stmts: Vec::new(), term: TermTrap }) }
+        let mut resume: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..orig.len() {
+            if out.len() != starts.at(i) {
+                panic(
+                    "block ${i}'s pieces must land at the id reserved for them, or every terminator naming it is off by however far the two drifted",
+                )
+            }
+            self.block_pieces(orig.at(i), starts, out, resume)
+        }
+        if out.len() != next { panic("the emitted block count must match the ids reserved for it") }
+        if total > 0 {
+            if resume.len() != total { panic("one resume point per await, in tag order") }
+            let mut stmts: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+            let tag = self.load(stmts, STATE, STATE_SLOT_TAG, MIR_I64)
+            let mut arms: Vec<(Int, Int)> = Vec::new()
+            arms.push((0, starts.at(0)))
+            for k in 0..resume.len() { arms.push((FIRST_RESUME_TAG + k, resume.at(k))) }
+            out.set(
+                DISPATCH,
+                MirBlock { stmts: stmts, term: TermSwitch(tag, arms, BAD_DISCRIMINANT) },
+            )
+        }
+        out
+    }
+
+    // One original block's pieces (`block_pieces`): the statements before
+    // its first await, then a poll, suspend and continuation per await,
+    // each await's poll block appended to `resume`, in tag order.
+    fn block_pieces(
+        mut self,
+        b: MirBlock,
+        starts: Vec<Int>,
+        mut out: Vec<MirBlock>,
+        mut resume: Vec<Int>,
+    ) {
+        let base = out.len()
+        let (segments, awaits) = split_at_awaits(b.stmts)
+        let n = awaits.len()
+        // Whichever piece is last takes the block's original terminator,
+        // and exactly one does.
+        let orig_term = TermSlot { term: Some(b.term) }
+        // The leading piece, which a resume skips.
+        let mut lead: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+        self.spill(next_segment(segments, 0), lead)
+        if n == 0 {
+            let term = self.finish(orig_term, lead, starts)
+            out.push(MirBlock { stmts: lead, term: term })
+            return
+        }
+        out.push(MirBlock { stmts: lead, term: TermGoto(base + 1) })
+        for k in 0..n {
+            let aw = awaits.at(k)
+            let poll = base + 1 + BLOCKS_PER_AWAIT * k
+            let suspend = poll + 1
+            let cont = poll + 2
+            let tag = FIRST_RESUME_TAG + resume.len()
+            resume.push(poll)
+            // The poll, entered by falling out of the code before the
+            // await and by a resume at `tag`: the poll alone.
+            if out.len() != poll { panic("the poll block's own id") }
+            let mut polls: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+            let future = self.reload(polls, aw.future)
+            let status = self.scratch(MIR_I64)
+            polls.push(
+                SCallIndirect(
+                    status,
+                    future,
+                    Vec::from_array([MIR_PTR]),
+                    MIR_I64,
+                    Vec::from_array([TASK_CTX]),
+                ),
+            )
+            // Exactly the two statuses the ABI defines, and a trap for the
+            // rest.
+            let statuses = Vec::from_array([(POLL_READY, cont), (POLL_PENDING, suspend)])
+            out.push(
+                MirBlock { stmts: polls, term: TermSwitch(status, statuses, BAD_DISCRIMINANT) },
+            )
+            // The suspend stores this await's own tag, so the next poll
+            // polls the same future again.
+            let mut suspends: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+            let t = self.const_i64(suspends, tag)
+            suspends.push(SSetField(STATE, STATE_SLOT_TAG, t, MIR_I64))
+            let pending = self.const_i64(suspends, POLL_PENDING)
+            out.push(MirBlock { stmts: suspends, term: TermReturn(pending) })
+            // The continuation, reached only from the ready arm: the
+            // awaited value, the inner state object's output slot, copied
+            // into the await result's slot before the statements that
+            // followed the await; a unit output has nothing to copy.
+            let mut conts: Vec<MirStmt> = Vec::new()
+            if aw.dst >= 0 && self.orig_ty(aw.dst) != MIR_UNIT {
+                let ty = self.orig_ty(aw.dst)
+                let fut = self.reload(conts, aw.future)
+                let inner = self.load(conts, fut, FUTURE_SLOT_STATE, MIR_PTR)
+                let value = self.load(conts, inner, STATE_SLOT_OUTPUT, ty)
+                conts.push(SSetField(STATE, slot(aw.dst), value, ty))
+            }
+            self.spill(next_segment(segments, k + 1), conts)
+            let term = if k + 1 < n {
+                // The next await's poll block, the very next one emitted.
+                TermGoto(cont + 1)
+            } else {
+                self.finish(orig_term, conts, starts)
+            }
+            out.push(MirBlock { stmts: conts, term: term })
+        }
+    }
+
+    // The original terminator taken and rewritten (`finish`); a second
+    // piece asking for it is a bug.
+    fn finish(mut self, mut orig_term: TermSlot, out: Vec<MirStmt>, starts: Vec<Int>) -> MirTerm {
+        let term = match orig_term.term {
+            Some(t) => t
+            None => panic("exactly one piece of a block takes its original terminator")
+        }
+        orig_term.term = None
+        self.terminator(term, out, starts)
+    }
+
+    // Every value one straight-line run of statements names moved into
+    // the state object, the rewritten statements appended to `out`
+    // (`spill`): each statement's loads, the statement, its stores.
+    fn spill(mut self, stmts: Vec<MirStmt>, mut out: Vec<MirStmt>) {
+        for i in 0..stmts.len() {
+            let v = SpillVisit { sp: self, loads: Vec::new(), stores: Vec::new() }
+            let s = visit_temps(stmts.at(i), v)
+            for l in v.loads.iter() { out.push(l) }
+            out.push(s)
+            for st in v.stores.iter() { out.push(st) }
+        }
+    }
+
+    // One terminator rewritten (`terminator`): whatever it reads reloaded,
+    // the blocks it names renumbered through `starts`, a `Return` turned
+    // into a completion that stores the output and returns the ready
+    // status.
+    fn terminator(mut self, term: MirTerm, mut out: Vec<MirStmt>, starts: Vec<Int>) -> MirTerm {
+        match term {
+            TermGoto(target) => TermGoto(starts.at(target))
+            TermBranch(cond, then_block, else_block) => {
+                let c = self.reload(out, cond)
+                TermBranch(c, starts.at(then_block), starts.at(else_block))
+            }
+            TermSwitch(disc, arms, default) => {
+                let d = self.reload(out, disc)
+                let mut mapped: Vec<(Int, Int)> = Vec::new()
+                for (v, b) in arms.iter() { mapped.push((v, starts.at(b))) }
+                TermSwitch(d, mapped, starts.at(default))
+            }
+            TermReturn(value) => {
+                if value >= 0 && self.orig_ty(value) != MIR_UNIT {
+                    let ty = self.orig_ty(value)
+                    let v = self.reload(out, value)
+                    out.push(SSetField(STATE, STATE_SLOT_OUTPUT, v, ty))
+                } else {
+                    // A unit output has no value, but the executor reads
+                    // the output slot regardless: an explicit zero.
+                    let z = self.const_i64(out, 0)
+                    out.push(SSetField(STATE, STATE_SLOT_OUTPUT, z, MIR_I64))
+                }
+                let ready = self.const_i64(out, POLL_READY)
+                TermReturn(ready)
+            }
+            // A trap aborts rather than unwinding.
+            TermTrap => TermTrap
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+// Original temp `t`'s state slot (`Spiller::slot`).
+fn slot(t: Int) -> Int { STATE_SLOT_TEMPS + t }
+
+// What `spill`'s callback holds for one statement (S0's closure): the
+// spiller, and the loads and stores that go before and after it.
+record SpillVisit { sp: Spiller, loads: Vec<MirStmt>, stores: Vec<MirStmt> }
+
+impl SpillVisit {
+    // Temp `t`, read or written, as the rewritten statement names it: a
+    // read is reloaded from its slot into a scratch temp before the
+    // statement; a write goes to a scratch temp stored into its slot
+    // after.
+    fn visit(mut self, t: Int, role: Role) -> Int {
+        match role {
+            RoleRead => self.sp.reload(self.loads, t)
+            RoleWrite => {
+                let ty = self.sp.orig_ty(t)
+                let dst = self.sp.scratch(ty)
+                self.stores.push(SSetField(STATE, slot(t), dst, ty))
+                dst
+            }
+        }
+    }
+
+    // Each of `ts` read, in order.
+    fn reads(mut self, ts: Vec<Int>) -> Vec<Int> {
+        let mut out: Vec<Int> = Vec::new()
+        for i in 0..ts.len() { out.push(self.visit(ts.at(i), RoleRead)) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    // Each temp of `pairs` read, in order, its class kept.
+    fn read_pairs(mut self, pairs: Vec<(Int, Int)>) -> Vec<(Int, Int)> {
+        let mut out: Vec<(Int, Int)> = Vec::new()
+        for (t, class) in pairs.iter() { out.push((self.visit(t, RoleRead), class)) }
+        out
+    }
+
+    // An optional destination written, -1 kept.
+    fn write_opt(mut self, dst: Int) -> Int {
+        if dst < 0 { -1 } else { self.visit(dst, RoleWrite) }
+    }
+}
+
+// `stmt` with every temp it mentions given to `v`, read or written, and
+// replaced by what `v` gives (`visit_temps`), in S0's order: a new
+// statement. Exhaustive, as S0's: each variant decides which of its temps
+// are operands and which results.
+fn visit_temps(stmt: MirStmt, mut v: SpillVisit) -> MirStmt {
+    match stmt {
+        SConstInt(dst, value) => SConstInt(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), value)
+        SConstFloat(dst, value) => SConstFloat(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), value)
+        SConstBool(dst, value) => SConstBool(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), value)
+        SConstStr(dst, value) => SConstStr(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), value)
+        SConstUnit(dst) => SConstUnit(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite))
+        SCopy(dst, src) => {
+            let s = v.visit(src, RoleRead)
+            SCopy(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), s)
+        }
+        SBin(dst, op, class, lhs, rhs) => {
+            let l = v.visit(lhs, RoleRead)
+            let r = v.visit(rhs, RoleRead)
+            SBin(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), op, class, l, r)
+        }
+        SNeg(dst, class, src) => {
+            let s = v.visit(src, RoleRead)
+            SNeg(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), class, s)
+        }
+        SNot(dst, src) => {
+            let s = v.visit(src, RoleRead)
+            SNot(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), s)
+        }
+        SBitNot(dst, src) => {
+            let s = v.visit(src, RoleRead)
+            SBitNot(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), s)
+        }
+        SCall(dst, callee, args) => {
+            let a = v.reads(args)
+            SCall(v.write_opt(dst), callee, a)
+        }
+        SCallRuntime(dst, func, args) => {
+            let a = v.reads(args)
+            SCallRuntime(v.write_opt(dst), func, a)
+        }
+        SCallIndirect(dst, callee, params, ret, args) => {
+            let c = v.visit(callee, RoleRead)
+            let a = v.reads(args)
+            SCallIndirect(v.write_opt(dst), c, params, ret, a)
+        }
+        // Not reached: `block_pieces` takes every await out before the
+        // spill; classified as `SCallIndirect` is, as S0's is.
+        SAwait(dst, future) => {
+            let f = v.visit(future, RoleRead)
+            SAwait(v.write_opt(dst), f)
+        }
+        SMakeClosure(dst, code, captures) => {
+            let c = v.read_pairs(captures)
+            SMakeClosure(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), code, c)
+        }
+        SMakeSum(dst, tag, fields) => {
+            let f = v.read_pairs(fields)
+            SMakeSum(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), tag, f)
+        }
+        SMakeRecord(dst, fields) => {
+            let f = v.read_pairs(fields)
+            SMakeRecord(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), f)
+        }
+        SSumTag(dst, sum) => {
+            let s = v.visit(sum, RoleRead)
+            SSumTag(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), s)
+        }
+        SSumField(dst, sum, index, ty) => {
+            let s = v.visit(sum, RoleRead)
+            SSumField(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), s, index, ty)
+        }
+        SRecordField(dst, rec, index, ty) => {
+            let r = v.visit(rec, RoleRead)
+            SRecordField(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), r, index, ty)
+        }
+        SSetField(rec, index, value, ty) => {
+            let r = v.visit(rec, RoleRead)
+            let val = v.visit(value, RoleRead)
+            SSetField(r, index, val, ty)
+        }
+        SMakeArray(dst, elems) => {
+            let e = v.read_pairs(elems)
+            SMakeArray(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), e)
+        }
+        SArrayAlloc(dst, len) => {
+            let l = v.visit(len, RoleRead)
+            SArrayAlloc(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), l)
+        }
+        SArrayLen(dst, arr) => {
+            let a = v.visit(arr, RoleRead)
+            SArrayLen(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), a)
+        }
+        SArrayGet(dst, arr, index, ty) => {
+            let a = v.visit(arr, RoleRead)
+            let i = v.visit(index, RoleRead)
+            SArrayGet(v.visit(dst, RoleWrite), a, i, ty)
+        }
+        SArraySet(arr, index, value, ty) => {
+            let a = v.visit(arr, RoleRead)
+            let i = v.visit(index, RoleRead)
+            let val = v.visit(value, RoleRead)
+            SArraySet(a, i, val, ty)
+        }
+    }
+}

 }
+
+/// The loose corpus's MIR dump in both forms ([`agree_on_mir_forms`]),
+/// compared, once a run: the corpus test's, and the MIR fuzzer's bases are
+/// read from it.
+fn loose_mir() -> &'static (String, String) {
+    static DUMP: OnceLock<(String, String)> = OnceLock::new();
+    DUMP.get_or_init(|| agree_on_mir_forms(&root(), &loose_corpus(), 0))
+}
+
+/// The MIR fuzzer's seeds (5.4, spec §7.4): its own, past the body
+/// fuzzer's.
+const MIR_SEEDS: [u64; 3] = [601, 602, 603];
+const MIR_COUNT: usize = 40;
+
+/// How many of the fixed seeds' inputs must hold each kind's mark (spec
+/// 5.4 §7.4).
+const MIR_FLOOR: usize = 10;
+
+/// The loose corpus programs the MIR fuzzer starts from (spec 5.4 §7.4):
+/// those that import no module and lower clean, `lowered=true` with no
+/// diagnostic. Read from the loose corpus's MIR dump ([`loose_mir`]), the
+/// same on every system, as the type fuzzer reads its bases from the HIR
+/// dump ([`type_bases`]).
+fn mir_bases() -> Vec<String> {
+    let root = root();
+    let candidates: Vec<String> = loose_corpus()
+        .into_iter()
+        .filter(|f| {
+            let text = std::fs::read_to_string(root.join(f)).expect("read a corpus file");
+            !text.lines().any(|l| l.starts_with("import "))
+                && fuzz::parses(&text.replace("\r\n", "\n"))
+        })
+        .collect();
+    parts_by_root(&loose_mir().0)
+        .into_iter()
+        .filter(|(root, part)| {
+            candidates.contains(root)
+                && part.starts_with("(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true ")
+                && part.ends_with("\n(Diagnostics)\n")
+        })
+        .map(|(root, _)| root)
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// `count` programs from `seed` given MIR additions ([`fuzz::mir_mutate`]),
+/// each one of `bases` that the seed picks, read with each `\r\n` as `\n`,
+/// so a seed gives the same inputs on every system.
+fn mir_inputs(bases: &[String], seed: u64, count: usize) -> Vec<String> {
+    let root = root();
+    let mut bases = bases.to_vec();
+    bases.sort();
+    let mut rng = SplitMix64::new(seed);
+    (0..count)
+        .map(|i| {
+            let file = rng.pick(&bases);
+            let source = std::fs::read_to_string(root.join(file))
+                .expect("read a corpus file")
+                .replace("\r\n", "\n");
+            fuzz::mir_mutate(&mut rng, &source, i)
+        })
+        .collect()
+}
+
+/// Which of [`fuzz::MIR_KINDS`]' marks a root's part of the MIR dump holds
+/// in a function an addition of that kind declared ([`fuzz::mir_kind_of`]):
+/// an instance's symbol, with type arguments; a poll function; a `Switch`;
+/// a `MakeClosure` and a `CallIndirect` (spec 5.4 §7.4).
+fn mir_marks(part: &str) -> [bool; 4] {
+    let mut kind = None;
+    let (mut instance, mut poll, mut switch, mut make, mut call) =
+        (false, false, false, false, false);
+    for line in part.lines() {
+        let line = line.trim_start();
+        if let Some(rest) = line.strip_prefix("(Function \"") {
+            let name = rest.split('"').next().unwrap_or("");
+            kind = fuzz::mir_kind_of(name);
+            instance |= kind == Some('g') && name.contains('$');
+            poll |= kind == Some('a') && name.ends_with("$poll");
+            continue;
+        }
+        switch |= kind == Some('p') && line.starts_with("(Switch ");
+        make |= kind == Some('c') && line.starts_with("(MakeClosure ");
+        call |= kind == Some('c') && line.starts_with("(CallIndirect ");
+    }
+    [instance, poll, switch, make && call]
+}
+
+/// The MIR fuzzer (5.4, spec §7.4, ADR 0057): each seed's inputs, written
+/// to `mir-fuzz/<seed>/` and dumped in one process a side, compared in the
+/// after form and, where a poll function is made, in the before form
+/// ([`agree_on_mir_forms`]); every input lowers clean, none skipped; and
+/// each kind's mark ([`mir_marks`]) occurs in at least [`MIR_FLOOR`] of the
+/// inputs.
+#[test]
+fn the_mir_fuzzers_inputs_agree_and_hold_each_kind() {
+    let bases = mir_bases();
+    // 183 of the loose corpus at 5.4.
+    assert!(bases.len() >= 100, "only {} bases", bases.len());
+    let dir = fresh_dir("mir-fuzz");
+    let mut roots = Vec::new();
+    for seed in MIR_SEEDS {
+        let sub = dir.join(seed.to_string());
+        std::fs::create_dir_all(&sub).expect("create a seed's directory");
+        for (i, input) in mir_inputs(&bases, seed, MIR_COUNT).iter().enumerate() {
+            let file = format!("f{i:05}.nova");
+            std::fs::write(sub.join(&file), input).expect("write a MIR-fuzz input");
+            roots.push(format!("{seed}/{file}"));
+        }
+    }
+    let (after, _) = agree_on_mir_forms(&dir, &roots, 0);
+    let parts = parts_by_root(&after);
+    assert_eq!(parts.len(), roots.len());
+    let unclean: Vec<&str> = parts
+        .iter()
+        .filter(|(_, part)| {
+            !part.starts_with("(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=true ")
+                || !part.ends_with("\n(Diagnostics)\n")
+        })
+        .map(|(root, _)| root.as_str())
+        .collect();
+    assert!(
+        unclean.is_empty(),
+        "inputs that do not lower clean: {unclean:?}"
+    );
+    let mut counts = [[0usize; 4]; MIR_SEEDS.len()];
+    let mut polls = 0;
+    for (root, part) in &parts {
+        let seed = MIR_SEEDS
+            .iter()
+            .position(|s| root.starts_with(&format!("{s}/")))
+            .expect("a seed's input");
+        for (count, held) in counts[seed].iter_mut().zip(mir_marks(part)) {
+            *count += usize::from(held);
+        }
+        polls += usize::from(holds_a_poll_function(part));
+    }
+    let total: Vec<usize> = (0..4).map(|k| counts.iter().map(|c| c[k]).sum()).collect();
+    let short: Vec<(&str, usize)> = fuzz::MIR_KINDS
+        .iter()
+        .zip(&total)
+        .filter(|(_, n)| **n < MIR_FLOOR)
+        .map(|((_, kind), n)| (*kind, *n))
+        .collect();
+    assert!(
+        short.is_empty(),
+        "kinds under {MIR_FLOOR} inputs: {short:?}"
+    );
+    for (seed, count) in MIR_SEEDS.iter().zip(&counts) {
+        eprintln!(
+            "the MIR fuzzer's seed {seed}: inputs holding each kind {:?}",
+            fuzz::MIR_KINDS
+                .iter()
+                .zip(count)
+                .map(|((_, kind), n)| format!("{kind} {n}"))
+                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
+        );
+    }
+    eprintln!(
+        "the MIR fuzzer over seeds {MIR_SEEDS:?}: {} inputs from {} bases, each kind {total:?}; \
+         {polls} compared in the before form; the after dump {} lines, {} bytes",
+        roots.len(),
+        bases.len(),
+        after.lines().count(),
+        after.len()
+    );
+}
+
+/// N's MIR on its own stack (5.4, spec §7.5, ADR 0057): N's `selfhost mir`
+/// on a main thread of 1 MB, as
+/// [`n_checks_128_levels_of_every_kind_on_a_1_mb_main_thread`] runs N's
+/// type checker, its dump S0's, which S0 makes on its 64 MB thread. The
+/// type checker's deep bodies, every kind of [`fuzz::BODY_DEEP_KINDS`] at
+/// the deepest level the parser takes, each in a function `main` calls and
+/// in a generic one `main` instantiates ([`fuzz::mir_deep`]), so that
+/// lowering and the specializer both walk it; and every kind of
+/// [`fuzz::MIR_DEEP_TYPES`], 128 levels deep, as an instance's type
+/// argument ([`fuzz::mir_deep_type`]), so that `mangle_ty` and the
+/// substitution walk it. Each lowers clean; none makes a poll function, so
+/// the after form is the only one to compare.
+#[test]
+fn n_lowers_the_depth_cases_on_a_1_mb_main_thread() {
+    let dir = fresh_dir("deep-n-mir");
+    let mut files = Vec::new();
+    for (kind, depth) in fuzz::BODY_DEEP_KINDS {
+        let file = format!("bodies-{kind}-{depth}.nova");
+        std::fs::write(dir.join(&file), fuzz::mir_deep(kind, depth)).expect("write a deep input");
+        files.push(file);
+    }
+    for (kind, _, _) in fuzz::MIR_DEEP_TYPES {
+        let file = format!("types-{kind}-128.nova");
+        std::fs::write(dir.join(&file), fuzz::mir_deep_type(kind, 128))
+            .expect("write a deep input");
+        files.push(file);
+    }
+    let s0 = run(&nova(), MIR_S0, &dir, &files).expect("run S0");
+    assert_eq!(s0.code, Some(0), "{}", s0.stderr);
+    let text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&s0.stdout);
+    let parts = parts_by_root(&text);
+    assert_eq!(parts.len(), files.len());
+    for (root, part) in &parts {
+        assert!(
+            part.starts_with("(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true ")
+                && part.ends_with("\n(Diagnostics)\n")
+                && !holds_a_poll_function(part),
+            "{root}: {part}"
+        );
+        // Walked twice, as `f` and as `g`'s instance at `Int`; or `id`'s
+        // instance at the deep type.
+        let walked = if root.starts_with("bodies-") {
+            part.contains("\n  (Function \"f.")
+                && part
+                    .lines()
+                    .any(|l| l.starts_with("  (Function \"g.") && l.contains("$i\" "))
+        } else {
+            part.lines()
+                .any(|l| l.starts_with("  (Function \"id.") && l.len() > 256)
+        };
+        assert!(walked, "{root}: {part}");
+    }
+    let std_dir = std_dir();
+    let mut args = MIR_N.to_vec();
+    args.extend(["--std", std_dir.as_str()]);
+    args.extend(files.iter().map(String::as_str));
+    let stack = if cfg!(unix) { Some(1024) } else { None };
+    let (code, n) = n_args_on_stack(&dir, stack, &args);
+    assert_eq!(code, Some(0), "N's `selfhost mir` on a {stack:?} KB stack");
+    if let Some(m) = first_difference(&s0.stdout, &n) {
+        panic!(
+            "{}",
+            report(&m, mir_in(MirForm::After), &dir, &nova(), selfhost())
+        );
+    }
+    // The stack is the one set: `bodies-for-ranges-127.nova`, whose least
+    // stack is about 212 KB (ADR 0057 §17), fails on 96 KB.
+    if cfg!(unix) {
+        let mut args = MIR_N.to_vec();
+        args.extend(["--std", std_dir.as_str(), "bodies-for-ranges-127.nova"]);
+        let (code, _) = n_args_on_stack(&dir, Some(96), &args);
+        assert_ne!(code, Some(0), "N's `selfhost mir` passed on a 96 KB stack");
+    }
+}

+// A pattern as text, each variant's sum and index.
+fn pattern_text(p: HirPattern) -> String {
+    match p {
+        PatVariant(sum, variant, fields) => "V${sum}.${variant}(${patterns_text(fields)})"
+        PatTuple(ps) => "T(${patterns_text(ps)})"
+        PatOr(ps) => "O(${patterns_text(ps)})"
+        _ => "_"
+    }
+}
+
+fn patterns_text(ps: Vec<HirPattern>) -> String {
+    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for q in ps.iter() { parts.push(pattern_text(q)) }
+    ",".join(parts.to_array())
+}
+

     let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
     for t in list.iter() { parts.push(ty_text(t)) }

 fn push_expr_types(mut out: StringBuilder, e: HirExpr) {
     out.push(" ${ty_text(e.ty)}")
+    // The ids a node names beyond its type, which the splice moves.
+    match e.kind {
+        EkCall(func, _, _) => match func {
+            CalleeDef(id) => out.push(" call${id}")
+            _ => ()
+        }
+        EkMakeClosure(func, _, _) => out.push(" closure${func}")
+        EkMakeVariant(sum, variant, _) => out.push(" variant${sum}.${variant}")
+        EkMakeRecord(rec, _) => out.push(" record${rec}")
+        EkTraitCall(trait_id, method, _, _, _, _) => out.push(" trait${trait_id}.${method}")
+        EkMatch(_, arms) => { for a in arms.iter() { out.push(" ${pattern_text(a.pattern)}") } }
+        _ => ()
+    }
     for child in child_exprs(e).iter() { push_expr_types(out, child) }

+// The program `source` checked in full, its bodies too, against a std of
+// one module, `$std.zz`, whose text is `std_text`, as the full check
+// resolves std again for each program.
+fn full_check_with(std_text: String, source: String) -> Checker {
+    let zz = std_module("$std.zz", std_label("$std.zz"), std_text)
+    let std_set = StdSet {
+        modules: Vec::from_array([zz]),
+        natives: Vec::new(),
+        builtins: builtin_table(),
+    }
+    let (tokens, _) = lex(source)
+    let (file, errors) = parse(tokens)
+    if !errors.is_empty() { panic("a test source does not parse") }
+    let mut m = module_source("main", "main", file)
+    m.text = Some(source)
+    let r = resolve_program_for(CompileTargetNative, Vec::from_array([m]), std_set)
+    if !r.diagnostics.is_empty() {
+        panic("a test source does not resolve: ${r.diagnostics.at(0).message}")
+    }
+    let mut texts: Vec<Option<String>> = Vec::new()
+    texts.push(Some(source))
+    texts.push(None)
+    let mut c = check_items(r.items, r.definitions, Vec::from_array(["main", zz.label]), texts)
+    c.check_bodies(r.newline_splits)
+    c
+}

+// a closure, a wrapper, a generic function bounded by the trait and a
+// constant.
+const SPLICE_STD: String = "pub record Zr { n: Int }\npub type Zs = | Za(Int) | Zb\npub trait Zt {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int\n}\nimpl Zt for Zr {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int { self.n }\n}\npub fn zz(x: Int) -> Int {\n    let f = |y: Int| y + 1\n    f(x)\n}\npub fn zw(s: Zs) -> Int {\n    let h = zz\n    match s {\n        Za(n) => h(n)\n        Zb => Zr { n: 2 }.zt()\n    }\n}\npub fn zg<T: Zt>(x: T) -> Int { x.zt() }\npub fn zmake(n: Int) -> Zs { if n > 0 { Za(n) } else { Zb } }\npub const ZC: Int = 3\n"
+
+@test
+fn a_spliced_module_is_the_full_checks_module() {
+    // Programs with no closure, one, and four with a wrapper, a method and
+    // a constant, so that the program's definitions and closures, `D` and
+    // `P`, both vary (S0's `shared_module_is_the_full_checks_module`).
+    let sources = [
+        "fn main() {\n    let a = zz(1) + ZC\n}\n",
+        "fn main() {\n    let f = |x: Int| x * 2\n    let a = f(zw(Zb))\n}\n",
+        "record Mine { x: Int }\nimpl Mine {\n    fn m(self) -> Int {\n        let g = |y: Int| y + self.x\n        g(1)\n    }\n}\nimpl Zt for Mine {\n    fn zt(self) -> Int { self.x }\n}\nconst K: Int = 4\nfn twice(f: fn(Int) -> Int, x: Int) -> Int { f(f(x)) }\nfn main() {\n    let a = |x: Int| x + K\n    let b = |x: Int| x * 2\n    let c = zz\n    let s = Za(1)\n    let r = twice(a, 1) + twice(b, 2) + c(3) + zg(Mine { x: 1 }) + Mine { x: 2 }.m() + zw(s) + zw(zmake(2))\n}\n",
+    ]
+    let mut lifted: Vec<String> = Vec::new()
+    for source in sources.iter() {
+        let (items, c) = shared_std_and_program_with(SPLICE_STD, source)
+        assert(c.diagnostics.is_empty(), "the program checks on the shared path")
+        lifted.push("${c.std_closures - c.closures}")
+        let full = full_check_with(SPLICE_STD, source)
+        assert(full.diagnostics.is_empty(), "the program checks in full")
+        assert_eq(module_text(shared_module(c, items)), module_text(full_module(full)))
+    }
+    assert_eq(" ".join(lifted.to_array()), "0 1 4")
+}
+
 // --- The async transform's state slots ---
 
 // Each block's accesses to the state object `state`, in order: `R<slot>`
````

 //!    binding (a `let mut` made `let`), a missing method, `break` outside a
 //!    loop, `.await` outside an `async fn`, an annotation removed, and

+//! 7. [`mir_mutate`] (5.4, spec

@@ -1644,3 +1652,435 @@ pub fn body_deep(kind: &str, depth: usize) -> String {
         other => panic!("no kind of nesting {other}"),
     }
 }
+
+// --- 5.4: the MIR fuzzer (spec
+// `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md` §7.4; ADR
+// 0057) and the depth cases made to lower (§7.5) ---
+
+/// The MIR fuzzer's four kinds of addition (spec 5.4 §7.4), each by the
+/// letter that ends its prefix, `__m<n>_<k>` and the letter, which starts
+/// the name of every item it declares, and so of every function MIR makes
+/// of them but closures and wrappers: generic instances, awaits, match
+/// shapes and closures.
+pub const MIR_KINDS: [(char, &str); 4] = [
+    ('g', "generic instances"),
+    ('a', "awaits"),
+    ('p', "match shapes"),
+    ('c', "closures"),
+];
+
+/// The record and the sum of its own every addition that draws a type
+/// declares (`{p}` its prefix), which [`mir_type`] may draw.
+const MIR_OWN: &str = "record {p}Rec { {p}f: Int }\n\ntype {p}Sum = | {p}No | {p}Yes(Int)";
+
+/// A type the seed draws, and a value of it to bind under it: a primitive,
+/// the addition's own record or sum ([`MIR_OWN`]), or, while `depth` lasts,
+/// a tuple, an array, an `Option`, a `Result` or a `Vec` of drawn types.
+/// `{p}` stands for the addition's prefix.
+fn mir_type(rng: &mut SplitMix64, depth: usize) -> (String, String) {
+    let pair = |s: &str, v: &str| (s.to_string(), v.to_string());
+    match rng.below(if depth == 0 { 7 } else { 12 }) {
+        0 => ("Int".to_string(), rng.below(100).to_string()),
+        1 => pair("Float", "2.5"),
+        2 => pair("Bool", rng.pick(&["true", "false"])),
+        3 => pair("Char", "'q'"),
+        4 => pair("String", "\"m\""),
+        5 => pair("{p}Rec", "{p}Rec { {p}f: 1 }"),
+        6 => pair("{p}Sum", rng.pick(&["{p}No", "{p}Yes(2)"])),
+        7 => {
+            let (a, x) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            let (b, y) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            (format!("({a}, {b})"), format!("({x}, {y})"))
+        }
+        8 => {
+            let (a, x) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            let value = if rng.one_in(3) {
+                "[]".to_string()
+            } else {
+                format!("[{x}, {x}]")
+            };
+            (format!("[{a}]"), value)
+        }
+        9 => {
+            let (a, x) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            let value = if rng.one_in(3) {
+                "None".to_string()
+            } else {
+                format!("Some({x})")
+            };
+            (format!("Option<{a}>"), value)
+        }
+        10 => {
+            let (a, x) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            let (b, y) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            let value = if rng.one_in(2) {
+                format!("Ok({x})")
+            } else {
+                format!("Err({y})")
+            };
+            (format!("Result<{a}, {b}>"), value)
+        }
+        _ => {
+            let (a, _) = mir_type(rng, depth - 1);
+            (format!("Vec<{a}>"), "Vec::new()".to_string())
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+/// A function `{p}go` whose body is `lines`, each a statement.
+fn mir_go(lines: &[String]) -> String {
+    let mut text = String::from("fn {p}go() {\n");
+    for line in lines {
+        text.push_str("    ");
+        text.push_str(line);
+        text.push('\n');
+    }
+    text.push('}');
+    text
+}
+
+/// Generic instances: a generic function always, and as the seed picks a
+/// generic record with a method, a generic sum with a method, a function
+/// of two type parameters, and a function bounded by a trait of its own
+/// that a generic record and a record of its own implement; each at two
+/// types the seed draws.
+fn mir_generic(rng: &mut SplitMix64) -> String {
+    let (t1, v1) = mir_type(rng, 2);
+    let (t2, v2) = mir_type(rng, 2);
+    let mut items = vec![
+        MIR_OWN.to_string(),
+        "fn {p}id<T>(x: T) -> T {\n    let y: T = x\n    y\n}".to_string(),
+    ];
+    let mut lines = vec![
+        format!("let a: {t1} = {v1}"),
+        format!("let b: {t2} = {v2}"),
+        "let c = {p}id(a)".to_string(),
+    ];
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "record {p}Box<T> { {p}v: T }\n\nimpl<T> {p}Box<T> {\n    fn {p}get(self) -> T { self.{p}v }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("let d = {p}Box { {p}v: b }.{p}get()".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "type {p}Two<A, B> = | {p}L(A) | {p}R(B)\n\nimpl<A, B> {p}Two<A, B> {\n    fn {p}left(self) -> Bool {\n        match self {\n            {p}L(_) => true\n            {p}R(_) => false\n        }\n    }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push(format!("let e: {{p}}Two<{t1}, {t2}> = {{p}}R(b)"));
+        lines.push("let f = e.{p}left()".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push("fn {p}swap<A, B>(a: A, b: B) -> (B, A) { (b, a) }".to_string());
+        lines.push("let g = {p}swap(a, b)".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "trait {p}Tr {\n    fn {p}m(self) -> Int\n}\n\nrecord {p}Own<T> { {p}k: Int, {p}t: T }\n\nimpl<T> {p}Tr for {p}Own<T> {\n    fn {p}m(self) -> Int { self.{p}k }\n}\n\nimpl {p}Tr for {p}Rec {\n    fn {p}m(self) -> Int { self.{p}f }\n}\n\nfn {p}bound<T: {p}Tr>(x: T) -> Int { x.{p}m() }"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("let h = {p}bound({p}Own { {p}k: 1, {p}t: a })".to_string());
+        lines.push("let i = {p}bound({p}Rec { {p}f: 2 })".to_string());
+        lines.push("println(\"${h} ${i}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    items.push(mir_go(&lines));
+    items.join("\n\n")
+}
+
+/// Awaits: an `async fn` that awaits another one to three times, a generic
+/// one as the seed picks, with values of each class and of two drawn types
+/// live across each await, driven by std's `block_on` from a plain
+/// function that `main` calls, through a third as the seed picks.
+fn mir_await(rng: &mut SplitMix64) -> String {
+    let (t1, v1) = mir_type(rng, 1);
+    let (t2, v2) = mir_type(rng, 1);
+    let generic = rng.one_in(2);
+    let leaf = if generic {
+        "async fn {p}leaf<T>(x: T) -> T {\n    yield_now().await\n    x\n}"
+    } else {
+        "async fn {p}leaf(x: Int) -> Int {\n    yield_now().await\n    x + 1\n}"
+    };
+    let mut mid = format!(
+        "async fn {{p}}mid(x: {t1}, y: {t2}) -> Int {{\n    let k = x\n    let n = 3\n    let fl = 1.5\n    let on = n > 1\n"
+    );
+    for j in 0..1 + rng.below(3) {
+        let arg = if generic {
+            *rng.pick(&["y", "k", "n", "fl", "on"])
+        } else {
+            "n"
+        };
+        mid.push_str(&format!("    let r{j} = {{p}}leaf({arg}).await\n"));
+    }
+    mid.push_str("    let z = (k, y, n, fl, on)\n    z.2 + 1\n}");
+    let call = format!("{{p}}mid({v1}, {v2})");
+    let mut items = vec![MIR_OWN.to_string(), leaf.to_string(), mid];
+    let driven = if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(format!(
+            "async fn {{p}}top() -> Int {{\n    let a = 7\n    let b = {call}.await\n    a + b\n}}"
+        ));
+        "{p}top()".to_string()
+    } else {
+        call
+    };
+    items.push(mir_go(&[
+        format!("let r = block_on({driven})"),
+        "println(\"${r}\")".to_string(),
+    ]));
+    items.join("\n\n")
+}
+
+/// The arms [`mir_match`] draws from for its chained `match` over its own
+/// sum, in an order in which none is unreachable, a wildcard after them.
+const MIR_ARMS: [&str; 8] = [
+    "{p}A(0, \"x\") => 1",
+    "{p}A(n, _) if n > 3 => n",
+    "{p}A(1..=9, s) => s.len()",
+    "{p}B((1..=5, 'a')) | {p}B((_, 'b')) => 2",
+    "{p}B((n, 'c'..='f')) => n",
+    "{p}C(Some({p}A(k, \"y\") | {p}B((k, _)))) => k",
+    "{p}C(Some({p}C(None))) => 4",
+    "{p}C(None) => 3",
+];
+
+/// Match shapes over a sum of its own: a `match` lowered by chains, its
+/// arms drawn from [`MIR_ARMS`] (nested variant and tuple patterns, a
+/// guard, or-patterns, ranges, string and char literals), then a
+/// wildcard; a flat one, a `Switch` on the tag; and as the seed picks a
+/// tuple's, an `Int`'s, a `Char`'s and a `String`'s.
+fn mir_match(rng: &mut SplitMix64) -> String {
+    let mut arms: Vec<&str> = MIR_ARMS.iter().copied().filter(|_| rng.one_in(2)).collect();
+    if arms.is_empty() {
+        arms.push(rng.pick(&MIR_ARMS));
+    }
+    let mut chain = String::from("fn {p}chain(v: {p}S) -> Int {\n    match v {\n");
+    for arm in arms {
+        chain.push_str(&format!("        {arm}\n"));
+    }
+    chain.push_str("        _ => 0\n    }\n}");
+    let mut items = vec![
+        "type {p}S = | {p}A(Int, String) | {p}B((Int, Char)) | {p}C(Option<{p}S>) | {p}D".to_string(),
+        chain,
+        "fn {p}flat(v: {p}S) -> Int {\n    match v {\n        {p}A(a, _) => a\n        {p}B(_) => 1\n        {p}C(_) => 2\n        {p}D => 3\n    }\n}"
+            .to_string(),
+    ];
+    let mut lines = vec![
+        "let a = {p}chain({p}A(0, \"x\")) + {p}chain({p}C(Some({p}B((2, 'b')))))".to_string(),
+        "let b = {p}chain({p}D) + {p}flat({p}B((1, 'z')))".to_string(),
+        "println(\"${a} ${b}\")".to_string(),
+    ];
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "fn {p}tuple(t: (Int, String)) -> Int {\n    match t {\n        (0, \"a\") | (1, \"b\") => 1\n        (n, s) if n == s.len() => 2\n        (5..=7, _) => 3\n        _ => 0\n    }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}tuple((6, \"c\"))}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "fn {p}int(n: Int) -> Int {\n    match n {\n        0 => 1\n        1 => 2\n        _ => 3\n    }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}int(1)}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "fn {p}char(c: Char) -> Int {\n    match c {\n        'a' => 1\n        'b' => 2\n        _ => 0\n    }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}char('b')}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        items.push(
+            "fn {p}text(s: String) -> Int {\n    match s {\n        \"a\" => 1\n        \"b\" => 2\n        _ => 0\n    }\n}"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}text(\"a\")}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    items.push(mir_go(&lines));
+    items.join("\n\n")
+}
+
+/// Closures: one capturing a value of each class (`Int`, `Float`, `Bool`,
+/// `String` and `()`) passed to a generic function that calls it through
+/// its function-typed parameter, and a bare function passed as a value;
+/// and as the seed picks a closure capturing an `Int`, one taking a value
+/// of a type the seed draws, a closure called directly, and a closure
+/// that makes and calls one of its own.
+fn mir_closure(rng: &mut SplitMix64) -> String {
+    let items = vec![
+        MIR_OWN.to_string(),
+        "fn {p}apply<T>(f: fn(T) -> T, x: T) -> T { f(x) }".to_string(),
+        "fn {p}twice(x: Int) -> Int { x * 2 }".to_string(),
+    ];
+    let mut lines: Vec<String> = [
+        "let k = 5",
+        "let s = \"cap\"",
+        "let fl = 1.5",
+        "let bo = true",
+        "let u = ()",
+        "let all = |y: String| {\n        let w = u\n        \"${y}${s}${fl}${bo}${k}\"\n    }",
+        "let r1 = {p}apply(all, \"a\")",
+        "let r2 = {p}apply({p}twice, 4)",
+        "println(\"${r1} ${r2}\")",
+    ]
+    .iter()
+    .map(|l| l.to_string())
+    .collect();
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        lines.push("let add = |x: Int| x + k".to_string());
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}apply(add, 3)}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        let (t, v) = mir_type(rng, 2);
+        lines.push(format!("let a: {t} = {v}"));
+        lines.push(format!(
+            "let keep = |z: {t}| {{\n        let q = (k, s)\n        z\n    }}"
+        ));
+        lines.push("let r3 = {p}apply(keep, a)".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        lines.push("println(all(\"b\"))".to_string());
+    }
+    if rng.one_in(2) {
+        lines.push(
+            "let outer = |x: Int| {\n        let inner = |y: Int| y + x + k\n        inner(x)\n    }"
+                .to_string(),
+        );
+        lines.push("println(\"${{p}apply(outer, 1)}\")".to_string());
+    }
+    let mut items = items;
+    items.push(mir_go(&lines));
+    items.join("\n\n")
+}
+
+/// The byte just inside `main`'s body, after its `{`, if `source` parses
+/// and declares a `main`.
+fn main_body(source: &str) -> Option<usize> {
+    use nova_ast::Item;
+    let (tokens, lex_errors) = nova_lexer::lex(source, FileId::DUMMY);
+    if !lex_errors.is_empty() {
+        return None;
+    }
+    let parsed = nova_parser::parse_recovering(&tokens, FileId::DUMMY);
+    if !parsed.errors.is_empty() {
+        return None;
+    }
+    parsed.file.items.iter().find_map(|item| match &item.value {
+        Item::Function(f) if f.name.value == "main" => Some(f.body.span.start as usize + 1),
+        _ => None,
+    })
+}
+
+/// `source`, a program that lowers clean, given one to three closed
+/// additions (module docs, 7.), each of a kind of [`MIR_KINDS`] the seed
+/// picks: items appended under the prefix `__m<n>_<k>` and the kind's
+/// letter, `<k>` the addition's place, and a statement at the start of
+/// `main`'s body, `<prefix>go()`, of type `()`, that reaches them. An
+/// addition names only its own items, std's and its locals, so every input
+/// type-checks and lowers clean, which the harness checks.
+pub fn mir_mutate(rng: &mut SplitMix64, source: &str, n: usize) -> String {
+    let at = main_body(source).expect("a base declares `main` and parses");
+    let mut calls = String::new();
+    let mut items = String::new();
+    for step in 0..1 + rng.below(3) {
+        let (kind, _) = *rng.pick(&MIR_KINDS);
+        let text = match kind {
+            'g' => mir_generic(rng),
+            'a' => mir_await(rng),
+            'p' => mir_match(rng),
+            _ => mir_closure(rng),
+        };
+        let prefix = format!("__m{n}_{step}{kind}");
+        items.push_str(&format!("\n{}\n", text.replace("{p}", &prefix)));
+        calls.push_str(&format!("\n    {prefix}go()"));
+    }
+    format!(
+        "{}{calls}\n{}\n{items}",
+        &source[..at],
+        source[at..].trim_end_matches('\n')
+    )
+}
+
+/// The kind of [`MIR_KINDS`] whose addition declared the function `name`
+/// of a MIR dump: the letter after `__m<n>_<k>`.
+pub fn mir_kind_of(name: &str) -> Option<char> {
+    let rest = name.strip_prefix("__m")?;
+    let rest = rest.trim_start_matches(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit());
+    let rest = rest.strip_prefix('_')?;
+    let rest = rest.trim_start_matches(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit());
+    rest.chars()
+        .next()
+        .filter(|c| MIR_KINDS.iter().any(|(k, _)| k == c))
+}
+
+/// [`BODY_DEEP_KINDS`]' programs made to lower (spec 5.4 §7.5): each nested
+/// body in a function `f` that `main` calls and again in a generic `g`
+/// that `main` instantiates at `Int`, so that lowering and the specializer
+/// both walk it. `main`'s statements go into `f` and `g` as they are, and
+/// a function `f` beside an empty `main` is copied to `g` with a type
+/// parameter first, each called with arguments of its parameters' types.
+pub fn mir_deep(kind: &str, depth: usize) -> String {
+    let text = body_deep(kind, depth);
+    if let Some(f) = text.strip_suffix("\n\nfn main() {}\n") {
+        let rest = f.strip_prefix("fn f(").expect("a function `f` first");
+        let generic = if rest.starts_with(')') {
+            format!("fn g<T>(u: T{rest}")
+        } else {
+            format!("fn g<T>(u: T, {rest}")
+        };
+        // Bound first where they nest, a `let`'s initializer being a level
+        // fewer than a call's argument.
+        let (bind, args) = match kind {
+            "questions" => ("", "Some(1)".to_string()),
+            "or-patterns" | "match-arms" | "match-scrutinees" => ("", "1".to_string()),
+            "let-patterns" => {
+                let deep = format!("{}1{}", "(".repeat(depth), ", 2)".repeat(depth));
+                return format!(
+                    "{f}\n\n{generic}\n\nfn main() {{\n    let a = {deep}\n    let p = f(a)\n    let q = g(1, a)\n    println(\"${{p}} ${{q}}\")\n}}\n"
+                );
+            }
+            "for-ranges" => ("", String::new()),
+            "for-arrays" => ("    let a = [1, 2]\n", "a".to_string()),
+            "for-patterns" => ("    let a = [(1, 2)]\n", "a".to_string()),
+            other => panic!("no function `f` for {other}"),
+        };
+        let comma = if args.is_empty() { "" } else { ", " };
+        return format!(
+            "{f}\n\n{generic}\n\nfn main() {{\n{bind}    let p = f({args})\n    let q = g(1{comma}{args})\n}}\n"
+        );
+    }
+    let body = text
+        .strip_prefix("fn main() {\n")
+        .and_then(|t| t.strip_suffix("\n}\n"))
+        .expect("`main`'s statements");
+    format!("fn f() {{\n{body}\n}}\n\nfn g<T>(u: T) {{\n{body}\n}}\n\nfn main() {{\n    f()\n    g(1)\n}}\n")
+}
+
+/// The written nestings of a type [`mir_deep_type`] puts in an instance's
+/// type argument, each level around `Int` (5.3b's that lower: arrays, a
+/// generic record's arguments, tuples, `fn` types' parameters and
+/// returns), and std's `Option`, a sum.
+pub const MIR_DEEP_TYPES: [(&str, &str, &str); 6] = [
+    ("arrays", "[", "]"),
+    ("generics", "W<", ">"),
+    ("tuples", "(", ", Int)"),
+    ("fn-parameters", "fn(", ") -> Int"),
+    ("fn-returns", "fn() -> ", ""),
+    ("options", "Option<", ">"),
+];
+
+/// A program whose generic functions `make` and `id` are instantiated at a
+/// type nested `depth` levels as `kind` names ([`MIR_DEEP_TYPES`]), so that
+/// `mangle_ty` and the specializer's substitution walk it all (spec 5.4
+/// §7.5).
+pub fn mir_deep_type(kind: &str, depth: usize) -> String {
+    let (_, open, close) = MIR_DEEP_TYPES
+        .iter()
+        .find(|(k, _, _)| *k == kind)
+        .unwrap_or_else(|| panic!("no nesting of a type {kind}"));
+    let ty = format!("{}Int{}", open.repeat(depth), close.repeat(depth));
+    format!(
+        "record W<T> {{ x: T }}\n\nfn make<T>() -> T {{ panic(\"never\") }}\n\nfn id<T>(x: T) -> T {{\n    let y: T = x\n    y\n}}\n\nfn main() {{\n    let v: {ty} = make()\n    let w = id(v)\n}}\n"
+    )
+}
````

```sh
mkdir -p "$(dirname "$P/plan54/adrcheck.py")"
cat > "$P/plan54/adrcheck.py" <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""adrcheck.py: run from the repository's root, after the build, with $P exported.
ADR 0057 §2's examples against S0's `nova check --dump-mir`. The block after
"One example of every form" is S0's dump of `cases/mir/mir_every_native_form.nova`
in part (its `;;` lines the source in brief, `…` lines left out): every other
line must be a line of S0's output, in order. The next block, the before form
of the same file (`--before-async`), likewise. The third holds whole parts,
each after its `== NAME` header: `d.nova`, the header test's program, written
into a fresh directory, the rest `cases/mir/`'s roots; each part must be S0's
part of that root, line for line. Prints the verdict and the count of lines
found."""
import os
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys

P = os.environ["P"]
ADR = "docs/adr/0057-the-self-hosted-mir-and-the-mir-dump.md"
NOVA = os.path.abspath("target/debug/nova")
CASES = os.path.abspath("crates/nova-cli/tests/selfhost/cases/mir")
text = open(ADR, encoding="utf-8").read().split("\n")
start = next(i for i, l in enumerate(text) if l.startswith("**One example of every form.**"))
blocks, i = [], start
while len(blocks) < 3:
    a = next(k for k in range(i, len(text)) if text[k] == "```")
    b = next(k for k in range(a + 1, len(text)) if text[k] == "```")
    blocks.append(text[a + 1 : b])
    i = b + 1


def dump(args, cwd):
    return subprocess.run([NOVA, "check", "--dump-mir", *args], cwd=cwd, capture_output=True,
                          text=True).stdout.split("\n")


def in_order(want, out):
    at = 0
    for line in want:
        while at < len(out) and out[at] != line:
            at += 1
        if at == len(out):
            return line
        at += 1
    return None


found, bad = 0, []
for block, args in [(blocks[0], ["mir_every_native_form.nova"]),
                    (blocks[1], ["--before-async", "mir_every_native_form.nova"])]:
    want = [l for l in block if not l.startswith(";;") and "…" not in l]
    missing = in_order(want, dump(args, CASES))
    found += len(want)
    if missing is not None:
        bad.append(f"{args}: MISSING {missing!r}")
work = f"{P}/plan54/adr-ex"
shutil.rmtree(work, ignore_errors=True)
os.makedirs(work)
open(os.path.join(work, "d.nova"), "w", encoding="utf-8").write('fn main() { let x: Int = "s" }\n')
parts, cur = [], None
for line in blocks[2]:
    if line.startswith("== "):
        cur = [line]
        parts.append(cur)
    else:
        cur.append(line)
for part in parts:
    root = part[0][3:]
    cwd = work if root == "d.nova" else CASES
    out = dump([root], cwd)
    if out[: len(part)] != part:
        bad.append(f"{root}: differs")
    found += len(part)
print(f"examples: {len(blocks[0]) + len(blocks[1]) + len(blocks[2])} block lines, {found} dump lines "
      f"checked, {len(parts)} parts, {'ok' if not bad else 'BAD ' + '; '.join(bad)}")
shutil.rmtree(work, ignore_errors=True)
sys.exit(1 if bad else 0)
EOF
```

  After the build, `python3 "$P/plan54/adrcheck.py"`. Expected, as the
  replay printed: `examples: 169 block lines, 135 dump lines checked, 4
  parts, ok`.

+# ADR 0057 — The self-hosted MIR, and the MIR dump
+
+## Status
+
+Accepted (2026-11-15). Branch `claude/loving-archimedes-frtf5q-54`
+(`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-phase-5-4-mir-design.md`), written as
+5.4's prototype was built. CI's figures and the user's answer on them
+(spec §8) are §19's.
+Part 1 (S0's side) wrote §1 to §9:
+- §1, the hidden flag and its before form;
+- §2, the dump's grammar, with one example of every form, and the
+  decisions it took;
+- §3, the hook before the async transform;
+- §4, `MirDumps`;
+- §5, std's checked bodies kept and spliced into each program, the
+  fallback and the guard;
+- §6, why 5.4 is native only;
+- §7, the cases, those left out, and the codes with no case;
+- §8, the harness's switch;
+- §9, part 1's figures.
+
+Part 2 (N's lowering and monomorphization) wrote §10 to §12:
+- §10, N's port, its idioms, its splice and `selfhost mir`;
+- §11, the harness in part 2: the before form compared on every input;
+- §12, part 2's figures.
+
+Part 3 (N's `async_lower`) wrote §13 to §15:
+- §13, N's port of the async transform, its asserts as panics;
+- §14, the harness in part 3: the after form compared on every input,
+  the before form narrowed to the roots the transform changes;
+- §15, part 3's figures.
+
+Part 4 (the fuzzer, the depth cases, the measurements and the records)
+wrote §16 to §21 and the Consequences:
+- §16, the MIR fuzzer;
+- §17, the depth cases, and N's stack;
+- §18, part 4's figures: agreement on every set, the time, N's memory;
+- §19, the time from the base to part 4, and CI's projection;
+- §20, departures from the spec and the phase plan, and decisions the
+  spec does not settle;
+- §21, the rule.
+
+Before the plan, its step 0 (2026-11-15) added the cases and checks the
+prototype's mutants asked for, each recorded where it falls: three cases
+(§7), the guard's native floors (§5), an N `@test` of the splice (§10),
+and N's MIR dump in the test of a std whose bodies give a diagnostic
+(§14); the MIR dump's mismatch report as spec §4.3 has it, which the
+prototype had not built (§11); and N's stack and memory, measured again
+at `430618f5`, the split's last commit before step 0 gained the MIR
+report, whose N, build and inputs are the plan's last commit's (§17,
+§18).
+
+## Decision
+
+### 1. The hidden flag, `nova check --dump-mir [--before-async] FILE…`
+
+ADR 0053's mechanism (spec §3.1, the user's decision 8): the form is
+taken only when `--dump-mir` is the first argument after `check`.
+`DumpCmd::from_args` gains a third arm, and `DumpCmd` becomes an enum
+of the two kinds it parses: `Front`, the resolution and HIR dumps with
+`DumpResolutionCmd`'s arguments, and `Mir(DumpMirCmd)`.
+
+`DumpMirCmd` is a parser of its own. It takes `--before-async`, once,
+and files, with `--` before a file that starts with `-`. Anything else
+is one clap error under the usage `nova check --dump-mir [OPTIONS]
+[FILES]...`, exit 2. That includes `--std`, `--target`, `--test`,
+`--help`, `-h`, the flag again and `--before-async` twice.
+
+`--dump-mir=…` is not the form, so the base's refusal stands, and
+`--dump-mir=before-async` with it. With no file it prints `error:
+--dump-mir needs a FILE` on stderr, nothing on stdout, and exits 2.
+
+Each file prints `== FILE` (the path as given) and its part. A root it
+cannot read prints `IOERR` and the error's kind, the rest are dumped,
+and the exit code is 2.
+
+- **No `--std`, `--target` or `--test`.** MIR is monomorphized per
+  program from `main` and every symbol carries an absolute `DefId`, so it
+  has no std part. WASM's and test mode's MIR are not compared (§6).
+- **`--before-async` an option, not a second leading flag.** The
+  mechanism finds one flag per dump. A second entry point, as 5.3b's
+  transitional `--dump-hir-items` was, would give one permanent dump two
+  hidden entries.
+- **Checked by:**
+  - `the_mir_dump_flag_is_hidden_and_refused_as_the_spec_says`
+    (`selfhost.rs`): `check --help` names neither `--dump-mir` nor
+    `--before-async`; every base shape around the flag gives the base's
+    error, byte for byte; each refusal of the form's own parser is one
+    error under its usage line; `--` takes a root that starts with `-`.
+  - `usagecmp.sh` (§9): 474 base shapes under six `argv[0]`s, and the
+    form's refusals.
+
+### 2. The dump
+
+`nova_mir::dump` (new; the printer lives in its stage's crate, as each
+earlier dump does). `dump_program(labels, reached)` gives a root's part.
+`Reached` says how far the root got: `NotRun`, `NotChecked`, or
+`Lowered { result, async_lowered }`.
+
+**The grammar.** `I(d)` is `d` levels of two-space indentation. A list
+is in parentheses, its items one space apart, `()` when empty.
+
+```
+part      = mir NL diagnostics
+mir       = "(Mir runs=false)"
+          | "(Mir runs=true checked=false)"
+          | "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=false)"
+          | "(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=" bool
+            " functions=" uint " externs=" uint { extern } { function } ")"
+extern    = I(1) "(Extern " string " abi=" ("C" | "Js") " (" { class } ") ret=" class ")"
+function  = I(1) "(Function " string " params=" uint " takes_env=" bool
+            " captures=" uint " ret=" class " async=" bool { temp } { block } ")"
+temp      = I(2) "(Temp t" uint " " class ")"
+block     = I(2) "(Block bb" uint { stmt } term ")"
+stmt      = I(3) "(" Kind { " " value } ")"
+term      = I(3) ( "(Goto " bb ")" | "(Branch " t " " bb " " bb ")"
+                 | "(Switch " t " (" { int ":" bb } ") " bb ")"
+                 | "(Return " (t | "_") ")" | "(Trap)" )
+class     = "I64" | "F64" | "I8" | "Ptr" | "Unit"
+diagnostics = 5.2's "(Diagnostics" … ")", MIR's alone
+```
+
+**A statement** prints its `Stmt` variant's name, then every field in
+declaration order:
+- a destination as `t<n>`, or `_` when it is absent;
+- a temp as `t<n>`, a block as `bb<n>`;
+- an integer in decimal;
+- a float as the 16 lowercase hex digits of `to_bits` (5.1's form);
+- a string or a symbol quoted as 5.1 quotes it;
+- a `BinOp`, an `OperandClass`, a `MirTy`, an `RtFunc` or a `MemOp` by
+  its variant's name;
+- a list in parentheses. A capture's, field's or element's class sits
+  beside its temp (`t3:Ptr`); a switch arm's value sits beside its
+  block (`0:bb2`).
+
+| Kind | Values |
+|---|---|
+| `ConstInt`, `ConstFloat`, `ConstBool`, `ConstStr` | dst, the value |
+| `ConstUnit` | dst |
+| `Copy`, `Not`, `BitNot` | dst, src |
+| `Bin` | dst, op, operand class, lhs, rhs |
+| `Neg` | dst, operand class, src |
+| `Call` | dst or `_`, the callee's symbol, the arguments |
+| `CallIndirect` | dst or `_`, the callee's temp, the parameters' classes, the return's class, the arguments |
+| `Await` | dst or `_`, the future |
+| `CallRuntime` | dst or `_`, the runtime function, the arguments |
+| `MakeClosure` | dst, the code's symbol, the captures with their classes |
+| `MakeSum` | dst, the tag, the fields with their classes |
+| `SumTag` | dst, the sum |
+| `SumField` | dst, the sum, the index, the class |
+| `MakeRecord`, `MakeArray` | dst, the fields or elements with their classes |
+| `RecordField` | dst, the record, the index, the class |
+| `SetField` | the record, the index, the value, the class |
+| `ArrayAlloc` | dst, the length |
+| `ArrayLen` | dst, the array |
+| `ArrayGet` | dst, the array, the index, the class |
+| `ArraySet` | the array, the index, the value, the class |
+| `Mem` | dst or `_`, the operation, the arguments |
+
+**Order** is MIR's own, nothing sorted:
+- `Module::functions` as the worklist made them (a LIFO stack seeded with
+  `main`), the async `main`'s shim after them, the transform's wrappers
+  after all;
+- the externs as collected;
+- temps and blocks by index.
+
+`runtime_alloc` and `runtime_exports` are WASM's, empty natively, and
+not printed.
+
+**Symbols print raw,** with absolute `DefId`s (`block_on.255$i`,
+`closure$722.722`). No std part is possible, and none is needed: the
+splice (§5) gives std's functions the full check's ids.
+
+**`KINDS`** lists every form the dump prints: each line's kind, and each
+`key=value` field whose value is a word. Every `key=bool` field is
+listed with both values. **`NOT_NATIVE`** lists the two forms only a
+WASM build's MIR holds, `(Mem` and `abi=Js`. The printer takes any
+module, so it prints them.
+
+**One example of every form.** S0's `nova check --dump-mir` of
+`cases/mir/mir_every_native_form.nova`, shown in part: every line below
+is S0's, byte for byte, and `…` stands for lines left out.
+
+```
+;; extern "C" { fn labs(x: Int) -> Int }
+;; record P { x: Int  y: Float }
+;; type S = | A(Int) | B
+;; fn pick(s: S) -> Int { match s { A(n) => n  B => 0 } }
+;; fn flip(b: Bool) -> Bool { !b }
+;; fn half(f: Float) -> Float { -f / 2.0 }
+;; async fn one() -> Int { 1 }
+;; async fn two() -> Int { one().await + 1 }
+;; fn main() {
+;;     let mut p = P { x: ~1, y: half(3.0) }
+;;     p.x = -p.x
+;;     let mut a = [0; 3]
+;;     a[1] = a.len() + [5, 6][1]
+;;     let k = 2
+;;     let f = |n: Int| n * k
+;;     let g = pick
+;;     println("${f(a[1])} ${g(A(4))} ${flip(true)} ${p.y} ${labs(-3)} ${block_on(two())}")
+;; }
+== mir_every_native_form.nova
+(Mir runs=true checked=true lowered=true async_lowered=true functions=11 externs=1
+  (Extern "labs" abi=C (I64) ret=I64)
+  (Function "main" params=0 takes_env=false captures=0 ret=Unit async=false
